## Highest-leverage sources the agents could NOT fully access

If you can read or provide any of these, this is the priority order for *these* questions:

1. **Compiling with Abstract Interpretation** — Bautista, Jensen, Monat, Miné — **PLDI 2024** — <https://hal.science/hal-04535159>
   *HAL PDF was access-blocked (FR4).* **← the one to get.** It is the published work that most directly anticipates "the compiler itself is a fixpoint / abstract interpretation, passes as functor domains, SSA derived as an abstract domain." FR4 explicitly flags it needs the full text to judge **how much of our totalization/phase-collapse novelty it already subsumes.** Decides where our defensible novelty actually sits.
2. **Optimism in Equality Saturation** — **PLDI 2026** — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.20782>
   Open, but dated `2511` (Nov-2025), *near the agents' knowledge cutoff* — worth you verifying it exists & reading. Named as the **nearest peer** to PReduce's analysis+rewrite half (optimistic TOP-start e-class analysis, à la SCCP, on the e-graph+Datalog+AI triad).
3. **Flix** (lattice-semantics, PLDI 2016, <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/2908080.2908096>) and **Goodman, "Semiring Parsing"** (Wiley, <https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/spe.1079>) — paywalled, no open mirror found; lower leverage (corroborating prior art, not decisive).

Accessible via open mirror (no need to provide):
- **Equality Saturation** (Tate et al., POPL 2009) — open LMCS-2011 version: <https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~lerner/papers/lmcs11-eqsat.pdf>
- **Composing Dataflow Analyses and Transformations** (Lerner-Grove-Chambers, POPL 2002 — *the direct ancestor of phase-collapse*) — open tech report: <https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~lerner/UW-CSE-01-11-01.pdf>

## All external citations (69), grouped by facet

### FR1 — Equality saturation all the way to codegen / instruction selection: where the production frontier actually is, and what it means for designing PReduce to feed an LLVM lowering.
- <https://cfallin.org/blog/2026/04/09/aegraph/> — Chris Fallin (Cranelift author), 'The acyclic e-graph: Cranelift's mid-end optimizer' (Apr 2026). THE key production data point: a shipping compiler whose mid-end IS an e-graph, with the SAME ISLE rule DSL used for both mid-end rewrites and instruction lowering. Extraordinarily concrete on costs, benefits, and what does/doesn't pay off.
- <https://github.com/bytecodealliance/rfcs/blob/main/accepted/cranelift-egraph.md> — Accepted Bytecode Alliance RFC for the Cranelift e-graph mid-end. Primary design doc behind the aegraph; covers verifiability-via-rewrite-rules motivation and the ISLE-for-both-mid-end-and-lowering thesis.
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2508.13020v1> — e-boost: Boosted E-Graph Extraction with Adaptive Heuristics and Exact Solving (Aug 2025). State-of-the-art extraction: heuristic prune + WARM-START ILP. Key numbers: extraction = 89.3% of total eqsat runtime; 558x speedup vs vanilla ILP at +5.6% quality; DAG-cost NP-completeness.
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2505.09363v2> — eqsat: An Equality Saturation Dialect for Non-destructive Rewriting (2025, xDSL/MLIR). Represents the e-graph AS an MLIR dialect (eqsat.eclass/egraph/yield) so eqsat state persists across passes — the closest existing analog to Prologos's 'substrate IS the IR' position. Uses MLIR graph-regions to permit cycles.
- <https://www.cs.cornell.edu/courses/cs6120/2025sp/blog/superopt/> — Cornell CS6120 (2025) survey blog on technology mapping with e-graphs. Frames instruction selection/tech-mapping AS e-graph extraction; rewrite rules encode lowering+optimization simultaneously; documents acyclicity as the main ILP-extraction bottleneck and solver-robustness failures (cbc 'infeasible').
- <https://effect.systems/blog/egraph-extraction.html> — 'The E-graph extraction problem is NP-complete' — reduction from minimum set cover; e-graph extraction with DAG/sharing-aware cost is hard to approximate within any constant ratio. Grounds the §2.5/§6.3 NP-hardness claim in our E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH doc.
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2408.17042> — 'E-Graphs as Circuits, and Optimal Extraction via Treewidth' (2024). Recasts extraction as a circuit/Boolean problem; parameterized algorithm optimal for low-treewidth e-graphs. A structural-tractability lever PReduce's E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH doc does not yet cite.
- <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3689801> — 'Fast and Optimal Extraction for Sparse Equality Graphs' (OOPSLA 2024). Optimal extraction made practical when the e-graph is sparse — another tractability regime relevant to PReduce's per-AST-PU bounded e-graphs.
- <https://courses.cs.washington.edu/courses/cse501/15sp/papers/joshi.pdf> — Denali: A Goal-directed Superoptimizer (Joshi/Nelson/Randall, PLDI 2002). The ORIGINAL eqsat-to-codegen system: builds an e-graph of equivalent computations via mathematical+architectural axioms, then extracts optimal machine code via constraint solving (matcher + SAT). Direct ancestor of PReduce's vision.
- <https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.04422> — Souper: A Synthesizing Superoptimizer (LLVM IR). SMT-based, not e-graph-based: extracts an LHS from LLVM IR, SYNTHESIZES an optimized RHS. Counterpoint to eqsat — shows the synthesis-vs-saturation axis for feeding LLVM.
- <https://users.cs.utah.edu/~regehr/minotaur-oopsla24.pdf> — Minotaur: A SIMD-Oriented Synthesizing Superoptimizer (OOPSLA 2024). Souper-lineage, targets LLVM vector IR; current SOTA on practical LLVM-IR superoptimization. Benchmark/quality reference for an LLVM-feeding optimizer.
- <https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11574> — E-morphic: Scalable Equality Saturation for Structural Exploration in Logic Synthesis (2025). Direct e-graph<->circuit conversion + simulated-annealing extraction; 12.54% area / 7.29% delay vs ABC. Evidence eqsat-to-hardware-codegen is crossing into competitive-with-production-EDA territory.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.09932> — 'Automatically Generating Compiler Backends from Tensor Descriptions' (2025). First phase is parameterized INSTRUCTION SELECTION via equality saturation — eqsat used to GENERATE a backend, the strongest recent statement of eqsat-spanning-to-codegen.
- <https://pldi25.sigplan.org/details/egraphs-2025-papers/8/Oatlog-A-performant-ahead-of-time-compiled-e-graph-engine> — Oatlog (EGRAPHS 2025): AOT-compiled egglog engine as a relational DB with semi-naive evaluation. Marks the 2025 egglog-as-relational-Datalog maturation — directly relevant to our 'eqsat = monotone Datalog = propagator quiescence' framing.

### FR2 — The Phase-Ordering Problem and Its Dissolution (positioning the Prologos "multi-pass compiler collapses to a lattice fixpoint on the propagator network" claim against the canon)
- <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/201059.201061> — Click & Cooper, 'Combining Analyses, Combining Optimizations', TOPLAS 17(2):181-196, 1995. The canonical 'combine on one structure' result: a monotone framework that runs Conditional Constant Propagation + Global Value Numbering + unreachable-code elimination SIMULTANEOUSLY to a single least fixpoint over a product lattice, finding facts no sequential ordering of the same passes can. Theoretical backbone of the 'lattice fixpoint dissolves phase ordering' claim.
- <https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~lerner/UW-CSE-01-11-01.pdf> — Lerner, Grove & Chambers, 'Composing Dataflow Analyses and Transformations', POPL 2002. The CLOSEST prior art to the Prologos claim: independently-specified analyses+transformations communicate IMPLICITLY via graph transformations and run to a mutual optimistic fixpoint ('super-analysis'), avoiding both the precision loss of iterating separate passes and the cost of hand-writing a combined pass.
- <https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~lerner/papers/lmcs11-eqsat.pdf> — Tate, Stepp, Tatlock & Lerner, 'Equality Saturation: a New Approach to Optimization' (POPL 2009 / LMCS 2011 journal). Introduces the E-PEG: optimizations become non-destructive equality analyses that saturate a shared IR with equalities; a SEPARATE global profitability/extraction step (pseudo-boolean/ILP) picks the best representative. The reference 'eqsat dissolves phase ordering by never destroying' strategy. (PDF rendered as binary on fetch; content corroborated from POPL09/abstract + AfterMath summary.)
- <https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.03082> — Willsey et al., 'egg: Fast and Extensible Equality Saturation', 2021 (POPL). Modern production-grade e-graph library; introduced deferred/rebuild-batched congruence closure (near-linear in practice) and made eqsat reusable infrastructure (egglog, Cranelift, DialEgg downstream).
- <https://v8.dev/blog/leaving-the-sea-of-nodes> — V8 team, 'Land ahoy: leaving the Sea of Nodes', 2025. FRESH frontier limit-datapoint: a flagship production user of the value-based sea-of-nodes IR ABANDONED it for a traditional CFG IR (Turboshaft) — compile time halved, 7x fewer L1 dcache misses, simpler code. Direct evidence that 'value-based IR dissolves scheduling' has real practical limits.
- <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_of_nodes> — Sea of Nodes overview: Click's mid-90s value-dependency-graph IR (relaxes total order into explicit data+control deps; needs Global Code Motion to recover a CFG). Adopted by HotSpot C2, GraalVM, libFirm, V8 TurboFan.
- <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superoptimization> — Superoptimization overview: Massalin 1987 ('Superoptimizer: A Look at the Smallest Program') brute-force search for the shortest instruction sequence; the probabilistic-test idea that makes exhaustive search practical. Frames the search-based (not fixpoint-based) family.
- <https://theory.stanford.edu/~aiken/publications/papers/asplos13.pdf> — Schkufza, Sharma & Aiken, 'Stochastic Superoptimization', ASPLOS 2013 (STOKE). MCMC sampling of x86 program space with a correctness-tolerant fitness function; matches/beats gcc -O3 on small kernels. The stochastic-search family that trades completeness for reach.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2208.04238> — POSET-RL (2022) + MLGO line: reinforcement-learning approaches to LLVM pass ordering. Treats phase ordering as an NP-hard sequential decision problem and learns a policy — the ML-for-phase-ordering family that MITIGATES rather than dissolves (still picks an order).
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2410.03120v2> — 'Solving the Phase Ordering Problem != Generating the Globally Optimal Code' (2024). Sharpens the canonical framing: even an oracle phase order over a fixed destructive-pass set need not reach the global optimum — motivates non-destructive (eqsat-style) and search (superopt) approaches over order-search.

### FR3 — Emitting OPTIMAL LLVM IR from a high-level / functional / dataflow front-end (the human's Q2b)
- <https://llvm.org/docs/Frontend/PerformanceTips.html> — LLVM 'Performance Tips for Frontend Authors' — THE canonical primary checklist for emitting IR the optimizer can handle: entry-block allocas (mem2reg/SROA), avoid aggregate values, noalias/align/dereferenceable/nonnull/noundef arg attrs, readnone/readonly/argmemonly/willreturn/nounwind fn attrs, TBAA + noalias metadata, lifetime.start/end, !invariant.load, inbounds GEP, poison-over-undef, nsw/nuw, avoid ptrtoint/inttoptr + wide ints + atomics + llvm.assume.
- <https://blog.llvm.org/2010/05/glasgow-haskell-compiler-and-llvm.html> — LLVM Project blog: GHC's LLVM backend lowers Cmm (C--) to LLVM, with the custom ghccc calling convention pinning STG machine registers into hardware registers for NCG ABI compatibility.
- <https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1805.08842> — Farvardin & Reppy, 'Compiling with Continuations and LLVM' (ML/OCaml 2016). Definitive treatment of functional-front-end→LLVM: musttail marker (caller/callee type match), custom JWA (Jump-With-Arguments) calling convention (all regs caller-clobbered, arg regs == live-on-return regs), naked attribute to skip prologue/epilogue, shared spill frame, CFG IR as first-order machine-like rep. Speedups vs MLRISC.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1908.05647> — Ullrich & de Moura, 'Counting Immutable Beans' (IFL 2019) — Lean4's λrc IR: explicit reference-counting, borrowed-vs-owned parameter inference, reuse analysis (FBIP destructive in-place update), drop specialization. The high-level-IR optimizations that must precede C/LLVM emission.
- <https://xnning.github.io/papers/perceus.pdf> — Reinking, Xie, de Moura, Leijen, 'Perceus: Garbage Free Reference Counting with Reuse' (PLDI 2021) — Koka's precise RC: ownership passed into functions, garbage-free, reuse for guaranteed in-place updates, drop specialization; beats OCaml/Haskell/sometimes C++.
- <https://github.com/swiftlang/swift/blob/main/docs/OptimizerDesign.md> — Swift OptimizerDesign — explicit statement that SIL-level optimizations (ARC optimizer, devirtualizer) run on Ownership-SSA BEFORE lowering to LLVM because 'when SIL is lowered to LLVM-IR the high-level information of the program is lost.'
- <https://mlir.llvm.org/docs/Tutorials/Toy/Ch-5/> — MLIR Toy Ch.5/6 — progressive/partial lowering: optimize in the high-level dialect, then discard structure on lowering to LLVM dialect; 'lowering too early obscures high-level structure.'
- <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09363> — 'eqsat: An Equality Saturation Dialect for Non-destructive Rewriting' (EGRAPHS/PLDI 2025) — represents the e-graph NATIVELY in MLIR so the IR stays an e-graph through compilation, reusing MLIR passes (CSE) and avoiding library↔IR translation. Direct prior art for PReduce's 'substrate IS the IR'.
- <https://dl.acm.org/doi/fullHtml/10.1145/3391902> — Reissmann et al., RVSDG (Regionalized Value State Dependence Graph, TACO 2020) — a dataflow IR that encodes control-flow as data-flow, relaxes CFG ordering, exposes parallelism, targets LLVM-IR abstraction level and lowers to it. Closest existing analog of 'a dataflow/propagator graph as the optimizing IR'.
- <https://llvm.org/docs/Statepoints.html> — LLVM 'Garbage Collection Safepoints' — gc.statepoint/gc.relocate; RewriteStatepointsForGC must run LATE (after most opt) because relocation forces pointer spills to stack + stack-map encoding, acting as an optimization barrier.
- <https://janvitek.org/pubs/oopsla18b.pdf> — Pelenitsyn et al., 'Julia: Dynamism and Performance Reconciled by Design' (OOPSLA 2018) — the three codegen optimizations (method-inlining devirtualization, unboxing, specialization) precede LLVM; turning off LLVM opt alone is 1.1–7.1x slower, but the front-end devirt/unbox is what makes IR optimizable at all.
- <https://groups.google.com/g/llvm-dev/c/N3r_a1Vbrog> — LLVMdev thread 'LLVM IR is a compiler IR' — community statement that LLVM is biased toward C runtime conventions and a poor target for heap-allocated continuation closures / specialized calling conventions / guaranteed TCO without custom work.

### FR4 — Dataflow/Lattice IRs + Abstract-Interpretation-as-Compilation + Declarative Compilers: novelty grounding for "the compiler IS a lattice fixpoint."
- <https://courses.cs.washington.edu/courses/cse501/15sp/papers/kildall.pdf> — Kildall, 'A Unified Approach to Global Program Optimization' (POPL 1973) — the founding lattice-dataflow framework: global optimization as a meet-over-paths / fixpoint computation of an 'optimizing function' on a lattice over a CFG. ESTABLISHED.
- <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/512950.512973> — Cousot & Cousot, 'Abstract Interpretation: A Unified Lattice Model for Static Analysis of Programs by Construction or Approximation of Fixpoints' (POPL 1977) — establishes static analysis = least/greatest fixpoint of a monotone operator on a complete lattice; dataflow analysis is a special case. ESTABLISHED.
- <https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~pingali/CS380C/2010/papers/p291-wegman.pdf> — Wegman & Zadeck, 'Constant Propagation with Conditional Branches' (POPL 1985 / TOPLAS 1991) — SCCP. The sharpest in-production example (LLVM, GCC) of analysis+optimization FUSED in one lattice fixpoint: a single dual-worklist fixpoint tracks CFG-edge executability AND SSA-value constants simultaneously; optimism (start at TOP) makes it strictly stronger than separate CP+DCE iterated. ESTABLISHED.
- <https://cs.nyu.edu/~pcousot/COUSOTpapers/POPL02.shtml> — Cousot & Cousot, 'Systematic Design of Program Transformation Frameworks by Abstract Interpretation' (POPL 2002) — optimization/transformation ITSELF designed AS abstract interpretation: composition of source→semantics, semantics→transformed-semantics, semantics→source abstractions over fixpoint trace semantics; constant prop, partial evaluation, monitoring as instances. ESTABLISHED.
- <https://hal.science/hal-04535159> — Bautista, Jensen, Monat, Miné, 'Compiling with Abstract Interpretation' (PACMPL/PLDI, June 2024) — turns abstract interpreters INTO compilers via a free algebra over the standard signature of abstract domains; functor domains = compiler passes; soundness=forward simulation, completeness=backward simulation; derives SSA translation as an abstract domain. The strongest recent 'compiler IS abstract interpretation' result. ESTABLISHED (2024).
- <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/2908080.2908096> — Madsen, Yee, Lhoták, 'From Datalog to Flix: A Declarative Language for Fixed Points on Lattices' (PLDI 2016); plus Flix OOPSLA 2025 'A Design for Language-Integrated Datalog' (PLG/Waterloo). A language whose core IS least-fixpoint-of-monotone-functions on user-declared lattices — Datalog generalized from relations to lattices. ESTABLISHED; closest existing 'lattice-semantics language.'
- <https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~nk480/datafun.pdf> — Arntzenius & Krishnaswami, 'Datafun: A Functional Datalog' (ICFP 2016; seminaïve follow-up POPL 2020) — pure total functional language that types monotonicity and computes fixed points of monotone maps on semilattices; generalizes Datalog; the fixpoint operator's least-fixpoint guarantee comes from monotonicity, paralleling stratification. ESTABLISHED.
- <https://souffle-lang.github.io/docs.html> — Soufflé (Datalog→parallel C++ via Futamura projections / staged compilation) + Doop (declarative points-to analysis in Datalog). Declarative-compiler prior art: the analysis is a logic spec, the 'compiler' synthesizes a fast fixpoint engine. Doop's Datalog-to-Datalog optimization passes = order-of-magnitude speedups. ESTABLISHED.
- <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3591239> — Zhang, Wang, Willsey, Tatlock et al., 'Better Together: Unifying Datalog and Equality Saturation' / egglog (PACMPL/PLDI; tutorialized PLDI 2025) — one fixpoint reasoning system unifying Datalog (lattice/relational fixpoint) and equality saturation (rewriting/optimization), with cooperating analyses and lattice-based reasoning. The closest existing system to Prologos's PReduce + on-network-typing collapse. ESTABLISHED (2023–2025).
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.20782> — Arbore, Cheung, Willsey, 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' (PACMPL/PLDI 2026; arXiv Nov 2025) — recasts e-class analysis as abstract interpretation and gives a UNIFIED fixpoint algorithm for OPTIMISTIC analysis (start at TOP, à la SCCP) + non-destructive rewriting on e-graphs, explicitly tying together equality saturation + Datalog + abstract interpretation as one lattice fixpoint. The freshest and most direct convergent prior art for 'analysis + optimization = one fixpoint on the IR.' ESTABLISHED (2025–2026).
- <https://mirror.csclub.uwaterloo.ca/csclub/sussman-propagator-slides.pdf> — Radul & Sussman, 'The Art of the Propagator' (MIT-CSAIL-TR-2009-002) + Radul's 'Propagation Networks' thesis — the propagator substrate Prologos builds on: autonomous machines over shared cells accumulating monotone partial information; computation by propagation-and-storage. ESTABLISHED foundation, but NOT used as a compiler IR by Radul/Sussman.

### FR5 — GPU/GPGPU + parallel-target lowering from a dataflow/BSP IR (keeping non-von-Neumann in reach)
- <https://mlir.llvm.org/docs/Dialects/GPU/> — MLIR 'gpu' dialect reference (fetched). Establishes that GPU lowering requires EXPLICITLY PARALLEL IR — grid/block/thread dims via gpu.launch, kernel boundaries (gpu.module/gpu.func), explicit memory address spaces (global/workgroup/private/constant), async deps via !gpu.async.token, in-kernel reductions via gpu.all_reduce/gpu.shuffle/gpu.subgroup_reduce. The pipeline 'does not do GPU parallelization' — the source IR must already expose the parallelism.
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2311.02206v4> — 'Optimizing Datalog for the GPU' (GDlog/GPUlog, Sun et al., fetched). THE closest match to a propagator network: monotone fixpoint over relations on GPU. HISA dense hash-indexed-sorted-array beats pointer/hash stores; semi-naive new/delta/full = delta-driven iteration; 4 kernel launches/iteration with kernel-boundary BSP barriers; temporal materialization to balance warp divergence on N-way joins; Eager Buffer Management for dynamic relation growth; 35-45x over Soufflé.
- <https://mlir.llvm.org/docs/Dialects/NVVMDialect/> — MLIR 'nvvm' dialect + GPUToNVVMPipeline. The default NVVM-target lowering path; nvgpu is the intermediary between target-agnostic gpu/vector dialects and NVVM intrinsics; models NVIDIA address spaces and PTX functionality. Shows the staged-dialect lowering model a Prologos backend would plug into.
- <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/advancing-gpu-programming-with-the-cuda-tile-ir-backend-for-openai-triton/> — NVIDIA CUDA Tile IR backend for OpenAI Triton (2025-2026). Triton operates on block-level tiles (statically-shaped sub-arrays); compiler auto-handles coalescing, shared memory, thread scheduling. Confirms the modern abstraction is TILE-of-dense-data, not single-element and not sparse-graph — the wrong granularity for sparse propagator activation.
- <https://futhark-lang.org/publications/ppopp19.pdf> — 'Incremental Flattening for Nested Data Parallelism' (Henriksen et al., Futhark). Moderate/incremental flattening — middle ground between NESL full flattening (max parallelism, O(n^3) blow-up, destroys locality) and no flattening. Relevant to how Prologos's nested/irregular network parallelism would need flattening to hit flat GPU SIMT.
- <https://hackage.haskell.org/package/accelerate> — Data.Array.Accelerate (Haskell EDSL -> CUDA via LLVM PTX). Explicitly LIMITED to flat data-parallelism over regular multidimensional arrays; the Acc/Exp stratification 'statically excludes nested, irregular data-parallelism.' Concrete evidence the array-DSL family is structurally mismatched to sparse dynamic propagator graphs.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1701.01170> — Gunrock: GPU Graph Analytics (Wang et al.). Data-centric BSP abstraction over a FRONTIER (explicit active subset of vertices/edges); all ops bulk-synchronous, manipulate/compute new frontier. The frontier IS an explicit activation set — the design pattern Prologos needs for sparse per-round propagator activation. 'Frontier' beats Gather-Apply-Scatter for GPU graph frameworks.
- <http://regent-lang.org/images/regent2015.pdf> — Regent/Legion (Slaughter, Bauer et al.). Dataflow task-graph runtime: logical regions express locality+independence; runtime DYNAMICALLY extracts parallelism by computing a dataflow graph; supports irregular parallelism; sequential-appearing programs, no explicit sync. The architectural cousin to Prologos's cell/propagator/scheduler split, but CPU/distributed-HPC, not pure SIMT.
- <https://www.comp.nus.edu.sg/~tulika/CGRA-Survey.pdf> — CGRA survey (Liu et al.) + FlexC (Woodruff 2023, via search). Coarse-grained reconfigurable arrays embrace the dataflow computing model; PE-array mapping is NP-complete; FlexC uses EQUALITY SATURATION (e-graphs) for dataflow rewriting to expand mapping coverage 2.2x. Direct bridge: PReduce's e-graphs could double as a spatial-mapping engine for a dataflow/CGRA target.
- <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dataflow_architecture> — Dataflow architecture overview + 2024-2025 hardware (via search): NextSilicon Maverick-2 dataflow HPC accelerator (Oct 2024, Sandia Spectra), Efficient Computer Electron E1 dataflow chip (Jul 2025), SPADA spatial-dataflow language (arXiv 2511.09447), content-addressable parallel processors. A propagator network IS a dataflow graph — these are MORE natural targets than von-Neumann GPUs.

### FR6 — Cost models / semirings+quantales / e-graph extraction at the frontier (validates the tropical-quantale choice + per-ISA cost gap for PReduce + non-VN codegen-across-targets)
- <https://effect.systems/blog/egraph-extraction.html> — external-url — community write-up (egglog/extraction-gym ecosystem) proving e-graph DAG-cost extraction is NP-complete via reduction FROM minimum set cover; in NP because reducible TO ILP; uses uniform node costs + strictly-monotonic cost model; cites Stepp 2023 independent Min-SAT-reduction proof.
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2408.17042> — external-url — 'E-Graphs as Circuits, and Optimal Extraction via Treewidth' (Sun/Goharshady et al. 2024). Bijection e-graph↔monotone Boolean circuit; extraction NP-hard AND hard to approximate to ANY constant factor; FPT-in-treewidth DP at 2^O(w^2)·poly(w,n); requires ADDITIVE + LOCAL + monotone node cost; explicitly flags non-additive local cost as OPEN.
- <https://arxiv.org/html/2403.14242> — external-url — 'E-Syn: E-Graph Rewriting with Technology-Aware Cost Functions for Logic Synthesis' (2024). Real per-target (7nm tech library) area/delay cost is NON-linear & NON-monotone, so they LEARN it with XGBoost (R^2≈0.76-0.78) instead of an algebraic cost; explicitly reject ILP's linear-monotone constraint; run SEPARATE per-objective optimizations + manual Pareto exploration; 'pool extraction' heuristic.
- <https://www.csl.cornell.edu/~yc2632/data/smoothe_asplos2025_final.pdf> — external-url — 'SmoothE: Differentiable E-Graph Extraction' (ASPLOS 2025). Softmax-relaxes the 0/1 selection variables → gradient-based extraction; competitive-or-better than ILP/greedy, much faster; but explicitly LIMITED to additive/local/node-wise scalar cost; single-objective; no per-target / non-local cost.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.02413> — external-url — 'Semantic Foundations of Equality Saturation' (ICDT 2025). Fixpoint semantics via TREE AUTOMATA + connection to the database CHASE; acyclicity-based syntactic termination criterion. Notably does NOT use semiring/quantale/lattice cost algebra — the canonical semantic foundation is automata-theoretic, leaving the cost/extraction algebra open.
- <https://2024.splashcon.org/details/splash-2024-oopsla/144/Fast-and-Optimal-Extraction-for-Sparse-Equality-Graphs> — external-url — 'Fast and Optimal Extraction for Sparse Equality Graphs' (OOPSLA 2024, Parreaux et al.; ACM doi 10.1145/3689801). Parameterized optimal DAG-cost extraction for low-treewidth e-graphs (treewidth ≤10) in a fraction of ILP time; complements the circuit/treewidth result.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.13020> — external-url — 'e-boost: Boosted E-Graph Extraction with Adaptive Heuristics and Exact Solving' (ICCAD 2025). State-of-art practical extractor: parallel heuristic extraction + adaptive cost-threshold pruning + warm-start ILP; frames extraction as the NP-hard bottleneck of equality saturation in logic synthesis.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.15882> — external-url — 'Equivalence Hypergraphs: DPO Rewriting for Monoidal E-Graphs' (2024). Categorical semantics: e-graphs as morphisms in SEMILATTICE-ENRICHED symmetric monoidal categories; the algebraic frontier is converging on enriched-category structure for e-graph REWRITING (not yet cost/extraction).
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2112.13290> — external-url — 'An equational approach to enriched distributivity' (2021). QUANTALE-enriched (V-enriched) categories + distributive law between down-set/up-set monads → completely-distributive lattices. Confirms quantale-enrichment is an active, mathematically mature direction adjacent to the cost-lattice story.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.18099> — external-url — 'A Ternary Gamma Semiring Framework for Multi-Objective Network Optimization' (Nov 2025). Ternary Tropical Gamma Semiring (TTGS): MULTI-parameter generalization of the tropical semiring for joint (cost,time,risk) optimization with an O(n^2 m) Bellman-Ford analogue. Closest published prior art to a multi-dimensional per-target cost algebra — but framed as a SEMIRING, not a quantale, and not for codegen.
- <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.06127> — external-url — 'Efficiently Synthesizing Lowest Cost Rewrite Rules for Instruction Selection' (2024). SMT-synthesized equivalence-preserving instruction-selection rewrite rules generated lowest-cost-first against a per-target cost metric; up-to-92x speedups. The per-ISA-cost-driven rewriting frontier in the LLVM/instruction-selection space.
- <https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/spe.1079> — external-url — Lokuciejewski et al. 2011, 'Approximating Pareto-optimal compiler optimization sequences — trade-off between WCET, ACET and code size'. Establishes that per-target multi-objective (worst-case time / avg time / size) compiler optimization is a genuine Pareto problem, historically attacked with evolutionary search, not algebraic cost extraction.

**Key findings:**
- PReduce's FULL design vision (when all tracks close): reduction lives natively on-network as e-graph + DPO + tropical-quantale + GoI on the SAME substrate that hosts parsing/typing/elaboration/module-loading. Four structural identities (not metaphors): the substrate IS the IR; rule application IS propagator firing; cost extraction IS quantale residuation; equivalence classes ARE shared cells. The imperative reduction.rkt (3562 lines verified) is retired in its entirety (Track 8 endgame).
- THREE-LAYER DECOMPOSITION. Layer 1: per-AST-PU compound regions — the AST is ONE Pocket Universe (per PPN Track 1 D.7's 5000-cells->5-PUs lesson); per-node occurrence-state (reduction status, cost-in-context, subst env, provenance) is compound-cell COMPONENTS keyed by node-position, NOT per-node PUs; component values reference Layer 2 by cell-id. Layer 2: shared e-class cells with REFINEMENT-POSET structure (`:lattice :structural :order :refinement`; A<=B iff every term in A is in B), merge = union-find (e-node-set union + structural-hash canonical rep + tropical-min cost + monotone provenance); hashcons = CHAMP (cell-id = structural hash); Lévy-optimal sharing preserved because identical terms share one e-class cell. Layer 3: unified rule registry — ONE CHAMP cell rule-id->rule-data, generalizing the SRE form registry; SRE's prop:ctor-desc-tag becomes a property tag, not a separate registry.
- TWO ORTHOGONAL AXES. Axis 1 (rule-property, determines stratum+parallelism): IN-fragment (Lafont binary-principal-port + strong confluence -> Lévy-optimal/HVM2-style parallelism, S0); adhesive-DPO (critical-pair analysis, S0); confluence-by-construction (SRE pattern, trivial parallelism, S0); non-monotone/retraction-eligible (S(-1)); opaque/FFI (effect stratum). Tags stack; scheduler exploits strongest guarantee. Axis 2 (persistence-regime, determines cacheability): ground/closed (persist freely, key=content-hash), contextual (persist w/ worldview-bitmask), retraction-eligible (contextual + retraction-bit consultation before promotion to ground), open (persist the rule template not the value), opaque (cannot persist). The combination of the two axes is what makes the SH super-optimization story SHIPPABLE.
- TWO-STRATUM ARCHITECTURE is the deliberate minimum. S0 = single monotone rewriting stratum hosting ALL confluent rewriting (IN-fragment + adhesive-DPO + confluence-by-construction); property-tag dispatch applies per-rule guarantees WITHIN S0 (no separate strata per reduction kind — they don't require each other's fixpoint, so stratification.md's test says one stratum). S(-1) = retraction stratum (consumes BSP-LE 2B). Effect-stratum boundary (Architecture AD Stratum 3) is respected via topology-request handoff for opaque cells. Rejected alternatives: many concern-strata (violates stratification test) and single-stratum-with-priority (imperative-flavored; CALM violation since retraction can't share S0).
- SUCCESS CRITERIA (§2.2, the closure test): (1) all 10 reduction.rkt kinds (β/δ/ι/structural/arithmetic/trait-dispatch/NAF-aware/capability-aware/session-typed/FFI) have on-network realizations as rule-registry entries with property tags; (2) reduction.rkt parity (bit-identical for closed terms, semantic-equivalence for open); (3) cost-guided extraction yields MEASURABLY cheaper reductions than naive imperative on targeted patterns (constant folding, CSE, β-η normalization); (4) .pnet round-trips ground-regime e-class state so cold-start re-compile times drop measurably; (5) substrate forward-compatible with future user-defined rewrite rules; (6) SH Track 4's production LLVM substrate has the perf foundation it needs (super-optimization story shippable).
- TRACK 0.3 (.pnet extension + LLVM lowering interface) DOES NOT YET EXIST as a standalone artifact (status ⬜ in the Master). The ONLY PReduce-track-numbered doc on disk is the Track 0.1 sketch. Its scope is enumerated in TWO places: PReduce Master §Track-0.3 (5 sections: e-class serialization/content-hashing; worldview-tag composition; cross-session lookup protocol; SUBSTRATE-CALL BOUNDARY = what an LLVM-substrate consumer reads from .pnet to execute reduction; rule-registry serialization forward-compatible with user-defined rules) and the engineering-inputs memo §2 Track-0.3 entry (cell schema enrichment annotation; rule-registry NAC + worldview-bitmask; e-class registry sub-schema; the substrate-call boundary's enrichment annotation must be VISIBLE to the lowering layer and DETERMINES parallelism strategy: IN-fragment<->HVM2-style massive parallelism, adhesive-DPO<->DPOI critical-pair-aware scheduling).
- The vision ALREADY says a great deal about designing the super-optimizer WITH LLVM lowering in mind — this is a first-class design driver, not an afterthought. Core architectural commitment (concurrency-primitives doc §1): `.pnet` lowers DIRECTLY to LLVM, NOT through MLIR — routing through MLIR's dialect tower is REJECTED on architectural grounds because it would split the single-fixpoint property (tropical-quantale-cost-on-cells makes equality-saturation intrinsic to the merge) across two layers. SH Master Track 2 (Low-PNet IR) is the thin lowering layer between propagator network and LLVM IR, and its thickness is GATED on PReduce outputs: if super-optimization happens at the propagator-network layer (PReduce's job), the lower IR can be thinner. The runtime is a thin Zig kernel (runtime/prologos-runtime.zig).
- The collaborator's independent LLVM-lowering prototype is the OPERATIONAL SPUR for the whole series (Master Origin): they need naive reduction-on-propagators NOW; PReduce delivers the canonical replacement. Crucially the design discipline is: the collaborator's prototype is ONE consumer voice that shapes the design, but the OUTPUT is OUR canonical .pnet commit — their prototype rebases to whatever lands on main. So Track 0.3 is co-designed with SH Track 1 (.pnet network-as-value) with the collaborator as input, not as authority.
- EXTRACTION = THE RESIDUAL OPERATOR, and that operator is ALREADY SHIPPED. The strong claim (E-Graphs §7.5, Tropical-Quantale §10.7): cost-guided extraction IS residuation in the tropical quantale; PReduce does NOT engineer an extractor — it consumes PPN 4C Phase 1B's residual. VERIFIED: tropical-fuel.rkt exists and exports tropical-left-residual = (a b)->(if (>= b a) (- b a) 0); the PPN 4C addendum PIR (2026-05-17) closed with this shipped and explicitly names PReduce Track 4 as the DIRECT consumer. NOTE the staleness: PReduce Master + Track 0.1 repeatedly call Phase 1B 'in flight' / 'shipping'; post-PIR reality is it is SHIPPED. The residual API is also resolved at signature level (\_Q : Q × M → M, Russo 2010 Q-modules) per the engineering memo, closing Track 0.1 §7.7.
- CATEGORICAL FRAME PIVOT (2026-05-09, load-bearing): the primary frame is NO LONGER Biondo et al. M-adhesive ('EGGs Are Adhesive!') but Tiurin-Barrett-Ghica-Hu (TBGH) semilattice-enriched SMC (LICS 2025, arXiv:2406.15882, Thm 6.5 full categorical equivalence). Why it matters for the substrate: our lattice-valued cells ARE the semilattices the enrichment requires (recognition, not new machinery); the theory is CLOSED (not at the active M-adhesive research frontier where parallelism/causality transfer is open per Biondo §7); bindings come free via Moss-Tiurin 2025; multi-dim cost extends via quantale-enrichment. Consequence: SRE 2D guarantees transfer to PReduce's e-class subsystem through SHARED ENRICHMENT, not per-theorem adhesivity audit. Master Q4 superseded.
- NEW LOAD-BEARING REQUIREMENT surfaced post-founding (engineering memo §7): the rule format must support NACs (Negative Application Conditions) FROM DAY ONE — without them, expansive rules (x -> (+ x 0)) don't terminate. NACs are a first-class field in the rule schema (Track 0.2 deliverable, serialized in Track 0.3 .pnet schema), orthogonal to the choice of categorical frame. This is an owner-approval point flagged before Track 0.2 design opens.
- The whole design is mantra-disciplined at every layer (Master §Notes): per-AST-PU regions on-network, e-class cells on-network, rule registry on-network, cost extraction on-network. reduction.rkt's deletion is a MILESTONE not a track activity — it lands only when Track 8 closes after parity + soak + production-default, per workflow.md 'validated != deployed' + 'belt-and-suspenders is a blocking red flag'.

**PReduce implications:**
- Track ordering / dependency chain (Master tracker): Track 1 (e-class cell substrate, the SRE-Track-0 analog) is the smallest unblocking move, gates on PPN 4C Phase 1B (now SHIPPED) + Track 0.1 closure. Tracks 2-3 add first rule kinds (β as IN-fragment; ι as adhesive-DPO) on Track 1's substrate. Track 4 (cost-guided extraction) is a direct consumer of the shipped tropical residual. Track 5 (persistence) extends .pnet with e-class state. Track 6 (speculative reduction) consumes BSP-LE 2B hypercube. Track 7 (effect boundary) coordinates with Architecture AD. Track 8 (reduction.rkt parity + retirement) is the multi-track endgame. Track 9 (user-facility forward-compat) is DESIGN-ONLY, not shipped.
- Because PPN 4C Phase 1B is now SHIPPED (post-PIR 2026-05-17), the documented prerequisite gate on Track 1/Track 4 is CLEARED. The remaining gate for Track 1 is 'Track 0.1 closure' — but Track 0.1 is a living research sketch (not a design commitment), so a concrete Track 1 DESIGN doc still has to be written. Recommend the series next-action be: write Track 0.2 (rule taxonomy, with NAC field) + Track 0.3 (.pnet schema) + the Track 0.1 §10.4/§2/§5/§7.7 revisions the engineering memo §9 enumerates (~1-3 days mechanical), BEFORE opening Track 1 implementation.
- Track 1 e-class cell NTT declaration was UPGRADED by the engineering memo: from `:lattice :structural :order :refinement` to `:lattice :structural :enrichment :semilattice` ( + `:Q-module Q` when cost in scope). Merge IS the semilattice join +; e-class membership IS existence of the join; hashcons+union-find IS the quotient functor π : C -> C/S,E. Any Track 1 design must adopt the enrichment-annotated declaration, not the refinement-poset-only one in Track 0.1 §4.2.
- The PIR forward-reference says Track 1 e-class cells adopt `:tier 'hot` + specialized-storage from the PPN 4C addendum's specialized-cell-type framework, and hashcons = cell-id-from-structural-hash. This couples PReduce Track 1 to the addendum's Cell/Propagator/Scheduler-orthogonality discipline (storage strategy is a CELL-level concern, scheduler-independent) — Track 1 design must locate the e-class cell's specialized storage at the cell layer, not piggyback on BSP machinery.
- C3.e is the single most consequential engineering claim (engineering memo §8): the SAME residuation operator (\_Q : Q × M → M) implements BOTH PReduce cost-guided extraction (against the cost-Q-module) AND the Logic Engine's backward-chaining propagators (against the propagator-Q-module) — they differ only in the (Q,M) instantiation, not the operator code. Falsification target: find a backward-chaining operation that doesn't fit the signature. If none surfaces, Track 4 yields a code-reduction merge with existing backward-chaining paths + tropical-fuel's tropical-left-residual.
- Open questions the vision explicitly leaves to implementation tracks: Q1 granularity dial for Layer 1 (per-node-position vs per-(node,concern) vs coarser — empirical, Track 1); Q2 e-class merge under partial information (enrichment says merge is monotone+order-independent but immediate-vs-wait is a scheduling choice); Q7 effect-stratum re-entry protocol (does re-entry retract prior cross-boundary reductions or only add?); §7.8 fixpoint termination across non-confluent rules (does the tropical fuel cell bound saturation DEPTH structurally, or only via fuel exhaustion? — the two are different).
- Binder handling (Q8, §7.2) is load-bearing because Prologos is binder-rich (dependent types, λ, Σ, Π). Leading direction: Moss-Tiurin 2025 'E-Graphs with Bindings' (builds on TBGH, same enrichment framework, lifted to closed SMCs) — handles α-equivalence, capture-avoiding substitution, de Bruijn indexing. Integration hooks: existing substitution + zonking infrastructure. Open: whether de Bruijn is canonical throughout or only at e-class boundaries (Track 1+ design).
- The SRE-2D-absorption question (§7.6) is a near-term design fork: do SRE Track 2D's 13 DPO rules cleanly absorb into PReduce's unified rule registry as confluence-by-construction entries (full absorption — SRE form registry IS the rule registry), or retain enough specialness (prop:ctor-desc-tag) to stay a separate-but-protocol-compatible registry (partial absorption)? Leading: full absorption; fallback if it introduces dispatch overhead or breaks SRE optimizations. Decided Track 1 or Track 3.
- Persistence (Track 5) has an owner-registered refinement: the load-bearing object is the persistent REGISTRY OF OPTIMAL REWRITES (cost-extraction RESULTS keyed by the residuation PROBLEM), not just the persistent e-graph (which Schlatt 2026 covers). Schema: key=(source-e-class-content-hash, cost-criterion-id, worldview-bitmask?), value=chosen-extraction, regime-tag, content-addressing via CHAMP structural hash. Content-addressed by the QUESTION, not the solution-space — richer than Schlatt 2026.
- reduction.rkt retirement (Track 8, Q10) gating criteria are explicitly DEFERRED to Track 8 design time: what parity tests + soak time + production-default duration are required before deletion. The discipline is named upfront (validated != deployed; belt-and-suspenders blocking red flag) so the series doesn't ship dual reducers as a permanent 'safety net'.
- Cross-series sequencing risk: Q11 — PPN Track 4D (Attribute Grammar Substrate Unification) would collapse typing/elaboration/reduction into one attribute-grammar substrate. PReduce can advance INDEPENDENTLY and contribute its substrate when 4D opens (4D's prereqs: PPN 4C completion + T-3 + PM Track 12). Decision-point #4: design PReduce pessimistically (stands alone) with 4D-compat hooks vs wait for 4D — lean is pessimistic.
- Staleness flags for any future PReduce reader (cross-check before relying): (a) Master + Track 0.1 say PPN 4C Phase 1B is 'in flight'/'shipping' — it is SHIPPED (PIR 2026-05-17); (b) Master says reduction.rkt is ~4000 lines — it is 3562; (c) the engineering-inputs memo §10 lists pending corpus-housekeeping amendments (E-Graphs §3.1, Hypergraph §3.3, Track-0.1 §10.4, Master cross-series, Novelty survey) that may or may not have landed — git status shows E-Graphs, Novelty-survey, and PReduce-Master as MODIFIED, suggesting the housekeeping pass is partially applied.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- CORE COMMITMENT for any super-optimizer-with-LLVM-in-mind design: .pnet lowers DIRECTLY to LLVM, NOT through MLIR (concurrency-primitives doc §1, citing super-optimizing-compiler §3.1). Rationale: tropical-quantale cost on cells makes equality saturation INTRINSIC to the merge operation; routing through MLIR's dialect tower would split that single-fixpoint property across two layers. This is the load-bearing architectural decision the Track 0.3 substrate-call boundary must respect — the LLVM consumer reads .pnet and executes reduction-as-merge, it does NOT translate to an intermediate e-graph dialect (unlike eqsat-MLIR / DialEgg / Schlatt-2026 which Prologos explicitly positions AGAINST).
- Track 0.3's substrate-call boundary (the bridge to the collaborator's LLVM lowering) has a SPECIFIC documented obligation (engineering memo §2 Track-0.3): the cell schema's ENRICHMENT ANNOTATION must be visible to the lowering layer because it DETERMINES the parallelism strategy at lowering time — IN-fragment cells lower to HVM2-style massive parallelism; adhesive-DPO cells lower to DPOI critical-pair-aware scheduling. So the .pnet serialization MUST carry, per cell: enrichment annotation (:semilattice for S0 / :commutative-monoid for S(-1) / :Q-module+quantale-id for cost-bearing cells), and per rule: property tags + NAC specs + worldview-bitmask for retraction-eligible entries.
- SH Master Track 2 (Low-PNet IR) is the thin lowering layer between the propagator network and LLVM IR (cells as typed memory regions; propagators as functions over those regions; scheduler as worklist data structure; lattice merges inlined or dispatched). Its THICKNESS is gated on PReduce: SH Open-Q2 — if super-optimization happens at the propagator-network layer (which is exactly PReduce's job), the lower IR can be thinner. Design implication: PReduce should aim to do extraction/optimization at the network layer so Track 2 stays a thin lowering, not a second optimizer.
- The runtime that executes lowered .pnet is a thin Zig kernel (runtime/prologos-runtime.zig, currently 544 lines single-threaded; HAMT in prologos-hamt.zig). The BSP discipline (snapshot -> fire-all -> merge -> schedule) is the parallelism target; CALM-monotone within S0. PReduce's e-graph merges land on THIS kernel — so PReduce's per-cell merge functions (set-union, tropical-min cost, union-find) become the kernel's per-domain merge dispatch.
- Cell-partitioned merge (concurrency-primitives §4) is the architecture that lets PReduce's expensive merges scale: per-(worker, PU) write logs (zero cross-worker atomics during fire), then cells partitioned by cid mod P so each cell has exactly ONE merger. §10 Q7 explicitly: PReduce makes the merge function more expensive (not last-write-wins) so cell-partitioned merge is EVEN MORE important under PReduce — re-measure post-PReduce. Design implication for PReduce: e-class merge must be expressible as a per-cell deterministic function the partitioned merger can call.
- Per-domain merge dispatch (§10 Q1): when PReduce's tropical-min + set-union + e-graph-union merges arrive, the kernel needs the right merge fn per cell; function-pointer dispatch ~5ns, comptime specialization (one merge loop per domain) eliminates it. The 'Write ourselves' catalog (§7.3) already budgets 'comptime per-tag specialized fire batches (~150 LOC, LLVM autovectorization target)' specific to the .pnet tag set — PReduce rule kinds become tags here.
- EQREL / parallel union-find is the algorithm to study for PReduce e-graph merges specifically (concurrency-primitives §8.1): if merge fns reduce to equivalence-closure (named candidate: PReduce e-graph merges + SRE structural unification once fully on-network), Soufflé's EQREL parallel union-find is the reference; wjakob/dset (lock-free C++ disjoint set, path-compression + union-by-rank) and uf_rush (Rust port) are the concrete impls. PReduce Track 1's union-find-as-cell-merge should be designed with this lock-free parallel union-find target in mind.
- .pnet content-addressing is what makes LLVM lowering TRACTABLE across sessions (Track 0.1 §8.4): SH Track 4 isn't 'compile to native' but 'compile + cache + reuse across sessions'; without cross-session e-class persistence (Track 5) every session pays full saturation cost; with it, ground-regime equivalences amortize across compilations — the Salsa-style incremental-compilation story realized at the equality-saturation level. Design hook: Track 0.3's content-hashing scheme + Track 5's regime-tagged cache are jointly the cross-session LLVM-compilation cache.
- Two .pnet content-addressing properties the super-optimizing-compiler doc makes load-bearing (§3.6): a propagator network has a stable hash from cell-values + propagator-structure; two .pnet files with the same hash are not just bit-equal but FIXPOINT-equal with the same derivation tree (stronger than IPVM deterministic-Wasm). PReduce's e-class cells inherit this; deployment artifacts carry not just the compiled program but the equivalence reasoning that proved it equivalent — design Track 0.3 so the e-class derivation/provenance round-trips.
- Stratified rewriting subsumes pass ordering (super-optimizing-compiler §3.7): instead of LLVM-style pass-pipeline tuning, optimizations live at strata determined by their algebraic properties (monotone -> S0, retracting -> S(-1)). For the LLVM target this means the 'optimization pipeline' is the stratified scheduler, and PReduce's S0/S(-1) two-stratum design IS the pass-order story — the LLVM backend receives an already-extracted cheapest term, not an optimization problem.
- NUMA / cell-affinity hooks at lowering time (concurrency-primitives §5): when .pnet is lowered the dataflow graph is known, so cells written by propagators in the same dataflow component should be NUMA-co-located; this is a static-analysis pass at LOWERING time, not a runtime concern. PReduce's e-class cell layout (Track 0.3 'specific to our .pnet format') should preserve the numa_hint API surface even though policy is deferred. False-sharing: cells must be cache-line-aligned (CellSlot extern struct, 64B / 128B-on-aarch64) before cells become dynamic — PReduce's many e-class cells make this acute.
- Sibling-PU parallelism for PReduce speculation (concurrency-primitives §6): pu_run_parallel batched-run primitive runs N independent child PUs concurrently (ATMS branches with disjoint worldview bits). PReduce Track 6 (cost-bounded ATMS branching for non-confluent rules) maps directly onto this — each ATMS branch = one enrichment specialization = one child PU; cost-bounded pruning across branches = tropical-quantale residuation across worldviews. Design Track 6 to emit the pu_run_parallel shape.
- GPU as a future PReduce target (concurrency-primitives §10 Q4): a BSP round is a kernel launch; per-cell merge is atomicOr/atomicAdd/segment-scan; the prologos_pu_run API is GPU-friendly — a GPU dialect on the SAME .pnet substrate, 100-1000x headroom for bit-parallel inner loops. Speculative, off the immediate path, but the .pnet/Track-0.3 schema should not preclude it (the e-graph match/merge inner loop is exactly the bit-parallel kind that benefits).

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- The engineering memo §1.4 + C4.f make a phase-collapse claim DIRECTLY tied to PReduce's substrate-IS-IR thesis: the GoI execution formula = Kleene star in a traced enriched SMC, and run-to-quiescence on the propagator network IS the GoI machine structurally — so 'compile time' and 'run time' are the SAME Kleene-star/trace operator firing on DIFFERENT cell contents. Phase collapse (Paper A0 H2) thus gets a categorical witness candidate: the typing-cell fixpoint and the reduction-cell fixpoint are the same kind of computation on the same scheduler.
- This is the conceptual core of 'the substrate IS the IR' (super-optimizing-compiler §3.1): there is no high-level-IR + optimization-framework stack; typing rules, reduction rules, optimization rules, and code-gen rules all fire as propagators on the SAME network. PReduce collapses the reduction PHASE into the same substrate that does typing/elaboration — phases become STRATA, cross-phase information flow becomes cell-state propagation (Schlatt-2026's cross-phase-persistence benefit obtained by construction rather than as an add-on, E-Graphs §5.5).
- INFERRED: phase collapse is what makes PReduce's cost-guided extraction able to feed back into typing — because reduction equivalences live in the same e-class cells the type checker reads, an equivalence discovered during reduction is immediately visible to type-level equality (E-Graphs §8.1 type-level e-classes: Vec n A ≡ Vec m A iff n ≡ m). This is the 'persistent compiler abstraction unlocks cross-phase optimization' story (Schlatt 2026) realized as a structural property of the collapsed-phase substrate.
- Falsification target C4.f (engineering memo §4) is load-bearing for Paper A0 and depends on PReduce implementation discipline: keeping the typing-cell and the reduction-cell on the SAME scheduler so the same Kleene-star/trace operator demonstrably fires on both. If PReduce implements reduction on a separate network/scheduler, the phase-collapse witness is lost — a design constraint on PReduce, not just a paper claim.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- INFERRED (not addressed in these docs under that name): the closest reach to non-von-Neumann execution is the massively-parallel interaction-net runtime target. PReduce Track 2 (β as IN-fragment) explicitly targets HVM2 as the benchmark/upper-bound (engineering memo Track 2 + C4.e): HVM2's 400 MIPS single-thread -> 5200 MIPS (16 threads) -> 74000 MIPS (RTX 4090, 32768 threads). The IN-fragment of PReduce's enriched SMC is CONJECTURED to inherit HVM2-style massive parallelism + Lévy-optimal sharing — the empirical implementation IS the test (Master Q8 + C4.e falsification target).
- GPU-as-concurrency-target (concurrency-primitives §10 Q4) is the most explicit non-CPU-architecture reach: a BSP round as a GPU kernel launch, per-cell merge as atomicOr/atomicAdd/segment-scan, a GPU dialect on the same .pnet substrate with 100-1000x headroom for bit-parallel inner loops. PReduce's e-graph match/merge is exactly the bit-parallel inner loop that would benefit. Speculative; off the immediate path; flagged so the schema doesn't preclude it.
- INFERRED: the Hyperlattice-Conjecture framing (CLAUDE.md mantra + Novelty survey) that 'the Hasse diagram IS the optimal parallel decomposition' is the theoretical basis for any non-von-Neumann reach — if the lattice topology uniquely determines the parallel structure, then PReduce's e-class refinement-poset Hasse diagram IS the parallel structure cost-extraction walks (E-Graphs §7.1, SRE lens Q6). The Novelty survey flags this lattice-Hasse-adjacency-IS-parallel-decomposition claim as APPARENT NOVELTY to verify — e-graphs achieve parallelism via phase-separation+rebuilding, not lattice topology.
- Distributed equality saturation (E-Graphs §8.6) is the multi-node reach: a distributed e-graph is a colimit of local e-graphs over a sync category; CALM says monotone computation is coordination-free; e-graph saturation is monotone -> distributed saturation is IN PRINCIPLE coordination-free. Practically deferred (sharding, sync frequency, identity-preserving partition are engineering questions); PReduce ships single-node first, distributed extension aligned with the multi-agent/Vat/DCR vision. No von-Neumann-bottleneck framing is used explicitly in these docs.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- VERIFIED novelty posture (Track 0.1 §8.1 + super-optimizing-compiler §10 + Novelty survey §5): each constituent piece is independently grounded in literature; the COMPOSITION is what is novel. The architectural endpoint puts on the SAME substrate what the existing super-optimization frontier addresses one engine at a time: equality saturation (egg/egglog), SMT/stochastic superoptimization (Souper/Hydra/Stoke/Minotaur), cost-driven scheduling (Halide/TVM), interaction-net runtimes (HVM2), GoI abstract machines. The thesis is explicitly NOT 'faster than LLVM' — it's that the COMBINATION of 12 dimensions on one substrate is novel (super-optimizing-compiler §4 table).
- Closest existing PEER is Schlatt et al. 2026 'E-Graphs as a Persistent Compiler Abstraction' (arXiv:2602.16707): e-graph as first-class IR construct (eqsat dialect, xDSL/MLIR), persistence ACROSS compilation phases. Prologos's e-class cells fill the eqsat-dialect role; cell-state persistence (.pnet + content-addressing) maps to their cross-phase persistence and EXTENDS it to cross-SESSION (E-Graphs §4.6, §5.5). Prologos's differentiators over Schlatt: substrate-level integration (not a dialect over the IR), cross-session (not just cross-phase), tropical-quantale extraction, BSP-LE speculation.
- eqsat-MLIR-dialect (arXiv:2505.09363) is the closest 'substrate IS the IR' analog — e-graph as a first-class IR construct. Prologos goes further: the substrate (propagator network) IS the IR rather than an e-graph dialect OVER an IR, and explicitly lowers .pnet to LLVM WITHOUT going through MLIR (concurrency-primitives §1). Cranelift aegraphs (Fallin 2023) is the first PRODUCTION e-graph compiler but acyclic/greedy single-pass — gives up expressivity for speed; Prologos argues adhesive/enriched guarantees make that trade unnecessary (super-optimizing-compiler §3.3).
- egglog (Zhang et al. PLDI 2023 'Better Together') is the closest PUBLISHED precedent for combining lattice-based reasoning (Datalog) with equality saturation (e-graphs) in a parallel-friendly engine (Novelty survey §5: PARTIAL PRECEDENT). egglog's lattice-value-per-eclass IS the direct analog of PReduce's cost-cell-per-e-class (E-Graphs §3.4, §4.2). DIFFERENCE: egglog uses lattices for incremental analysis + e-graphs for term equality (two mechanisms); Prologos puts EVERYTHING on lattice-valued cells. The APPARENT-NOVELTY claim (flagged for external review): lattice-Hasse-adjacency IS the parallel decomposition — egg/egglog parallelize via phase-separation+rebuilding+`-j`, NOT via lattice topology.
- Primary categorical frame is Tiurin-Barrett-Ghica-Hu (TBGH) LICS 2025 'Equivalence Hypergraphs: DPO Rewriting for Monoidal E-Graphs' (arXiv:2406.15882), full equivalence Thm 6.5; bindings via Moss-Tiurin 2025 'E-Graphs With Bindings' (arXiv:2505.00807, builds on TBGH not Biondo). Alternative/comparison frame: Biondo-Castelnovo-Gadducci CALCO 2025 'EGGs are Adhesive!' (arXiv:2503.13678), T_Σ-adhesive (a form of M-adhesive); standard DPO parallelism/causality transfer is OPEN per their own §7. The substrate-research note recommends TBGH because our lattice-valued cells ARE the required semilattices and the theory is closed.
- Residual-operator-as-extraction is grounded in Russo 2010 'Quantale Modules and their Operators' (arXiv:1002.0968, signature \_Q : Q × M → M) + Module Theory §6 (e-graphs as quotient modules) + the tropical-quantale residual (Lawvere quantale, Gaubert et al. residuation of tropical series — decidable/polynomial under finiteness). Bonchi-Gadducci-Kissinger-Sobociński-Zanasi 'String diagram rewrite theory III' (MSCS 2022) gives DECIDABILITY of DPOI confluence for terminating systems — directly applicable to Track 3 (ι-reduction), moving it off the open M-adhesive frontier into a closed framework.
- The OVERALL apparent-novelty conjunction (Novelty survey §9, flagged for external review): a compiler that is (a) self-hosted, (b) dependently typed, (c) the compiler IS a propagator network, and (d) parallel-decomposition tied to lattice structure — no production-grade dependently-typed compiler (Lean 4, Idris 2) has made this architectural claim. PReduce is the linchpin that adds 'super-optimizing' to this list (super-optimizing-compiler §5). HONEST CAVEAT (§6 speculative): whether the novel combination is also FASTER/more-correct/more-usable than the existing literature is an empirical question requiring PReduce to land.

**Open questions:**
- Track 0.3 (.pnet + LLVM lowering interface) exists ONLY as a scope outline (Master §Track-0.3 + engineering-memo §2 Track-0.3) — no standalone artifact written. Its detailed substrate-call-boundary protocol (precisely what bytes the LLVM consumer reads to execute reduction; how rule property tags + NAC + enrichment annotations + worldview-bitmask serialize; CHAMP-friendly chunking for load-on-demand) is UNDESIGNED. This is the largest gap between vision and concrete spec for the LLVM-lowering dimension.
- Whether the corpus-housekeeping amendments listed in the engineering memo §10 have all landed: git status shows E-Graphs, Novelty-survey, and PReduce-Master as MODIFIED (consistent with partial application of the TBGH-reframe), but Track-0.1 §10.4 already shows the amended TBGH-primary references — so the pass is partially done. A future reader cannot assume any single doc reflects the final TBGH framing without checking; could not fully confirm which amendments are complete from disk state alone.
- §7.8 / Master open: does the tropical fuel cell bound saturation DEPTH structurally, or only via fuel exhaustion? The two are different (structural termination would let us PROVE specific reductions terminate; fuel only guarantees forward progress within budget). Open theoretical question, Track 4+ — unresolved in the read docs.
- Q2 (e-class merge under partial information): enrichment axioms say merge is monotone + order-independent, but immediate-vs-wait when only some equivalence-witnesses are computed remains a SCHEDULING choice (parallelism vs information-preservation trade-off). Named but not decided.
- Effect-stratum re-entry semantics (§7.5, Q7, decision-point #5): when a previously-opaque cell becomes evaluable, does PReduce re-entry RETRACT prior reductions that crossed the opaque boundary, or only ADD new ones? Leading proposal is a per-effect-stratum-completion topology request PReduce subscribes to, but the retract-vs-add semantics is undesigned. Track 7.
- Cost lattice composition (Q5): single tropical quantale vs product/tensor/lex of multiple quantales (instruction-count × code-size × power × latency)? Math foundation supplied (Candidate D quantale-enriched extension; per-Q-shape algorithms in engineering memo Track 4) but the choice of Q is an S1 commitment NOT yet made — and it materially affects the LLVM cost model the extractor optimizes against.
- Lévy-optimality reachability (Q8): does Lévy-optimal sharing extend to dependent types + QTT + sessions, or only a restricted fragment? Conjectural transfer via C4.e (HVM2 connection); empirical implementation (Track 2 β-reduction benchmark vs HVM2) is the test. Whether PReduce's IN-fragment actually achieves HVM2-class parallelism is unverified.
- The NAC requirement (engineering memo §7) is an OWNER-APPROVAL point flagged before Track 0.2 opens but no evidence it has been acknowledged/approved in the read docs — could not confirm whether Track 0.2 design has incorporated it as the first-class rule-schema field the memo demands.
- INFERRED open question (not raised in docs): the docs assert .pnet-direct-to-LLVM (no MLIR) AND that SH Track 2 (Low-PNet IR) is a thin lowering layer whose thickness is gated on PReduce. But how PReduce's extracted-cheapest-term hands off to Track 2's typed-memory-region lowering — and whether extraction happens before or interleaved with lowering — is not specified anywhere read. The boundary between 'PReduce extraction at network layer' and 'Track 2 lowering to LLVM IR' is conceptually asserted but operationally undefined.
- Whether the C3.e merge target (one residual operator for both cost-extraction and Logic-Engine backward-chaining) actually holds is an explicit FALSIFICATION TARGET, not a confirmed result — the unification refactor (engineering memo §9 step 5) has not been done; if a backward-chaining operation that doesn't fit \_Q : Q × M → M surfaces, the merge target fails.
