This is 'partly upstream of NTT progress.' The on-write-check / on-read-check predicate fields face the identical problem and the identical symbol-ref solution.
- Q2b LOWERING SEMANTICS — directive-by-directive (dailies line 66, VERIFIED struct at propagator.rkt:327). A native (LLVM/Zig) backend reading a CELL-TABLE entry emits: tier='hot → place in hot memory region + inline fast-path read/write; storage='monotone-counter → atomic fixnum cell (lock-free increment, bypass CHAMP merge); storage='general → CHAMP/hashmap-backed; fires-on='threshold-crossing → emit dependent-notification ONLY inside the on-write-check-true branch (elide notification on the common monotone-grow case); fires-on='any-change → notify on every write. on-write-check (when symbolic-ref-resolved) lowers to an inline predicate that, when true, writes the contradiction cell structurally. This is a DECLARATION→CODEGEN table, not an interpreter.
- SCHEDULER-AS-IR must also round-trip (PM-13 §3, SH Master Track 2). Low-PNet IR treats 'scheduler as worklist data structure' — so the worklist (currently off-network in prop-net-warm), the stratum-handler registry (currently a Racket box), and fuel/worldview-cache (ALREADY cells) are all part of the serializable IR. Q2a is incomplete until scheduler-state is on-network cells; handler-as-data (PM-13 Option β) is required because handler-as-closure reintroduces the unserializable-closure problem at the scheduler layer.
- CURRENT STALENESS MODEL CONSTRAINS THE ARTIFACT-FORMAT CLAIM (VERIFIED pnet-serialize.rkt:465-496). .pnet today invalidates on (a) source mtime+size change and (b) driver_rkt.zo newer than .pnet (infrastructure-stale?). For .pnet to be a DEPLOYMENT artifact (Stage A.5), the format needs content-addressing (path note §10 names IPVM/Fission content-addressed precedent) rather than mtime+zo-timestamp heuristics — the current model assumes a co-located Racket build, which is exactly the dependency Stage A.5 retires.
- INFERRED: the Q2b declaration-half can ship BEFORE the Q2a closure-half. Because specialized-cell-meta's tier/storage/fires-on fields are already plain symbols on a #:transparent struct, a .pnet extension that serializes the CELL-TABLE declarations (minus merge-fn/checks) is mechanically straightforward via the existing struct→vector path — it would round-trip the LLVM-directive vocabulary even while merge-fns still rebuild from a fixed registry. This suggests an incremental Track-1 partition: (1) cell-table declarations first (unblocks Q2b lowering experiments), (2) symbol-ref merge/body table second (unblocks full topology round-trip / Q2a).

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- INFERRED (no explicit phase-collapse / VN-reachability content in the IG4 source set). 'Phase collapse' does not appear in any of the listed docs; the relevant collapse-adjacent concept here is the IR-LAYER collapse: SH Master open-Q2 frames a spectrum from direct-lowering (1 layer, simpler, less optimization) → STG/Cmm two-layer (GHC) → MLIR n-layer dialect tower. The thinner the Low-PNet IR, the more 'collapsed' the propagator-network→LLVM path; PReduce-at-network-layer is what permits collapsing the lower IR (see preduce_implications). This is an IR-stratification choice, not the dynamical phase-collapse this schema field likely targets.
- See open_questions for the cross-facet flag — phase-collapse implications are out of scope for the .pnet-IR facet and should be sourced from the facet covering reduction/quiescence dynamics.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- INFERRED (no von-Neumann-reachability material in the IG4 source set). The nearest architecturally-relevant point: the §4.6 IR vocabulary's whole purpose (dailies line 74) is that 'each runtime READS THE SAME DECLARATIONS DIFFERENTLY' — a .pnet's specialized-cell declarations are interpreted by a Racket scheduler, an LLVM-native backend, a WASM backend (SH Track 8), and future concurrent/distributed/CRDT schedulers (:concurrency 'lock-free / :distribution 'crdt) without re-deriving the cell/propagator layer. That portability across NON-von-Neumann execution substrates (distributed, content-addressed WASM per IPVM/Fission, future actor/Vat runtimes) is enabled BY the declaration-as-IR-vocabulary design — but the source docs do not frame this in von-Neumann-reachability terms.
- The Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality principle (on-network.md, codified in Phase 1) is the load-bearing constraint that keeps the IR backend-neutral: optimizations must live at the cell or propagator layer (declarations), never piggyback scheduler-specific machinery — precisely so the .pnet IR lowers to ANY scheduler including non-von-Neumann ones.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- GENUINELY DISTINCTIVE: specialized-cell DECLARATIONS as a shared IR vocabulary read differently by interpreter / LLVM / WASM / distributed schedulers (dailies §4.6 framing). This is not the standard 'IR with metadata' pattern — the declarations (:tier/:storage/:fires-on) are first-class struct fields ALREADY exercised at runtime in Racket (3.94 ns fast path) that double as codegen directives. The dual-use (runtime-hint AND backend-directive from the same declaration, validated at one layer before being lowered to another) is the novel claim.
- PRIOR ART HONESTLY NAMED in the docs themselves: SH Master Track 2 explicitly analogizes Low-PNet IR to GHC's STG/Cmm split and MLIR's dialect tower; path note §4 names NTT-style propagators-as-data; SH Master/path note name Idris 2 (QTT erasure), Lean 4 (self-hosting + native compilation, 'closest peer in design space'), Rust (OCaml bootstrap retirement), GHC (C runtime decades before Haskell-in-Haskell). The propagator-network-as-IR is the Prologos-distinctive layer; the LOWERING machinery (ANF/closure-conversion, GC, worklist) is acknowledged standard compiler tech.
- 'merge-functions-as-symbolic-refs-in-a-form-registry' (path note §4) is the propagator-network analog of defunctionalization / closure-conversion + a symbol table — standard technique, novel application (the registry is the SRE form registry, shared with structural unification). The honest framing: the TECHNIQUE is prior art; binding it to the SRE/NTT declarative-propagator vocabulary is the new part.
- INFERRED novelty caveat: the claim 'the compiler EMERGED from the infrastructure build-out, not designed-for' (dailies line 68, Hyperlattice Conjecture self-vindication) is a strong originality narrative but is a project-internal interpretive claim, not an externally-benchmarked result; flag as aspirational until the Zip/Zig+LLVM PoC is documented in a committed artifact (see open_questions).

**Open questions:**
- The 'Zip PoC' / 'Zig scheduler + LLVM lowering' that allegedly PROVED .pnet→LLVM lowering feasible (dailies lines 60,68,4735) is referenced but NOT located in a committed artifact within the IG4 source set. Where is its code/writeup? The task explicitly excludes 'the contributor PoC' — is the Zip/Zig PoC the same thing as the contributor PoC, or a separate internal experiment? This distinction matters for whether the 'weeks-to-months, feasibility proven' claim is grounded. FLAG: confirm artifact location before treating feasibility as established.
- Does the symbolic merge-fn registry already exist in a form ready to receive .pnet symbol-refs? The path note says merge-fns must become 'symbolic references registered in the SRE form registry'; VERIFIED that lookup-merge-fn-domain / register-merge-fn!/lattice exist (referenced in propagator.rkt:1368 + propagator-design.md), but NOT confirmed whether every production merge-fn is registered-by-symbol vs anonymous-closure. If anonymous closures remain, Q2a needs a registration sweep first. Needs a grep audit of all merge-fn definitions.
- Exact serialization status of the specialized-cell-meta predicate fields (on-write-check, on-read-check): these are closures TODAY (e.g., fuel-cost's cost-crosses-budget check). They face the same closure-unserializability as merge-fn and need the same symbol-ref treatment, but no doc explicitly addresses them — confirm they are in the Q2a symbol-table scope.
- Stratification round-trip: how do stratum handlers (off-network Racket box + procedures today, PM-13 PRIMARY scope) serialize into .pnet? PM-13 is a Stage-0 SEED note with NO design committed; until handler-as-data (Option β) is designed, the scheduler-state half of a round-tripped network is undefined. Is Q2a (Track 1) blocked on PM-13, or can it round-trip value/topology cells while leaving the handler registry rebuilt-fresh as a documented partial?
- Content-addressing for the deployment-artifact format: path note §10 names IPVM/Fission content-addressed precedent, but the current staleness model (mtime + driver.zo timestamp) is build-co-located. No doc specifies the content-addressing scheme that would make .pnet a standalone deployment artifact independent of a Racket build. Open design question for Track 1/Stage A.5.
- Schema mismatch flag: this facet (.pnet→IR) has no material for phase_collapse_implications or non_vn_reach_implications — those dimensions belong to a reduction-dynamics / VN-reachability facet, not the serialization/lowering facet. Populated as INFERRED with explicit out-of-scope notes.


## IG5 — LRP / CALM / lattice substrate vs the von-Neumann bottleneck (keep-non-vN-in-reach grounding)

**Mode:** internal-doc  |  **SHA:** e5ede4b2bfffd0f672b7b574b451667112ad522f (verified via git rev-parse HEAD; matches the expected PIN)

**Sources:**
- (our-doc) docs/tracking/principles/EFFECTFUL_COMPUTATION_ON_PROPAGATORS.org — The Layered Recovery Principle (LRP) canonical doc. Defines LRP precisely; logic-engine + session/effect 5-layer applications; CALM-optimality argument; theoretical-foundations + inspirations sections. Read in full (396 lines).
- (our-doc) docs/tracking/principles/DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org §'Cell / Propagator / Scheduler Orthogonality' (lines 731-1049) + §'Hyperlattice Conjecture' (432-470) + §'Propagator-First / Stratified / Data Orientation' (483-673) — The scheduler-as-portability-seam principle (the load-bearing source for non-vN reachability): 3 demonstrated schedulers + Zig+LLVM + distributed runtime all run the SAME network identically; optimization-location rule; scheduler-state cells; where the distributed-concurrent runtime lives. LRP cross-referenced at 540, 665. Read in detail.
- (our-doc) .claude/rules/on-network.md §'Cell / Propagator / Scheduler Orthogonality' — Operational form of the orthogonality constraint: on-network optimization is principled iff its semantics are identical under ANY scheduler; red-flag = scheduler-coupled optimization (BSP-round-drain piggyback). In conversation context.
- (our-doc) docs/research/2026-05-02_PARALLEL_ALGORITHMS_SURVEY.md (full, 783 lines) — The parallel-decomposition basis. BSP as Valiant's 'bridging model' deliberately playing von Neumann's role for parallel; KPN/Kahn-principle = monotone-fixpoint = parallel determinism; CALM in the synthesis (Themes 4,5); Theme 4 = communication (data movement, ~100x flop energy) dominates modern cost = the von-Neumann/memory-wall constraint; PIM + CA-algorithms as the two responses; hypercube = Hasse diagram of Boolean lattice Q_n; dataflow architecture inverts the fetch-execute cycle.
- (our-doc) docs/research/2026-05-05_ASYNCHRONOUS_PROGRAMMING_QUANTALE_RESEARCH_PROGRAM.md (556KB; read §0-2 frame/dichotomy, §3.3.6-3.3.10 CCP-quantale isomorphism, §3.5.4 Q-modules, §7.5 quantale-substrate hypothesis, §4.3/§4.6/§5.5 diagnostic+CALM+timely-dataflow, §4.5.5/§6.2 distributed runtime, §8 synthesis, §9.1 L2 track) — The quantale-enriched cc(D) research program. Establishes propagator net = Saraswat cc(D) (settled, §3.3.7); tropical-cost cells = soft cc(Q); the lattice substrate 'dissolves the sync/async dichotomy by replacing both with a third: refinement on a lattice' (§2.6); BloomL/CALM as the existing industrial-quality lattice-distributed model (§5.5); L2 multi-network runtime = federation of vat-like single-stratum BSP nets with N⊗T-valued causal clocks (§8.4, §9.1).
- (our-doc) docs/tracking/principles/DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org §'Propagators as Universal Computational Substrate' + §'Hyperlattice Conjecture' — The thesis that propagator nets are a universal computational substrate; Hyperlattice Conjecture (every computable fn = lattice fixpoint; Hasse diagram = optimal parallel decomposition). The universality argument 'relies on scheduler-independence' (line 817-821) — directly ties non-vN reachability to the orthogonality principle.

**Key findings:**
- LRP — PRECISE STATEMENT (verbatim, EFFECTFUL_COMPUTATION_ON_PROPAGATORS.org §'The Insight'): 'Non-monotone behavior is recovered on a monotone substrate by inserting control layers between phases of monotone computation. The control layers perform the non-monotone operations (effect execution, negation evaluation, choice commitment) at precisely the points where coordination is required.' The mechanism: REASONING about non-monotone domains (what effects, what order, what dependencies) stays monotone ON the propagator network; EXECUTING the non-monotone step happens at a control boundary AFTER the monotone reasoning reaches fixpoint. It is a general design methodology (5 steps: identify monotone core -> encode as cells/propagators -> identify non-monotone boundary -> insert control layer -> use ATMS for hypotheticals), proven on 2 domains (logic engine negation/cut; session-derived effect ordering).
- LRP IS THE CALM THEOREM MADE CONSTRUCTIVE. CALM (Hellerstein 2010): a program has a consistent coordination-free implementation iff it is monotone. IO/negation/choice are non-monotone => coordination required. LRP's claim of OPTIMALITY (§'The CALM Optimality'): all reasoning is monotone+coordination-free; the ONLY coordination point is the final control barrier (Layer 5 effect handler); 'no architecture can eliminate this barrier without violating the CALM theorem.' So LRP minimizes coordination to the provable CALM-mandated minimum — it does not fight the monotone substrate, it layers control on top of it.
- THE rel/proc PARALLEL: both Prologos engines (logic rel; session/process proc) share ONE 3-layer architecture: Propagator Network (monotone fixpoints) + ATMS (hypothetical/worldview reasoning, still monotone — adding nogoods only shrinks valid worldviews) + Control Layer (the minimal non-monotone extension at the CALM-required boundary). LRP is the common theory under both. This is the structural evidence that the monotone-substrate + layered-recovery pattern generalizes.
- CELL/PROPAGATOR/SCHEDULER ORTHOGONALITY is the LOAD-BEARING non-vN-reachability principle (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org §731+). Three orthogonal layers: Cell (state + lattice + merge + storage-strategy), Propagator (rule + dependencies + fire-pattern), Scheduler (firing order + parallelism + round structure). Per CALM, monotone computation on fixed topology converges to the SAME fixpoint regardless of evaluation order => the order is PURELY the scheduler's concern. Therefore a CALM-compliant network can be run by ARBITRARY schedulers with identical semantics. Already demonstrated with 3 schedulers (Gauss-Seidel sequential, parallel BSP, sequential BFS-like BSP); Zig+LLVM scheduler experimental; future self-hosted + distributed runtime planned. 'All of these schedulers run the same network with the same semantics.'
- THE VON-NEUMANN BOTTLENECK is named explicitly in the parallel-algorithms survey (Theme 4 + §6 CA-connections): on modern hardware DATA MOVEMENT dominates ARITHMETIC (~100x more energy for off-chip access than a flop, Horowitz/Demmel); 'the von Neumann bottleneck (data movement >> compute) motivates both CA-algorithms (avoid movement) and PIM (move compute to data) — two responses to the same memory-wall constraint.' Dataflow architecture (Dennis-Misunas 1975, quoted in the quantale doc §2.4) 'fundamentally inverts the von Neumann fetch-execute-cycle' — an instruction fires when operands are available, not when a sequencer reaches it. The propagator network IS a dataflow architecture in this exact sense: cells hold data, propagators fire on operand-availability (input-cell refinement), there is no program counter.
- HOW THE DESIGN OVERCOMES THE BOTTLENECK (synthesized): (1) The computation is specified as a fixed-topology lattice fixpoint (cells + monotone propagators), NOT as a sequential instruction stream over shared mutable memory; there is no program-counter/fetch-execute serialization. (2) CALM guarantees the fixpoint is order-independent, so the computation carries NO imposed sequencing for the scheduler/hardware to honor — ordering EMERGES from dataflow depth (the Hasse diagram), which IS the optimal parallel decomposition (Hyperlattice Conjecture optimality claim). (3) Because behavior is DECLARED (lattice + merge + dependencies + fire-pattern) rather than imperatively encoded, the WHAT (cell+propagator) is fully decomplected from the HOW/WHERE (scheduler+runtime), so the same network can be lowered to whatever hardware minimizes data movement (GPU stencil, PIM, distributed nodes) without redesign.
- SCHEDULER-AS-PORTABILITY-SEAM enables future targets WITHOUT redesigning cell+propagator (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org §'Where the Distributed-Concurrent Runtime Lives', 837+): the distributed-concurrent runtime (multi-process, multi-node, async I/O, cross-network message-passing) belongs at the SCHEDULER+NETWORKING layer, NOT in the network. Concretely: per-network parallelism = scheduler concern (work distribution in a BSP round / Gauss-Seidel sub-round); cross-network communication = scheduler+cell-API concern (cells are the boundary; the scheduler marshals values across); async I/O = scheduler concern (scheduler awaits I/O between firings). 'Future runtimes inherit the current networks unchanged.' Optimization-location rule: storage-strategy at Cell, fire-pattern at Propagator, scheduling-efficiency at Scheduler — and the test for a legitimate scheduler-level optimization is 'does it change SEMANTICS (which propagators fire on which cell states) [=> must be cell/propagator] or only EFFICIENCY (when/where/how, same fixpoint) [=> can be scheduler].'
- THE DESIGN PROPERTIES THAT MUST BE PRESERVED to keep non-vN targets reachable (the 'invariants'): (i) MONOTONICITY — every cell merge is a join-semilattice operation; every propagator fire-function is a closure operator (extensive, idempotent, monotone). This is what makes the fixpoint exist (Knaster-Tarski/Kleene) and be coordination-free (CALM). Non-monotone work MUST be isolated to identifiable strata via LRP, not smeared into S0. (ii) ORDER-INDEPENDENCE — no cell's value and no propagator's correctness may depend on scheduler execution mode (sequential vs parallel, sync vs async, fire-immediately vs fire-lazily). A network 'correct under Gauss-Seidel but wrong under BSP has a layering violation.' (iii) DECLARATION-DRIVEN behavior — cell+propagator declarations (lattice, merge, dependencies, fire-pattern, component-paths) must be SUFFICIENT to specify the computation; the scheduler needs only 'fire propagators when their input cells change.' Optimizations declared at cell/propagator level survive any scheduler; optimizations that piggyback on scheduler-specific machinery do not. If any of the three is violated, networks stop being a universal substrate and become 'a specific scheduler's specific computational model' (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org:817-821) — and non-vN portability is lost.
- THE LATTICE SUBSTRATE DISSOLVES THE SYNC/ASYNC DICHOTOMY (quantale doc §2.6, load-bearing): Era-I concurrency theory left an irreducible split — synchronous rendezvous (CSP/CCS/pi) vs asynchronous fire-and-forget (actors), proven non-equivalent (Palamidessi: sync-pi can express mixed-choice that async-pi cannot encode). A propagator is NEITHER: it is 'information-flow-on-network' — it fires when input cells refine (async in that it is not synchronized with the triggering write) yet its output is a monotone refinement observable by others (sync in that sense). The lattice-monotone framing 'replaces both operational shapes with a third: refinement on a lattice.' This is why the substrate can target both the synchronous-clocked BSP world AND the asynchronous-distributed world from one model.
- CONVERGENT-EVIDENCE for the substrate's universality (quantale doc §3.3.9, §8.1): FOUR independent research communities reinvented the SAME primitive (lattice-valued shared state + monotone update + threshold reads) over ~25 years with near-zero cross-citation — AI/dataflow propagators (Sussman-Radul), concurrent-logic CCP (Saraswat cc(D) + closure-operator semantics), deterministic-parallel I-structures/LVars (Arvind, Kuper-Newton), and distributed-DB Bloom/BloomL (Hellerstein, Conway). 'There is essentially one good answer to how to design a deterministic monotone shared-memory primitive.' The propagator net = cc(D) for D=arbitrary lattice (settled at per-cell store level, §3.3.7); the Prologos extensions (dynamic topology, generalized stratification, per-cell locality) are extensions of cc(D), and the closure-operator/parallel-as-intersection/least-fixpoint semantics carry through verbatim — so the formal results (CALM, BloomL coordination-freedom, soft-cc(Q) cost) transfer.

**PReduce implications:**
- PReduce (e-graph cost extraction) is flagged in on-network.md AND DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org as a HIGH optimization-pressure track that the orthogonality discipline is specifically meant to protect prophylactically (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org §Provenance: 'The reframing applies prophylactically to future PReduce, OE, and SH Series tracks'). IMPLICATION: any cost-extraction optimization in PReduce must be a property of the CELL (cost-storage strategy / tropical-merge) or PROPAGATOR (fire-on-cost-threshold), NEVER a piggyback on BSP's worklist-drain — otherwise it will not survive the Gauss-Seidel/Zig-LLVM/distributed schedulers and breaks non-vN reachability.
- The tropical (min,+) quantale cost layer (PPN 4C addendum) is the soft-cc(Q) instance for Q = the Lawvere quantale (quantale doc §3.3.10 I3, §7.2.5). PReduce e-graph extraction is a cost-minimization over a tropical semiring; per the Hyperlattice Conjecture worked example (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org:446-450) and the quantale-substrate hypothesis, the greedy/dynamic-programming extraction 'algorithm' should DISSOLVE into a tropical lattice fixpoint solvable on-network rather than an off-network imperative pass — keeping extraction itself CALM-monotone and scheduler-portable.
- INFERRED: e-graph rebuild/congruence-closure is a monotone-growth operation (the equivalence relation only coarsens) and so belongs in S0; cost-extraction that involves CHOICE (pick the min-cost representative) is the non-monotone step that, per LRP, should be a control layer reading the S0 cost fixpoint — i.e., extraction is the 'effect handler' analogue (Layer 5) for PReduce. Treating extraction as a between-strata control boundary keeps the monotone substrate clean and preserves order-independence.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- The experimental Zig+LLVM scheduler is named in DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org (lines 758-759, 778, 875, 981, 1104) as a CURRENT instance of the portability seam: 'An experimental Zig+LLVM scheduler is exploring early lowering with a scheduler written outside the BEAM.' It runs the SAME network with the SAME semantics — it is a SCHEDULER substitution, not a network redesign. This is the concrete proof-of-concept that the cell+propagator layer survives a cross-language lowering.
- For pnet->LLVM lowering to preserve non-vN reachability, the lowering must respect the orthogonality invariants: it may freely choose firing order / round structure / LLVM-lowered firing (efficiency, scheduler's concern) but MUST NOT alter which propagators fire on which cell states (semantics, cell/propagator's concern). The .pnet serialized network (lattice + merge + dependencies + fire-pattern + component-paths) is the declaration-driven spec that the LLVM scheduler consumes; if the LLVM backend needs to re-implement cell/propagator optimizations rather than inherit them, the optimization was mislocated.
- Scheduler/variant assignment table (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org:975-981) explicitly slots 'Future Zig-LLVM scheduler' as 'Per-loop-batch (likely) -> Variant A or hybrid' for scheduler-state-cell deferred-write — i.e., the LLVM scheduler is expected to CHOOSE its own write-batching variant for its own bookkeeping cells (fuel-cost, worklist, round-counter) without changing propagator-state-cell semantics. This is the template for how an LLVM backend may optimize its internal scratch while staying CALM-order-independent.
- INFERRED: LLVM lowering is the bridge toward true non-vN targets (GPU/SIMT, PIM) — the parallel-algorithms survey notes GPU kernels are analyzed as work-depth WITHIN an SM and BSP-style ACROSS SMs (Theme/§2c), and CA/stencil maps cleanly to CUDA. A pnet whose dataflow depth = Hasse-diagram depth gives the LLVM/GPU scheduler the optimal parallel decomposition for free, provided monotonicity + order-independence are preserved through lowering.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- INFERRED (term 'phase collapse' not found in any read doc — flagged in open_questions). The nearest in-corpus analogues that a 'phase collapse' notion would interact with: (a) TWO-PHASE ZONKING (intermediate preserves unsolved metas vs final defaults to lzero/mw, per CLAUDE.md) and (b) PHASE separation across STRATA (S0 monotone / S(-1) retraction / S1 NAF / topology), where each stratum is itself a monotone fixpoint phase and the BSP outer loop sequences them.
- If 'phase collapse' means fusing previously-sequential compiler phases into one network: this is directly enabled by the substrate per stratification.md candidate-future-strata ('Self-hosted compiler passes: each pass (parsing, type inference, code generation) is stratum-separable. Running them as BSP strata on the same base gives incremental-compilation for free via cell persistence'). LRP/orthogonality IMPLICATION: collapsing phases onto one network is sound ONLY if each collapsed phase stays internally CALM-monotone and the non-monotone hand-offs between phases are realized as LRP control boundaries / stratum handlers — collapsing two phases that share a non-monotone dependency into a single S0 would violate monotonicity.
- If 'phase collapse' means a degeneracy where distinct strata or worldview-phases merge unintentionally: the orthogonality + monotonicity invariants are the guard — order-independence ensures a phase boundary cannot be silently crossed by reordering, and the strata-termination guarantees (stratification.md / GÖDEL_COMPLETENESS.org) bound each phase's fixpoint.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- THE CENTRAL THESIS (synthesized across all sources): non-von-Neumann targets stay REACHABLE because the computation is specified as a fixed-topology, monotone lattice FIXPOINT (cells + closure-operator propagators) rather than a von-Neumann instruction stream over shared mutable memory. CALM guarantees the fixpoint is order-independent => there is NO imposed sequencing for hardware to serialize on; ordering emerges from dataflow depth (the Hasse diagram), which the Hyperlattice Conjecture asserts IS the optimal parallel decomposition. The von-Neumann fetch-execute-cycle is INVERTED (dataflow firing on operand-availability, no program counter), so the network is hardware-agnostic at the WHAT level.
- THE SCHEDULER IS THE SINGLE PORTABILITY SEAM. Because Cell/Propagator/Scheduler are orthogonal and CALM makes evaluation-order the scheduler's exclusive concern, retargeting to a new hardware/runtime is a SCHEDULER SUBSTITUTION, not a network redesign. Demonstrated: 3 schedulers (Gauss-Seidel, parallel BSP, sequential BFS-BSP) already run the same network identically; Zig+LLVM in progress; distributed runtime planned. Each candidate non-vN target (GPU/SIMT, PIM/processing-in-memory, FPGA stencil, multi-node distributed) is a new scheduler that inherits the unchanged cell+propagator network and its declared optimizations (fire-once, broadcast, set-latch, monotone-counter cells).
- THE BOTTLENECK THE DESIGN ANSWERS is the memory wall: data-movement >> compute energy (~100x, parallel-algorithms-survey Theme 4 / §6). Two literature responses — communication-avoiding algorithms (avoid movement) and PIM (move compute to data) — are BOTH scheduler/runtime-layer concerns under the orthogonality split (work distribution, where cells live, where firing happens). The network DECLARES dataflow locality (component-paths, compound cells = cohesive scopes); a non-vN scheduler can exploit that locality to minimize movement WITHOUT the network knowing which hardware it runs on. (Compound-cell-over-N-separate-cells and broadcast-over-N-propagators, per propagator-design.md, are exactly movement/CHAMP-op reductions declared at the cell/propagator layer.)
- THE THREE PRESERVATION INVARIANTS (must hold or non-vN reach is lost): (i) MONOTONICITY of every merge + every fire-function (closure-operator: extensive/idempotent/monotone) — gives fixpoint existence + CALM coordination-freedom; non-monotone work isolated to LRP control boundaries / strata. (ii) ORDER-INDEPENDENCE — no cell value or propagator correctness may depend on scheduler mode; 'correct under Gauss-Seidel but wrong under BSP = layering violation.' (iii) DECLARATION-DRIVEN behavior — cell+propagator declarations are a COMPLETE spec; scheduler only needs 'fire when input cells change.' DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org:817-821 makes the dependency explicit: 'the universality argument relies on scheduler-independence. If networks are scheduler-coupled, they're... a specific scheduler's specific computational model. The Hyperlattice Conjecture rests on this orthogonality.'
- DISTRIBUTED / ASYNC is the highest-value non-vN target and is ALREADY architecturally located off the network: DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org §837 puts multi-process/multi-node/async-I/O/cross-network message-passing at the scheduler+networking layer ('future runtimes inherit the current networks unchanged'). The quantale doc §8.4/§9.1 gives the concrete shape: an L2 runtime = a FEDERATION of vat-like single-stratum BSP propagator networks communicating via CapTP-style messages bearing N⊗T-valued (round-number ⊗ tropical-cost) quantale-causal clocks, with cross-network TOPOLOGY REQUESTS as the protocol primitive and the capability hierarchy as the security model. BloomL (§5.5) is the proof that a non-trivial lattice-based distributed-programming model already exists at industrial quality with CALM-guaranteed coordination-freedom for its monotone fragment — 'a propagator network with monotone merge is a BloomL program viewed through a different surface syntax.'
- LRP IS WHAT MAKES non-vN reach SOUND FOR EFFECTFUL/NON-MONOTONE work, not just pure computation. Without LRP, IO/negation/choice would force coordination smeared across the substrate, re-coupling to a specific execution model. With LRP, ALL non-monotone work is pushed to identifiable control boundaries (effect-handler Layer 5; stratum handlers; ATMS choice-commitment) that are CALM-minimal — so the monotone, scheduler-portable core stays maximal. The session-types-as-causal-clocks insight (LRP §) is the same vector-clock structure distributed systems use, derived from the TYPE SYSTEM — and the quantale doc generalizes it to Q-valued causal clocks (§7.5.3), the algebraic bridge from the single-machine BSP substrate to the distributed runtime.
- BSP IS DELIBERATELY a 'BRIDGING MODEL' for parallel hardware the way von Neumann is for sequential (Valiant 1990, parallel-algorithms-survey §1). Prologos's choice of BSP as the production scheduler is therefore not an accident of the Racket prototype — it is the parallel-portability analogue of the vN model, abstract enough that the SAME network targets MPP/cluster/shared-memory/GPU/AI-accelerator (the survey catalogues Multi-BSP, streaming-BSP for manycore/GPU, BSP-on-RISC-V/AI-accelerators). The hypercube = Hasse diagram of the Boolean worldview lattice Q_n (survey §4d, Theme 3) means the ATMS worldview exploration's optimal communication topology IS a structural property of the lattice — directly the Hyperlattice optimality claim, and the reason collective-ops (all-reduce/broadcast/scan) hit the log-p information-theoretic floor on the substrate's native structure.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- LRP is NOT novel as a coordination-placement idea — it is the CONSTRUCTIVE/architectural reading of the CALM theorem (Hellerstein 2010; proven by Ameloot-Neven-Van den Bussche 2011/2013 via relational transducers). Prior art the LRP doc itself cites: CALM (Hellerstein), propagators (Radul-Sussman 2009, who EXPLICITLY excluded non-commutative effects — LRP is the answer to that exclusion), session-types-as-linear-logic (Caires-Pfenning, Wadler), Galois connections (Cousot-Cousot abstract interpretation), vector clocks (Lamport/Fidge/Mattern), partial-order planning (Weld), effect quantales (Katsumata, Gordon). NOVELTY of the Prologos contribution = the SPECIFIC realization: one 3-layer architecture (propagator+ATMS+control) shared by both the logic engine (rel) AND the session runtime (proc), with session-types reinterpreted as causal clocks via a quantale-morphism Galois connection.
- Cell/Propagator/Scheduler ORTHOGONALITY as an explicit named principle (with the portability argument) appears to be a genuine Prologos articulation (provenance: user during PPN 4C tropical-addendum critique, 2026-05-14), but it is the application of CALM order-independence + Decomplection (Hickey) to the computation/runtime boundary — not a new theorem. The 'scheduler-state cell' three-category taxonomy (propagator-state / scheduler-state / topology-state) is a Prologos-internal refinement.
- STRONG prior art the quantale doc surfaces that the project under-leverages: propagator networks = Saraswat's cc(D) (1989-1991) with closure-operator semantics carrying through VERBATIM; the four-community convergence (cc(D), I-structures/LVars, Bloom/BloomL, propagators) means the 'universal substrate' thesis is empirically corroborated by independent rediscovery. The tropical-cost layer = soft cc(Q) (Bistarelli-Montanari-Rossi 1997 c-semirings = commutative quantales; soft CCP 2006). So the project's 'cost-on-cells' is an instance of a 30-year research program, NOT a fresh invention (quantale doc §8.1, §8.7 gap-e is the genuine crossover novelty: min-plus network-calculus on a PL type substrate).
- GENUINE FRONTIER GAPS (quantale doc §6.6/§8.7, candidate Prologos research outputs, NOT yet in literature): (b) propagator network as a quantale-enriched category; (c) explicit 'cc(D) is the operational form of computation in a quantale-enriched category over a propagator substrate'; (d) UNIFIED treatment of cost+order+fairness+resource as projections of ONE quantale substrate (H4, the genuine frontier hypothesis); (f) quantale-causal clocks (vs Lamport/vector); (h) function-coloring dissolved at the SUBSTRATE level; (i) stratification on quantale-modules; (j) propagator-network-as-fibred-hyperdoctrine. The vat-model L2 design (§8.4) is candidate-architecture, NOT settled — vats are single-threaded run-to-completion, BSP rounds permit intra-round parallelism, a non-trivial residual difference flagged honestly.
- Timely/differential dataflow (Naiad, McSherry et al. 2013; Materialize) is called 'the closest practical predecessor to a propagator network' (quantale doc §4.6): cell≈operator-state, propagator≈operator, lattice-merge≈differential-update, BSP-round≈frontier-advancement. What it LACKS vs the Prologos design = the quantale-enriched cost model (timestamps order progress but carry no quantitative weight). This is the nearest external prior art for a distributed lattice/dataflow runtime.

**Open questions:**
- The term 'phase collapse' (in phase_collapse_implications) does NOT appear in any of the four read documents (verified by reading; not exhaustively grepped across the whole repo). I inferred its likely meanings (phase-fusion of compiler passes onto one network; or unintended stratum/worldview degeneracy) but cannot confirm the intended referent — flag for the requester to define, or point at the doc where it is introduced.
- Stratification on quantale-modules is EXPLICITLY OPEN (quantale doc §7.5.4, gap-i): how do S0/S1/S(-1)/topology strata carry to the quantale-enriched substrate? Two speculative candidates (stratum = quantale-module retract via closure operators; stratum = iterated quantaloid layering per Stubbe). Unresolved — bears directly on whether the cost/causal-clock quantale story composes with the existing LRP stratification.
- The L2 multi-network runtime (vat ≈ single-stratum BSP net) composition questions are open (quantale doc §9.1): does the strict vat single-threaded-turn boundary compose with intra-round BSP parallelism? Can CapTP carry N⊗T-valued causal clocks without breaking the OCapN interop suite, and at what wire-cost? Does CapTP per-table GC transfer to dynamic-topology propagator-cell references? These gate whether the distributed (most important non-vN) target is actually reachable in practice vs in principle.
- Whether the von-Neumann-bottleneck OVERCOMING is empirically realized (not just architecturally enabled) is unestablished in these docs: no benchmark in the read corpus measures a non-BSP/non-Racket scheduler's data-movement or speedup. The Zig+LLVM scheduler is 'experimental'/'exploring'; GPU/PIM targets are aspirational. The orthogonality principle makes the targets reachable-in-principle; actual lowering + measurement is future SH/OE-series work. INFERRED gap, flagged.
- Stratum-3 (session/effect verification) referenced in LRP/effect-executor.rkt and the stratification rule is 'referenced but not fully realized.' The effect-handler Layer 5 (the actual IO control boundary) is the placeholder 'current-effect-executor' stub (quantale doc §8.4: 'sequential default and a placeholder concurrent stub'). So LRP's non-monotone boundary for effects is DESIGNED but the concurrent/distributed executor — the piece that would exercise non-vN async — is not yet built.
- The Hyperlattice Conjecture's OPTIMALITY claim (Hasse diagram = optimal parallel decomposition) is a DESIGN MANDATE, not a theorem (DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org:441 explicitly: 'It is not a theorem — it is a design mandate'). The strongest evidence is the hypercube=Q_n-Hasse identity for the ATMS worldview lattice. Whether arbitrary computations' lattices yield optimal decompositions is unproven — this caveat matters for any claim that the substrate is OPTIMALLY (not merely portably) non-vN-targetable.


## IG6 — Polyglot Hub + LLVM-as-integration-onramp + FFI (why LLVM is the first/main target)

**Key findings:**
- POLYGLOT HUB = THE FFI THESIS (VERIFIED, FFI_MASTER:5,23-32). Prologos's intended position is 'a hub language whose lattice + propagator + dependent-type substrate provides a shared semantic foundation under multiple language frontends and execution backends.' The FFI surface is 'where languages, runtimes, and substrates meet — Racket today, more languages and platforms over time.' This is NOT vague aspiration: it is the load-bearing organizing thesis of the entire FFI Series tracker.
- FOUR HUB INTEGRATION REQUIREMENTS (VERIFIED, FFI_MASTER:27-31). Each foreign language must: (1) MARSHAL values through structurally-principled lattice-aware bridges (not ad-hoc per-type glue); (2) EXPRESS functions as CELL HANDLES not procedure boundaries (long-term; procedure-shaped scaffolding accepted near-term); (3) TRACK effects through capability-typed surfaces consistent with session-types direction; (4) COMPOSE with the propagator network's worldview/retraction/stratification semantics. Requirement (2) — functions-as-cells — is the architecturally distinctive demand: it is what makes the hub on-network rather than a conventional FFI shim, and is the explicit retirement target of FFI Track 3 / issue #37.
- WHY LLVM IS FIRST/MAIN TARGET = IT IS THE LINGUA FRANCA THAT EVERY OTHER LANGUAGE/PLATFORM ALSO TARGETS (VERIFIED, SH-path §10:194-204). 'LLVM IR is the lingua franca; LLVM compiles to native or WASM.' The polyglot-hub payoff of being ON LLVM is structural: a Prologos substrate compiled to LLVM emits objects that share the SAME native ABI / object-file format / linker as C, C++, Rust, Swift, and any other LLVM-frontend language — so cross-language linking, shared calling conventions, and the entire LLVM ecosystem (optimization passes, sanitizers, target backends) come for free. LLVM is the integration ON-RAMP: it is the substrate at which Prologos meets every other native language WITHOUT bespoke per-language bridges. INFERRED (the docs assert LLVM-as-lingua-franca + native-ABI sharing but do not spell out the 'shared object format/linker = free cross-language linking' mechanism in one place; this is the natural reading assembled from SH §10 + IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10.1's C-ABI emission).
- FFI DIRECTION INVERSION IS THE PIVOT (VERIFIED, SH-path §9:180-190). Today's FFI is 'Prologos calls OUT through Racket's FFI infrastructure to other runtimes' (kumavis PR #35 lambda-passing, eigentrust marshaling, Posit/List). Stage A.5+ INVERTS: 'Prologos's substrate is native; it calls OS / native libs DIRECTLY.' This is the moment the polyglot hub stops being mediated by Racket. Three concrete implications named: (a) the foreign.rkt marshaler 'should not assume a Racket runtime' (it cleanly does today) — Track 7 refactor; (b) capability-gating of FFI calls is the INVARIANT THAT SURVIVES INVERSION ('the OCap-style discipline is independent of which side is native — both sides have capabilities, the gates check at the call boundary'); (c) the on-network-callback retirement (#37) 'lands more naturally post-A.5 because propagator-native is genuinely native.'
- TWO INFRASTRUCTURE PIECES UNLOCK THE TWO SELF-HOSTING AXES (VERIFIED, SH-path §15 + SH_MASTER:18-20). The executive summary states it crisply: 'the FFI Series and the LLVM backend are the two infrastructure pieces that respectively unlock Stage B (compiler-in-Prologos with substrate hosted) and Stage A.5 (substrate native, runtime Racket-free).' So FFI and LLVM are not parallel nice-to-haves — they are the two load-bearing legs: LLVM retires runtime-Racket (axis 1), FFI (inverted) gives the native substrate its OS/native-lib reach. The polyglot-hub vision and the self-hosting path are the SAME project viewed from two angles.
- LLVM EMISSION REQUIREMENTS ARE FULLY ENUMERATED FOR THE NATIVE PATH (VERIFIED, IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10): elaborate→optimize→ANF→closure-convert→emit-LLVM-IR→link-runtime; ADTs→tagged unions (tag byte + payload union); closures→function-pointer+environment-pointer pairs; tail-call attribute on tail-position calls (critical for functional recursion AND logic backtracking); GC via Boehm (proto) then gc.statepoint (precise/production); bindings via inkwell(Rust). These are the conventional-functional-language LLVM requirements. INFERRED GAP: NONE of these docs specify the ABI/calling-convention/data-layout requirements that the POLYGLOT-HUB specifically demands (C-compatible struct layout for marshaled data, extern-C calling convention for outbound/inbound FFI, target-datalayout matching) beyond the generic 'ADTs→tagged unions / closures→fn-ptr+env' — the hub-specific ABI surface is undocumented; see pnet_llvm_implications + open_questions.
- 'BABEL-SRC-BLOCK / ORG-MODE LITERATE NOTEBOOK' FRAMING IS NOT IN THE DOCS (HONEST NOVELTY FLAG). The facet prompt asks for 'org-mode-babel-style seamless multi-language' inspiration. This phrasing appears NOWHERE in the three target docs (grep-confirmed across all three + the principles dir). The DOCUMENTED vision is narrower and more precise: 'polyglot HUB' (a shared semantic substrate under many frontends/backends — FFI_MASTER), 'interop should feel seamless, not foreign' (LANGUAGE_VISION §FFI), and 'Embeddable Logic Engine callable from Python/JS/Rust, Lua/SQLite-style' (LANGUAGE_VISION §Long-Term). The org-babel literate-programming / single-document-many-languages notebook model is the FACET AUTHOR'S gloss, not project-grounded text. Treating it as the vision would over-claim; the real vision is substrate-level interop + embeddability, not a literate-programming code-block executor.
- WASM IS A SECOND LLVM DESTINATION, NOT A SEPARATE TARGET (VERIFIED, SH-path §10 / SH_MASTER Track 8). 'Decoupling target IR (LLVM) from destination (native binary, WASM module, embedded firmware) early avoids a costly retrofit.' WASM matters for the polyglot hub specifically because (a) the multi-agent/Vat vision wants browser-side execution; (b) the WASM COMPONENT MODEL 'aligns with our capability-typed boundary discipline — each component has explicit imports/exports; capability-gating maps onto WASM's component-level type system'; (c) IPVM/Fission deterministic-Wasm + content-addressed computation aligns with .pnet content-addressing. So the same capability-typed FFI boundary maps onto BOTH the native C-ABI AND the WASM component model — one discipline, two ABIs.
- THE 'RACKET IS SLOW' → LLVM REASONING IS DELIBERATELY DISAMBIGUATED (VERIFIED, SH-path §12). LLVM port does NOT fix algorithmic slowness (reduction is 64% of compile time, 3739 lines of imperative tree-walking — that is fixed by PReduce/Track 9 IN RACKET). What LLVM DOES fix: 'distribution (no Racket install required), self-hosting closure (bootstrap chain ends), perf ceiling once algorithms are good (constant factor matters when you've extracted the algorithmic wins).' Critical ordering for the hub: PReduce first (algorithmic, in Racket), THEN LLVM (constant-factor on an already-fast algorithm). This is the honest framing the FFI/polyglot story must not over-promise against — LLVM-as-onramp is about REACH and DISTRIBUTION (the polyglot/embeddable payoff), not raw speed.

**PReduce implications:**
- PReduce is the GATE before any LLVM port, per the explicit ordering (SH-path §5,§12 + executive summary §15): 'Land PReduce / Track 9 first … Then SH series. With PReduce delivered, self-hosted Prologos has a competitive performance story before any porting begins.' For the polyglot-hub/LLVM-onramp facet this means: the LLVM native substrate (which is what gives the hub its cross-language ABI reach) lowers an ALREADY-FAST algorithm. Porting before PReduce = 'porting a slow algorithm and getting a slightly-less-slow algorithm.' The polyglot reach should not be rushed ahead of the algorithmic win.
- INFERRED: PReduce's super-optimization / e-graph cost-extraction at the propagator-network layer directly shapes how THIN the Low-PNet→LLVM lowering can be (SH_MASTER OQ §94 says exactly this: 'if super-optimization happens at the propagator-network layer, the lower IR can be thinner'). For the hub, a thinner lowering IR means the LLVM-emission ABI surface (what must be C-compatible, what calling conventions must be honored) is correspondingly simpler to get right — fewer IR layers between the optimized network and the native object file that links against foreign code.
- FFI cost is itself a PReduce-adjacent concern: FFI_MASTER:54 'Cost-aware FFI: tropical-quantale fuel for foreign call cost (cross-references OE)' and :20 cross-series note. A foreign/native call across the LLVM ABI boundary has a cost that the same tropical/quantale fuel machinery PReduce uses can model — so the optimization substrate that makes reduction fast also lets the hub reason about when a foreign call is worth making. This is a latent unification (native reduction cost + foreign call cost on the same lattice), not yet designed.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- FUNCTIONS-AS-CELLS IS THE HUB'S DEEPEST LLVM-EMISSION DEMAND (VERIFIED req, FFI_MASTER:28 + SH §4). The polyglot-hub requirement (2) — 'express functions as CELL HANDLES, not procedure boundaries' — means the .pnet→LLVM lowering must be able to emit a foreign-callable entry point FROM a cell handle (and register an inbound foreign value AS a cell). This is strictly harder than IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10's generic 'closures→function-pointer+environment-pointer pairs': a cell handle is a network-resident identity, not a stack closure. LLVM emission for the hub must bridge cell-identity ↔ C-function-pointer at the ABI boundary. INFERRED: this is the on-network successor (Track 3/#37) of today's procedure-shaped scaffolding, and its LLVM realization is undesigned.
- C-ABI / EXTERN-C CALLING CONVENTION IS REQUIRED BUT UNDOCUMENTED AT THE ABI LEVEL. IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10.1 specifies inkwell/llvm-hs binding + ADTs→tagged-unions + closures→fn-ptr+env, but does NOT specify: (a) which LLVM calling convention foreign-facing functions use (presumably ccc/C, but unstated); (b) the C-compatible memory layout for marshaled compound data (List, PVec, Map, Sigma — Track-1-future + FFI_MASTER:50) so a Rust/C caller can read Prologos data without a Racket runtime; (c) target-datalayout / target-triple selection. These are the load-bearing ABI requirements the polyglot hub needs and they are GAPS — see open_questions.
- DATA LAYOUT MUST BE C-COMPATIBLE FOR LATTICE-AWARE MARSHALING (INFERRED from FFI_MASTER:27,72 + §9). The hub invariant 'foreign types map onto the lattice infrastructure … not opaque blobs' (FFI_MASTER:72) means marshaled values participate in lattice merges. Post-inversion (substrate native), the LLVM data layout of a marshaled value must be readable/writable by BOTH the native substrate AND the foreign native lib it bridges to — i.e., the tagged-union ADT representation (IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10.1) must have a documented, C-stable layout. Today foreign.rkt marshals to/from Racket S-expr values (Nat numerals, literals); the native-side equivalent layout is undesigned (Track 7).
- .pnet ROUND-TRIPPING PROPAGATOR STRUCTURE IS THE STRUCTURAL LINCHPIN FOR THE NATIVE LLVM SUBSTRATE (VERIFIED, SH-path §4:85-95). Today .pnet captures elaboration state (cells + 10 registries) but 'the propagator and elaboration networks themselves are replaced with sentinels and rebuilt fresh on load.' For the LLVM substrate to LOAD AND RUN a compiled Prologos program (the input the native runtime executes), .pnet must serialize propagator TOPOLOGY + merge functions. 'Merge functions are Racket closures today; they need to become SYMBOLIC REFERENCES to merges registered in the SRE form registry, dispatched at load time' — this connects to NTT-style propagators-as-data. Until this lands, the LLVM substrate has no well-defined input format. This is upstream of the entire hub-via-native-LLVM story.
- TAIL-CALL + GC EMISSION REQUIREMENTS (VERIFIED, IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10.1-10.3). LLVM emission must annotate tail-position calls with the `tail` attribute (critical for BOTH functional recursion AND logic-programming backtracking — relevant because the hub exposes the logic engine as embeddable). GC: Boehm conservative (libgc drop-in, prototype) then gc.statepoint precise (production, safepoint metadata at calls). For an EMBEDDABLE engine (LANGUAGE_VISION §Long-Term: callable from Python/JS/Rust), GC strategy interacts with the host's GC — a conservative collector embedded in a host process is a known footgun; this is unaddressed.
- LOW-PNET IR IS THE LOWERING LAYER BETWEEN NETWORK AND LLVM (VERIFIED, SH_MASTER Track 2): 'Cells as typed memory regions; propagators as functions over those regions; scheduler as worklist data structure; lattice merges inlined or dispatched. Analog: GHC STG/Cmm, MLIR dialect tower.' The number of IR layers (direct vs STG/Cmm-two-layer vs MLIR-n-layer) is an OPEN design question (SH_MASTER OQ §94) gated on PReduce outputs. For the hub, this IR is where the C-ABI boundary and capability-gated FFI calls get lowered.
- WASM COMPONENT-MODEL IS A SECOND LLVM DESTINATION SHARING THE SAME CAPABILITY-TYPED BOUNDARY (VERIFIED, SH-path §10). LLVM-emission for the hub should target BOTH native (C ABI) and WASM (Component Model imports/exports). 'Capability-gating maps onto WASM's component-level type system.' INFERRED: this argues for designing the capability-typed FFI boundary ABI-AGNOSTICALLY (one discipline, lowered to C-ABI OR WASM-component-interface) rather than hardcoding the native C ABI — a design constraint on Track 2 (Low-PNet IR) that is implied but not stated.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- The polyglot-hub-via-LLVM vision IS a non-von-Neumann-reach concern in the sense that the END STATE keeps the propagator-network execution model: the native LLVM substrate reimplements 'cells + propagators + BSP + CHAMP + ATMS' (SH-path §3 Stage A.5; SH_MASTER Track 4) as native code — NOT a conventional sequential VM. So 'on LLVM' does not mean 'collapsed to a von-Neumann instruction stream'; it means the dataflow/lattice execution model is COMPILED TO native code while preserving parallel BSP rounds. SH-path §7.2 confirms the native substrate needs 'a thread pool, work-stealing for BSP rounds, lock-free CHAMP operations' — the non-VN parallel-dataflow character is carried INTO the native target, not flattened out of it.
- INFERRED: this is the load-bearing distinction for the hub's value proposition — Prologos-on-LLVM offers other languages access to a NON-von-Neumann reasoning substrate (lattice fixpoint / propagator / proof-search) through a conventional ABI. The embeddable-logic-engine vision (LANGUAGE_VISION §Long-Term: 'Ship the propagator-based logic engine as an embeddable library … callable from Python, JavaScript, Rust') is precisely 'non-VN reach packaged behind a VN-conventional FFI boundary.' The LLVM/C ABI is the VN-shaped onramp; what is reached THROUGH it is the non-VN propagator engine. This framing is assembled from the docs, not stated as a 'non-VN' claim in them.
- Per-propagator-worldview bitmask filtering (SH-path §7.2) is what lets parallel BSP rounds run without cell-write conflicts in the native substrate — 'already designed at the algorithmic level; engineering at native level.' This is the mechanism by which the non-VN parallelism survives the lowering to native threads.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- NOVELTY (genuine, for the field): the hub's requirement (2) 'express functions as CELL HANDLES not procedure boundaries' (FFI_MASTER:28) is a distinctive claim — most FFI systems (GHC FFI, Rust extern, JNI, Idris2 backends) are procedure-boundary-based. Making the FFI boundary CELL-SHAPED (network-resident, monotone-merge, worldview-aware, retractable) appears to be Prologos-specific. Whether it is achievable/worthwhile is an open design question (FFI Track 3 lists 'several open design questions: cell identity, multi-arg dispatch, synchronization, worldview, retraction').
- PRIOR ART (acknowledged, not novel — the docs are honest about this): LLVM-as-lingua-franca + ADTs→tagged-unions + closures→fn-ptr+env + ANF + Boehm/statepoint GC (IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10) is the STANDARD functional-language-to-LLVM recipe (cited: GHC LLVM backend, Idris 2 backends). The two-axis self-hosting retirement is explicitly compared to GHC (C runtime since 1990s; Haskell-in-Haskell came later), Rust (OCaml bootstrap), Idris 2 (QTT erasure + backend variety), Lean 4 ('closest peer in design space', SH-path §14). NONE of the native-emission mechanics are claimed as novel.
- PRIOR ART: capability-typed boundary ↔ WASM Component Model mapping (SH-path §10) cites IPVM / Fission Codes (deterministic Wasm, content-addressed computation) as precedent. The WASM Component Model itself is external prior art; the novel angle is mapping Prologos's capability-typed FFI surface onto component-level imports/exports.
- HONEST SCOPE CORRECTION (novelty-deflation): the 'org-mode-babel literate multi-language notebook' framing (facet prompt) is NOT a documented Prologos vision and should not be attributed as one. The actual documented ambition (polyglot HUB = shared substrate + embeddable engine) is more conventional than the babel framing implies and overlaps with the established 'embeddable engine' tradition (Lua, SQLite, explicitly cited LANGUAGE_VISION §Long-Term). Claiming a babel-notebook capability would be unsupported over-reach.
- PRIOR ART (internal): the cross-network Galois-bridge infra (net-add-cross-domain-propagator, ≥4 live sites: cap-type-bridge.rkt:218, effect-bridge.rkt, driver.rkt:2728) already realizes 'different subsystems on different lattice-domain networks communicate via Galois connections' (LANGUAGE_VISION §Cross-network interfacing). This is EXISTING infrastructure that the multi-language/multi-substrate hub composition could reuse — the 'many networks, bridged' pattern is not new to the hub. (Cross-ref MEMORY.md note: prior-art search before claiming 'new pattern' — this is exactly the prevalent prior art that speculation-only framing would miss.)

**Open questions:**
- ABI SPECIFICATION GAP: None of the three target docs (nor IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10) specify the C-ABI calling convention, C-stable data layout for marshaled COMPOUND types (List/PVec/Map/Sigma), or target-datalayout/triple selection that the polyglot hub's outbound+inbound native FFI requires. The generic 'ADTs→tagged unions, closures→fn-ptr+env' is necessary but not sufficient for a hub where foreign native code must read/write Prologos data. Where (if anywhere) is the hub-specific ABI documented? (Likely nowhere yet — flag for a future FFI/SH track.)
- FUNCTIONS-AS-CELLS LLVM REALIZATION: how does cell-identity lower to a C-callable function pointer (outbound) and how does an inbound foreign function get registered AS a cell (inbound), at the LLVM/native level? FFI Track 3/#37 lists the design questions (cell identity, multi-arg dispatch, synchronization, worldview, retraction) but no LLVM-lowering answer exists. This is the deepest undesigned piece of the hub-on-native story.
- BABEL/LITERATE FRAMING PROVENANCE: the facet prompt's 'org-mode-babel-style seamless multi-language' phrasing is not in any target doc. Is there a SEPARATE document (research note, standup, a doc outside the three) that introduces a literate/notebook/babel polyglot vision? Grep across LANGUAGE_VISION + FFI_MASTER + SH-path + principles dir found nothing. If it exists elsewhere it was not in scope; if it does not exist, the facet's framing is an over-statement of the documented 'polyglot hub' + 'embeddable engine' vision.
- EMBEDDABLE-ENGINE GC INTEROP: LANGUAGE_VISION pitches an embeddable logic engine callable from Python/JS/Rust, but the GC story (Boehm conservative / statepoint precise, IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDANCE §10.3) is framed for a standalone native binary, not for embedding inside a host process with its OWN GC. How does the conservative/precise collector coexist with a host runtime's collector when embedded? Unaddressed.
- INVERSION TIMING vs FFI-SERIES DESIGN: SH-path §9 says 'FFI Series' design choices today (stage A) want to anticipate the inversion that lands at stage A.5' — but the FFI_MASTER tracker (Tracks 0-3) is all stage-A 'call-out-through-Racket' work. Is there active design pressure to make foreign.rkt's marshaler runtime-agnostic NOW (forward-compat), or is that deferred entirely to SH Track 7? The two docs gesture at coordination but no concrete forward-compat requirement is recorded against the current FFI tracks.
- CAPABILITY-WITNESS ERASURE ↔ FFI ABI: SH-path §8 (Track 5 erasure) says capability witnesses are 'ambiguous — effect capabilities can erase; authority capabilities for OCap/multi-agent must persist' and this 'has implications for native-call ABI, FFI compatibility.' If some capabilities persist at runtime as handles passed across the FFI boundary, what is their ABI representation? This couples Track 5 (erasure) to the hub's ABI and is explicitly flagged as undecided.


## FR1 — Equality saturation all the way to codegen / instruction selection: where the production frontier actually is, and what it means for designing PReduce to feed an LLVM lowering.

**Mode:** frontier-web  |  **SHA:** n/a (web)

**Sources:**
- (external-url) https://cfallin.org/blog/2026/04/09/aegraph/ — Chris Fallin (Cranelift author), 'The acyclic e-graph: Cranelift's mid-end optimizer' (Apr 2026). THE key production data point: a shipping compiler whose mid-end IS an e-graph, with the SAME ISLE rule DSL used for both mid-end rewrites and instruction lowering. Extraordinarily concrete on costs, benefits, and what does/doesn't pay off.
- (external-url) https://github.com/bytecodealliance/rfcs/blob/main/accepted/cranelift-egraph.md — Accepted Bytecode Alliance RFC for the Cranelift e-graph mid-end. Primary design doc behind the aegraph; covers verifiability-via-rewrite-rules motivation and the ISLE-for-both-mid-end-and-lowering thesis.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2508.13020v1 — e-boost: Boosted E-Graph Extraction with Adaptive Heuristics and Exact Solving (Aug 2025). State-of-the-art extraction: heuristic prune + WARM-START ILP. Key numbers: extraction = 89.3% of total eqsat runtime; 558x speedup vs vanilla ILP at +5.6% quality; DAG-cost NP-completeness.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2505.09363v2 — eqsat: An Equality Saturation Dialect for Non-destructive Rewriting (2025, xDSL/MLIR). Represents the e-graph AS an MLIR dialect (eqsat.eclass/egraph/yield) so eqsat state persists across passes — the closest existing analog to Prologos's 'substrate IS the IR' position. Uses MLIR graph-regions to permit cycles.
- (external-url) https://www.cs.cornell.edu/courses/cs6120/2025sp/blog/superopt/ — Cornell CS6120 (2025) survey blog on technology mapping with e-graphs. Frames instruction selection/tech-mapping AS e-graph extraction; rewrite rules encode lowering+optimization simultaneously; documents acyclicity as the main ILP-extraction bottleneck and solver-robustness failures (cbc 'infeasible').
- (external-url) https://effect.systems/blog/egraph-extraction.html — 'The E-graph extraction problem is NP-complete' — reduction from minimum set cover; e-graph extraction with DAG/sharing-aware cost is hard to approximate within any constant ratio. Grounds the §2.5/§6.3 NP-hardness claim in our E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH doc.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2408.17042 — 'E-Graphs as Circuits, and Optimal Extraction via Treewidth' (2024). Recasts extraction as a circuit/Boolean problem; parameterized algorithm optimal for low-treewidth e-graphs. A structural-tractability lever PReduce's E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH doc does not yet cite.
- (external-url) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3689801 — 'Fast and Optimal Extraction for Sparse Equality Graphs' (OOPSLA 2024). Optimal extraction made practical when the e-graph is sparse — another tractability regime relevant to PReduce's per-AST-PU bounded e-graphs.
- (external-url) https://courses.cs.washington.edu/courses/cse501/15sp/papers/joshi.pdf — Denali: A Goal-directed Superoptimizer (Joshi/Nelson/Randall, PLDI 2002). The ORIGINAL eqsat-to-codegen system: builds an e-graph of equivalent computations via mathematical+architectural axioms, then extracts optimal machine code via constraint solving (matcher + SAT). Direct ancestor of PReduce's vision.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.04422 — Souper: A Synthesizing Superoptimizer (LLVM IR). SMT-based, not e-graph-based: extracts an LHS from LLVM IR, SYNTHESIZES an optimized RHS. Counterpoint to eqsat — shows the synthesis-vs-saturation axis for feeding LLVM.
- (external-url) https://users.cs.utah.edu/~regehr/minotaur-oopsla24.pdf — Minotaur: A SIMD-Oriented Synthesizing Superoptimizer (OOPSLA 2024). Souper-lineage, targets LLVM vector IR; current SOTA on practical LLVM-IR superoptimization. Benchmark/quality reference for an LLVM-feeding optimizer.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11574 — E-morphic: Scalable Equality Saturation for Structural Exploration in Logic Synthesis (2025). Direct e-graph<->circuit conversion + simulated-annealing extraction; 12.54% area / 7.29% delay vs ABC. Evidence eqsat-to-hardware-codegen is crossing into competitive-with-production-EDA territory.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.09932 — 'Automatically Generating Compiler Backends from Tensor Descriptions' (2025). First phase is parameterized INSTRUCTION SELECTION via equality saturation — eqsat used to GENERATE a backend, the strongest recent statement of eqsat-spanning-to-codegen.
- (external-url) https://pldi25.sigplan.org/details/egraphs-2025-papers/8/Oatlog-A-performant-ahead-of-time-compiled-e-graph-engine — Oatlog (EGRAPHS 2025): AOT-compiled egglog engine as a relational DB with semi-naive evaluation. Marks the 2025 egglog-as-relational-Datalog maturation — directly relevant to our 'eqsat = monotone Datalog = propagator quiescence' framing.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/research/2026-05-02_E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH.md — Our foundational e-graph survey. §4.3 Cranelift acyclic e-graph, §2.5/§6.3 extraction NP-hardness, §4.6 Schlatt persistence, §7.5 tropical-residuation-as-extraction. This facet supplies the 2026 production-frontier data (esp. Fallin's aegraph retrospective) that updates several of its claims.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/tracking/2026-05-02_PREDUCE_MASTER.md — PReduce series master. Track 0.3 (.pnet + LLVM lowering interface), Track 4 (tropical extraction), Track 8 (reduction.rkt parity/retirement). The consumer of this facet's lessons; collaborator running independent LLVM-lowering prototypes is the operational driver.

**Key findings:**
- PRODUCTION FRONTIER, PRECISELY: exactly ONE compiler ships an e-graph as a real codegen-adjacent mid-end — Cranelift's 'aegraph' (acyclic e-graph), as of Fallin's Apr-2026 retrospective. It is NOT full bidirectional equality saturation: it is EAGER, single-pass, append-only rewriting where 'once we create a node, we never update it' — acyclicity by construction. This is the live counterweight to PReduce's saturate-to-fixpoint framing.
- THE DUAL-USE LESSON (most important for PReduce<->LLVM): Cranelift uses the SAME rule DSL (ISLE) for BOTH mid-end rewrites AND instruction lowering. ISLE was originally the instruction-SELECTION language; it was extended with 'multi-extractors/multi-constructors' (take ALL applicable rules, not one) to drive the aegraph. So 'one rule registry, lowering is just more rules' is VALIDATED IN PRODUCTION — this is exactly PReduce's 'unified rule registry, lowering rules as one more property-tagged kind' (E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §7.9). BUT: the aegraph itself STOPS at the mid-end (CLIF->CLIF); lowering runs as a SEPARATE ISLE rule-application phase over the aegraph's output. Same DSL, two phases — not one fixpoint spanning to machine code.
- SURPRISE THAT SHOULD RESHAPE PReduce PRIORITIES: in production Cranelift the average e-class size is 1.13 e-nodes — i.e. ~88% of values have exactly ONE representation and the multi-value e-graph 'may not (yet?) be pulling its weight.' Most of the measured ~2% speedup comes from the sea-of-nodes+GVN+LICM (code-motion/elaboration) machinery, NOT from holding many equivalent forms. Implication: the EXPENSIVE part of an e-graph (saturation + extraction over fat e-classes) bought little; the CHEAP structural-sharing + good code-placement bought most of it.
- EXTRACTION IS NP-COMPLETE for the realistic (DAG / sharing-aware) cost model — counts each shared subexpression once; hard to approximate within ANY constant ratio (reduction from min set cover). This is the central obstacle to spanning eqsat to codegen, and it is exactly the cost model PReduce needs for LLVM (shared subexpressions are real instructions emitted once).
- EXTRACTION DOMINATES RUNTIME when you do pursue optimal: e-boost (Aug 2025) measures extraction = 89.3% of total equality-saturation runtime. Their fix is the current SOTA recipe: parallel greedy heuristic -> adaptive prune (keep only e-nodes with cost <= min*theta) -> WARM-START ILP seeded by the heuristic. Result: 558x faster than vanilla ILP at +5.6% quality. The bottleneck is extraction, NOT saturation.
- CRUCIAL TENSION with PReduce's 'extraction = tropical residuation, we get it for free from PPN 4C Phase 1B': that residuation/greedy-DP computes the TREE (non-shared) cost in poly time. It is NOT the NP-complete DAG/sharing-aware optimum that real codegen wants. The free residual is the polynomial APPROXIMATION; closing to optimal requires ILP/treewidth/sparse-extraction machinery PReduce does not currently plan. E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §6.3 acknowledges this in passing but Track 4's 'we inherit the algebra' framing understates the gap.
- NEW TRACTABILITY LEVERS (2024) absent from our survey: 'E-Graphs as Circuits, Optimal Extraction via Treewidth' (optimal for low-treewidth e-graphs) and 'Fast & Optimal Extraction for Sparse Equality Graphs' (OOPSLA 2024). Both say: when the e-graph is STRUCTURALLY THIN, optimal extraction is practical. PReduce's per-AST-PU bounded e-graphs (E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §7.1, Layer-1) are plausibly low-treewidth/sparse — a real opportunity to get optimal extraction cheaply that the current design doesn't name.
- COUNTERINTUITIVE COST FINDING (Fallin): in Cranelift, extraction is NOT the compile-time bottleneck — BUILDING the e-graph and 'data movement transcribing IR to the e-graph' is. He avoids it by making the aegraph an IMPLICIT representation that lives inside the existing SSA IR (no separate copy). Suboptimal extraction occurred only twice across 4 MILLION value nodes. This is because the ruleset is designed for OPTIMIZATION (monotonically reduce cost), not EXPLORATION — so greedy bottom-up DP is essentially always right. Directly relevant: PReduce plans CHAMP cells as a SEPARATE structure from the AST — the very data-movement cost Fallin engineered away.
- TWO DISTINCT ARCHITECTURAL FRONTIERS, both 2025-2026: (A) e-graph-as-IR-DIALECT — eqsat MLIR dialect (eqsat.eclass/egraph/yield as SSA ops, non-destructive, persists across passes) + Schlatt's persistent-compiler-abstraction (arXiv 2602.16707). This is Prologos's nearest cousin ('substrate IS the IR'). (B) eqsat-as-BACKEND-GENERATOR — tensor-descriptions->backend (2510.09932) does parameterized instruction selection via eqsat; E-morphic/E-Syn/Churchroad take eqsat through to gates/LUTs and beat ABC by ~12% area. Hardware/EDA is FURTHER along the to-codegen path than general-purpose compilers.
- HISTORICAL ANCHOR: Denali (Joshi/Nelson/Randall, PLDI 2002) already did eqsat-to-optimal-machine-code 24 years ago: seed e-graph with desired behavior, grow it with mathematical AND architectural (instruction) axioms, extract optimal code via a matcher + SAT/constraint solver. PReduce is recognizably Denali's program on a propagator substrate — the novelty is the substrate + persistence + cost-algebra, not the eqsat-to-codegen idea itself. Worth citing as direct prior art rather than a generic reference.
- THE SYNTHESIS AXIS (orthogonal to saturation): Souper and Minotaur (OOPSLA 2024, SIMD) feed LLVM by SMT-SYNTHESIZING optimized RHS from extracted LHS — not by rewriting within an e-graph. This is the dominant *practical* LLVM-superoptimization lineage. For PReduce-feeding-LLVM, the relevant SOTA comparison/benchmark is Minotaur, and a hybrid (saturate to find candidates, synthesize/verify to close) is a real design point our docs don't consider.
- 2025 egglog maturation: relational e-matching (worst-case-optimal join) is now standard, and Oatlog (EGRAPHS 2025) compiles egglog AOT as a relational DB with semi-naive evaluation. This concretely validates our 'eqsat = monotone Datalog = propagator-network quiescence' equation (E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §3.4/§4.2) — the production engines literally ARE semi-naive Datalog now. PReduce's BSP-round rebuild semantics maps onto semi-naive iteration; worth borrowing the relational-join e-matching plan rather than backtracking match.

**PReduce implications:**
- RE-EXAMINE 'saturate to fixpoint' vs Cranelift's EAGER single-pass. The single hard production data point chose eager-cost-reducing rewriting over saturation BECAUSE saturation's rebuild + blowup didn't pay (avg e-class = 1.13 enodes). PReduce should add a design axis: per-rule-kind EAGER vs SATURATING. IN-fragment/confluence-by-construction/arithmetic (Axis-1 tags) are deterministic-cost-reducing and should run EAGER (no fixpoint, no held alternatives) — this collapses to Cranelift's regime and gets the cheap win. Reserve true saturation for the genuinely non-confluent optimization rules where multiple forms have different cost. Today PReduce's S0 'monotone rewriting stratum' implicitly assumes saturation for everything; that is the expensive default Cranelift abandoned.
- BENCHMARK FIRST whether fat e-classes earn their keep. Before Track 1 builds the full e-class-cell substrate, measure the e-class-size DISTRIBUTION on real Prologos reduction. If, like Cranelift, ~88% of classes are singletons, the heavy machinery (union-find merge, multi-form extraction, worldview-tagged alternatives) is over-engineering for the common case. The architecturally-cheap win (CHAMP structural sharing + good elaboration/code-placement) may deliver most of the benefit. Add this as a Track-1 gating measurement; it could re-scope Tracks 4/6 substantially.
- Track 4 (cost extraction) MUST distinguish TREE-cost (the free tropical residual / greedy DP — poly-time, what PPN 4C Phase 1B ships) from DAG/sharing-aware cost (NP-complete — what LLVM codegen actually needs: a shared subexpression is one emitted instruction). The Master's 'extraction IS residuation, inherited for free' is true only for tree-cost. Name the gap explicitly and choose: (a) accept tree-cost approximation (fine if rulesets are optimization-shaped per Fallin's 2-in-4M finding), or (b) add e-boost-style warm-start-ILP / treewidth / sparse-extraction for optimal. Do NOT let Track 4 ship believing the residual gives DAG-optimal — it doesn't.
- EXPLOIT STRUCTURAL THINNESS. PReduce's Layer-1 design already bounds e-graphs per-AST-PU (E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §7.1). If these per-PU e-graphs are low-treewidth or sparse, the 2024 results (treewidth extraction; sparse-eqgraph optimal extraction OOPSLA'24) give OPTIMAL DAG extraction in poly time — sidestepping the NP-completeness entirely for the common case. This is a concrete, citable opportunity the current design misses; add a Track-4 sub-question 'are per-PU e-graphs sparse/low-treewidth, and can we use the structural-tractability extractors?'
- ADOPT e-boost's three-phase extraction recipe as the Track-4 fallback for when an e-class IS fat: parallel greedy heuristic (PReduce already has broadcast propagators for the parallel part) -> adaptive prune by cost-threshold theta (PReduce already has tropical-threshold/set-latch machinery — this is a natural fit!) -> warm-start the exact solve seeded by the heuristic. The prune step maps cleanly onto the existing tropical-fuel threshold propagator: keep only e-nodes whose residual cost <= min*theta. This is a propagator-native realization of the SOTA extractor.
- Track 0.3 / LLVM interface: the SAME-DSL-FOR-MID-END-AND-LOWERING pattern (ISLE) is VALIDATED — PReduce's unified rule registry should explicitly host LLVM-lowering rules as a property-tagged rule kind, NOT a separate codegen module. BUT mirror Cranelift's PHASE separation: lowering rules run as a distinct extraction-then-lower phase over the reduced e-graph, not folded into the reduction fixpoint. 'One registry, two phases' is the production-proven shape; encode it as an explicit Axis-1 tag ('lowering') + a scheduler phase boundary.
- Track 8 (reduction.rkt parity/retirement) realism check: Cranelift, with a mature ISLE ruleset and years of work, gets ~2% runtime improvement at 7-8% MORE compile time, and even then the e-graph proper is marginal. PReduce's super-optimization claim should set HONEST expectations: the eqsat substrate's payoff over a good conventional reducer is modest-and-hard-won on general code, large only in specific domains (tensor/EDA). The strategic value is the UNIFIED SUBSTRATE + persistence + on-network parallelism, not a guaranteed speedup. Frame the SH 'super-optimizing compiler' claim accordingly to avoid over-promising.
- Consider a SYNTHESIS escape hatch for the hard cases (Souper/Minotaur lineage). Where saturation can't reach a known-better form, an SMT-synthesize-and-verify step (extract LHS, synthesize cheaper RHS, verify equivalent, add to e-class) is the practical LLVM-superoptimization SOTA. This composes with PReduce: synthesized equivalences are just new e-class members under the 'opaque/verified' regime. Minotaur is the benchmark to beat for the LLVM-feeding story; our docs currently have no synthesis axis at all.
- VALIDATE the 'eqsat = monotone Datalog = propagator quiescence' bridge against 2025 engines. Oatlog/relational-e-matching show production eqsat IS semi-naive Datalog. PReduce should borrow the RELATIONAL e-matching query-plan (worst-case-optimal join) rather than tree-walking e-class match — this is the on-network-natural way to do e-matching (a join over cells) and is now SOTA. Add to Track 1/Track 3 e-matching design.
- Persistence (Track 5) is the genuinely novel-and-defensible contribution. Cranelift does NOT persist (per-function, discarded); eqsat-MLIR/Schlatt persist across PHASES but not SESSIONS. PReduce's .pnet cross-SESSION e-class persistence is past every cited frontier. Concentrate the novelty claim here (and on the propagator-parallel rebuild), not on eqsat-reaches-codegen (Denali did that in 2002) nor on e-graph-as-IR (eqsat-MLIR/Schlatt did that in 2025-2026).

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- The .pnet -> LLVM lowering interface (Track 0.3) should be a TWO-STAGE contract mirroring Cranelift: stage 1 = extract a chosen DAG from the reduced e-class cells (cost-driven); stage 2 = apply LLVM-lowering rewrite rules to that DAG. The .pnet payload an LLVM consumer reads is therefore the EXTRACTED, post-reduction term-DAG plus the cost annotations — NOT the full saturated e-graph. Persisting the full e-graph is for cross-session REUSE of equivalences; the lowering handoff is the extracted representative. Make this split explicit in the format.
- Cost-model fidelity is the load-bearing risk for an LLVM target. Cranelift's simple per-node-additive cost works because its ruleset is optimization-shaped and code is then placed by a separate elaboration pass (LICM/remat) that handles the sharing/placement concerns. For .pnet->LLVM, decide WHERE register-pressure / sharing / code-motion cost lives: if in the e-graph cost (DAG-cost, NP-complete), extraction is hard; if deferred to an LLVM-side placement pass (Cranelift's choice), the e-graph cost can stay simple/tree-like. RECOMMEND Cranelift's split: keep PReduce extraction tree-cost-cheap, let LLVM's own backend (or a thin placement pass) own sharing/scheduling. This de-risks Track 4 enormously.
- LLVM is NOT acyclic-free at the IR level (phi nodes, loops) — but Cranelift's aegraph stays acyclic by only lifting PURE operators into the e-graph and leaving the CFG/control skeleton outside. PReduce's per-AST-PU e-graphs should adopt the same discipline for the LLVM path: reduce PURE sub-terms in e-class cells, keep control flow / effects OUTSIDE (PReduce already has this via the effect-stratum boundary, Track 7 / Axis-1 'opaque' tag). This makes the acyclic-extraction tractability results (treewidth/sparse) APPLICABLE and keeps the LLVM lowering well-defined. Confirm the effect-boundary = pure/impure split aligns with what's liftable into e-class cells.
- eqsat-MLIR's xDSL prototype and Schlatt's MLIR work both target MLIR; the collaborator's independent LLVM-lowering prototype is LLVM-IR-direct.
