There is a strategic choice for the .pnet consumer surface: lower to LLVM IR directly (collaborator's path, simplest for the super-optimizer story) vs go through MLIR (gets the eqsat-dialect ecosystem + multi-level lowering for free). Track 0.3 should at least NOTE that an MLIR landing would let PReduce e-class cells round-trip with the eqsat dialect — a possible interop win — even if the first cut is LLVM-direct.
- The .pnet content-addressing + Cranelift's 'implicit e-graph inside SSA IR avoids data-movement cost' lesson combine into a concrete optimization: for the LLVM handoff, the extracted DAG should be emitted in a layout that the LLVM frontend can ingest WITHOUT a second transcription. The cost Fallin engineered away (transcribing IR<->e-graph) reappears at the .pnet boundary as serialize/deserialize. Design the .pnet e-class fragment format so the lowering consumer reads the extracted representative DIRECTLY (zero-copy-ish), not by reconstructing a separate term tree.
- Treewidth/sparse-extraction matters specifically at the .pnet scale question (Master Open Question 12: does e-class persistence scale to millions of e-classes). If per-module e-graphs are kept sparse/low-treewidth by the per-AST-PU bounding, BOTH optimal extraction AND .pnet chunking get easier (sparse graphs partition cleanly for content-addressed fragment loading). Tie Track 0.3 chunking strategy to the same structural-thinness property Track 4 exploits for extraction — one structural invariant serves both.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- The 'phases ARE strata / cross-phase = cell-state propagation' vision (E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §5.5) is EXACTLY what eqsat-MLIR + Schlatt 2026 implement at the cross-PHASE level — and the value they report (equalities found early stay available late) is real and validated. PReduce inherits this for free. BUT Cranelift is a cautionary note: persisting/maintaining the e-graph across the whole pipeline added 7-8% compile time and the cross-fixpoint payoff was small on general code. Phase-collapse buys CORRECTNESS/EXPRESSIVENESS (no information loss across phase boundaries) more reliably than it buys SPEED. Sell the collapse on the former.
- Cranelift's three-stage shape — (1) lift pure ops into the e-graph, (2) rewrite, (3) elaborate back to sequential CFG with code placement — is itself a MINI phase-collapse: GVN + LICM + const-fold + instruction-pre-selection all happen in one rewrite fixpoint instead of as ordered passes, and 'repeating the classical pipeline multiple times does NOT get the same result.' This is concrete production evidence FOR phase-collapse eliminating phase-ordering. PReduce's typing/elaboration/reduction collapse (PPN 4D ambition) has a real production precedent here — cite it.
- Where phase-collapse genuinely wins per the literature: the PHASE-ORDERING problem (tensat/TENSAT's entire motivation; Cranelift's pass-interaction win). PReduce should target collapse where ordering currently HURTS (e.g. reduce-then-typecheck vs typecheck-then-reduce interactions; trait-resolution-feeds-reduction-feeds-trait-resolution loops). The §8.1 'e-class equivalences <-> trait/universe/coercion resolution coupling' open question IS a phase-ordering problem that collapse onto one fixpoint dissolves — strongest collapse argument in our setting.
- Caution on collapsing LOWERING into the same fixpoint: Cranelift deliberately did NOT — lowering is a separate ISLE phase over the e-graph output, even though it's the same DSL. The reason is that lowering extraction needs a SETTLED cost surface (you can't pick instructions while the e-graph is still growing). So PReduce should collapse typing/elaboration/reduction (PPN 4D) but keep a PHASE BOUNDARY before codegen extraction+lowering. 'Collapse the front, phase-separate the back' is the production-validated topology.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- The eqsat-to-hardware lineage (E-morphic, E-Syn, Churchroad, ROVER, tensor-descriptions->backend) is precisely eqsat reaching NON-von-Neumann targets — ASIC standard cells, FPGA LUTs, tensor accelerators. The pattern is uniform: rewrite rules encode logic->cells AND optimization simultaneously; extraction picks the gate/LUT mapping. This is strong evidence PReduce's 'rule registry + extraction' substrate is the RIGHT shape for a GPU/spatial/non-vN backend — the same machinery that targets LLVM can target a LUT/PE-array by swapping the lowering rule kind and the cost model (area/delay instead of cycles).
- Extraction's NP-completeness BITES HARDER for non-vN targets: the EDA results show DAG-cost (area/LUT-count, each shared cell once) is the realistic model, exactly the NP-complete case, and that exact ILP extraction 'rarely produces better designs than greedy' while failing on solver robustness (cbc 'infeasible'). The non-vN reach therefore DEPENDS on the structural-tractability extractors (treewidth/sparse) or e-boost-style warm-start-ILP. PReduce's per-PU sparsity is the lever that could make a spatial-target extraction tractable where monolithic eqsat fails.
- Cost-model PLURALITY is required for non-vN reach: cycles (LLVM/CPU), area+delay (ASIC), LUT-count (FPGA), FLOPs+memory-traffic (tensor). eqsat-MLIR explicitly defers 'combining multiple cost models' to future work; E-morphic/E-Syn each hard-code one. PReduce's tropical-QUANTALE (multi-dimensional, product/tensor of quantales — Master Open Question 5) is actually AHEAD here: a product quantale natively carries (cycles, area, energy) as components. This is a genuine PReduce advantage for non-vN reach the docs under-sell — multi-quantale cost extraction IS multi-target-aware extraction.
- The massive-parallelism IN-fragment story (HVM2-style, Levy-optimal sharing) is the natural fit for GPU/spatial execution of REDUCTION itself (not just the compiled output). PReduce's Axis-1 IN-fragment tag is the substrate for running reduction on a non-vN engine. But note Cranelift's finding that fat e-classes are rare in practice — the parallelism payoff of IN-fragment reduction is realized only when there's genuine independent work; bounded by the actual e-class-size distribution, which should be measured (see preduce_implications).

**Novelty / prior art:**
- DIRECT PRIOR ART for eqsat-to-codegen, under-cited in our docs: Denali (Joshi/Nelson/Randall, PLDI 2002) already did seed-e-graph -> grow-with-architectural-axioms -> extract-optimal-machine-code-via-constraint-solver. PReduce's vision is recognizably Denali on a propagator substrate. Cite Denali as the founding instance; the novelty is substrate+persistence+quantale-cost, NOT the idea of extracting optimal code from an e-graph.
- Cranelift aegraph (Fallin, in production via Wasmtime; RFC + EGRAPHS 2023 + Apr-2026 retrospective) is the canonical PRODUCTION instance of e-graph-as-mid-end with shared DSL for lowering. It is the single most important external data point for PReduce and directly tests several PReduce assumptions (saturate-vs-eager, fat-e-classes, extraction-bottleneck-or-not). Our E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §4.3 cites the 2025 acyclic-egraph meeting but NOT Fallin's 2026 retrospective with the 1.13-enode / 2-in-4M / 7-8%-overhead measurements — UPDATE §4.3.
- Extraction-as-the-real-problem is a 2024-2025 research wave our survey only partially captures: 'E-graph extraction is NP-complete' (effect.systems), e-boost (warm-start ILP, extraction=89% of runtime), E-Graphs-as-Circuits/treewidth (2024), Fast&Optimal-Sparse-Extraction (OOPSLA 2024), SmoothE (differentiable extraction, beaten by e-boost). E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §6.3 mentions ILP/sampling/learned but predates these specific results — add e-boost + treewidth + sparse as the current SOTA extraction toolkit.
- Synthesis-based LLVM superoptimization (Souper 2017; Minotaur OOPSLA 2024 for SIMD) is a parallel lineage ABSENT from our docs entirely. It is the dominant PRACTICAL approach to feeding LLVM a better instruction sequence, and is the benchmark/SOTA PReduce's LLVM story will be compared against. Not e-graph-based — but composes (synthesized equivalences = new e-class members). Add as prior art + benchmark target.
- eqsat-as-IR-dialect: eqsat MLIR dialect (2505.09363, xDSL) + Schlatt 'E-Graphs as a Persistent Compiler Abstraction' (2602.16707) are the two 2025-2026 systems that make the e-graph a first-class IR construct persisting across passes — Prologos's nearest architectural cousins. Both are cited in E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH (§4.5/§4.6); this facet confirms they STOP at cross-phase (not cross-session) persistence, leaving PReduce's .pnet cross-session story as the genuinely-novel extension.
- Relational/AOT egglog: relational e-matching (worst-case-optimal join) + Oatlog (EGRAPHS 2025, AOT-compiled semi-naive relational DB). Confirms 'eqsat = monotone Datalog' is now literal production reality. Strengthens (does not threaten) PReduce's propagator-quiescence framing; borrow the relational e-matching plan.
- NOT-novel to claim: (a) e-graph-as-IR (eqsat-MLIR/Schlatt), (b) eqsat-to-codegen (Denali), (c) same-DSL-mid-end-and-lowering (Cranelift ISLE), (d) tropical/semiring cost (Goodman 1999; widely used). GENUINELY novel for PReduce: cross-SESSION .pnet persistence; on-propagator-network BSP-parallel rebuild; MULTI-quantale (product) cost for multi-target extraction; worldview-tagged (colored) e-classes integrated with ATMS speculation. Concentrate novelty claims on these four.

**Open questions:**
- What is the actual e-class-size DISTRIBUTION for real Prologos reduction (beta/delta/iota + structural + arithmetic)? Cranelift's 1.13 avg suggests it may be near-singleton, which would re-scope Tracks 4/6 (extraction + speculation) from 'central' to 'rare-case'. This is the single highest-value measurement to take BEFORE Track 1 builds the full substrate. MEASURE EARLY.
- Are PReduce's per-AST-PU-bounded e-graphs low-treewidth and/or sparse? If yes, the 2024 structural-tractability extractors give OPTIMAL DAG extraction in poly time, dissolving the NP-completeness for the common case and feeding BOTH Track-4 extraction and Track-0.3 .pnet chunking. Needs an empirical treewidth/sparsity study on representative modules.
- Does PReduce want EAGER (cost-reducing, single-pass, Cranelift-style) rewriting for the deterministic rule kinds (IN-fragment/confluence-by-construction/arithmetic) and SATURATION only for non-confluent optimization rules? This dual-mode design is not in the current Master. Resolving it changes the S0-stratum semantics from 'always saturate' to 'tag-driven eager-vs-saturate'.
- For the LLVM path: does sharing-aware (DAG) cost live in PReduce's extraction (NP-complete, needs ILP/treewidth) or is it deferred to an LLVM-side placement/scheduling pass (Cranelift's choice, keeps PReduce extraction tree-cheap)? This is the pivotal Track-4/Track-0.3 cost-model-locus decision and it determines whether Track 4 is easy or hard.
- Should PReduce add a SYNTHESIS escape hatch (Souper/Minotaur-style SMT synthesize-and-verify) for hard cases saturation can't reach, with synthesized equivalences entering e-class cells under a verified regime? No synthesis axis exists in the docs today; Minotaur is the LLVM-superopt SOTA to benchmark against.
- Does the effect-stratum boundary (pure/impure split, Track 7) coincide exactly with the 'liftable into acyclic e-class cells' boundary (pure operators only, per Cranelift)? If they diverge, the acyclic-extraction tractability and the LLVM lowering both break at the seam. Needs a precise pure/liftable characterization.
- Can PReduce's tropical-MULTI-quantale (product) cost (Master OQ5) actually deliver multi-target (cycles/area/energy) extraction in one pass, beating the single-cost-model limitation that eqsat-MLIR and the EDA tools each hit? This is a potential genuine advantage but is currently only conjectured at the algebra level — needs a worked extraction example over a product quantale.
- Given Cranelift's finding that BUILDING the e-graph (not extracting from it) is the compile-time cost, and that an implicit-in-SSA representation avoids it: can PReduce make e-class cells share storage with the AST PU (implicit) rather than a separate CHAMP structure, to avoid the analogous transcription cost? Tension with the current Layer-1/Layer-2 separation in the Master.


## FR2 — The Phase-Ordering Problem and Its Dissolution (positioning the Prologos "multi-pass compiler collapses to a lattice fixpoint on the propagator network" claim against the canon)

**Mode:** frontier-web  |  **SHA:** n/a (web)

**Sources:**
- (external-url) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/201059.201061 — Click & Cooper, 'Combining Analyses, Combining Optimizations', TOPLAS 17(2):181-196, 1995. The canonical 'combine on one structure' result: a monotone framework that runs Conditional Constant Propagation + Global Value Numbering + unreachable-code elimination SIMULTANEOUSLY to a single least fixpoint over a product lattice, finding facts no sequential ordering of the same passes can. Theoretical backbone of the 'lattice fixpoint dissolves phase ordering' claim.
- (external-url) https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~lerner/UW-CSE-01-11-01.pdf — Lerner, Grove & Chambers, 'Composing Dataflow Analyses and Transformations', POPL 2002. The CLOSEST prior art to the Prologos claim: independently-specified analyses+transformations communicate IMPLICITLY via graph transformations and run to a mutual optimistic fixpoint ('super-analysis'), avoiding both the precision loss of iterating separate passes and the cost of hand-writing a combined pass.
- (external-url) https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~lerner/papers/lmcs11-eqsat.pdf — Tate, Stepp, Tatlock & Lerner, 'Equality Saturation: a New Approach to Optimization' (POPL 2009 / LMCS 2011 journal). Introduces the E-PEG: optimizations become non-destructive equality analyses that saturate a shared IR with equalities; a SEPARATE global profitability/extraction step (pseudo-boolean/ILP) picks the best representative. The reference 'eqsat dissolves phase ordering by never destroying' strategy. (PDF rendered as binary on fetch; content corroborated from POPL09/abstract + AfterMath summary.)
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.03082 — Willsey et al., 'egg: Fast and Extensible Equality Saturation', 2021 (POPL). Modern production-grade e-graph library; introduced deferred/rebuild-batched congruence closure (near-linear in practice) and made eqsat reusable infrastructure (egglog, Cranelift, DialEgg downstream).
- (external-url) https://v8.dev/blog/leaving-the-sea-of-nodes — V8 team, 'Land ahoy: leaving the Sea of Nodes', 2025. FRESH frontier limit-datapoint: a flagship production user of the value-based sea-of-nodes IR ABANDONED it for a traditional CFG IR (Turboshaft) — compile time halved, 7x fewer L1 dcache misses, simpler code. Direct evidence that 'value-based IR dissolves scheduling' has real practical limits.
- (external-url) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_of_nodes — Sea of Nodes overview: Click's mid-90s value-dependency-graph IR (relaxes total order into explicit data+control deps; needs Global Code Motion to recover a CFG). Adopted by HotSpot C2, GraalVM, libFirm, V8 TurboFan.
- (external-url) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superoptimization — Superoptimization overview: Massalin 1987 ('Superoptimizer: A Look at the Smallest Program') brute-force search for the shortest instruction sequence; the probabilistic-test idea that makes exhaustive search practical. Frames the search-based (not fixpoint-based) family.
- (external-url) https://theory.stanford.edu/~aiken/publications/papers/asplos13.pdf — Schkufza, Sharma & Aiken, 'Stochastic Superoptimization', ASPLOS 2013 (STOKE). MCMC sampling of x86 program space with a correctness-tolerant fitness function; matches/beats gcc -O3 on small kernels. The stochastic-search family that trades completeness for reach.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2208.04238 — POSET-RL (2022) + MLGO line: reinforcement-learning approaches to LLVM pass ordering. Treats phase ordering as an NP-hard sequential decision problem and learns a policy — the ML-for-phase-ordering family that MITIGATES rather than dissolves (still picks an order).
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2410.03120v2 — 'Solving the Phase Ordering Problem != Generating the Globally Optimal Code' (2024). Sharpens the canonical framing: even an oracle phase order over a fixed destructive-pass set need not reach the global optimum — motivates non-destructive (eqsat-style) and search (superopt) approaches over order-search.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/research/2026-04-30_PROPAGATOR_NETWORK_AS_SUPEROPTIMIZING_COMPILER.md — Prologos SH-series Stage 0/1 research note. §3.7 already states the 'stratified rewriting subsumes pass ordering' claim and §3.1 the 'propagator network IS the IR' claim; §4 comparison table; §6 separates grounded/designed/speculative. The doc this facet must sharpen and position against the canon.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/research/2026-05-02_E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH.md — Prologos PReduce-series e-graph deep-dive. §2 e-graph data structure + equality-saturation algorithm + extraction (greedy O(n) when additive/non-negative; ILP/sharing-aware DAG extraction NP-hard); §2.4 notes rule application is monotone == CALM-compatible. The internal anchor for the eqsat-on-propagators realization.

**Key findings:**
- The canonical phase-ordering problem (established, Click-Cooper 1995; sharpened by arXiv:2410.03120, 2024): an optimizing compiler has N largely-commutable DESTRUCTIVE passes; pass i enables/disables opportunities for pass j, so output quality depends on application order; there is NO universal best order (it depends on the function, the compiler, and the target); finding the best order is NP-hard AND — the 2024 sharpening — even the oracle-best order over a fixed destructive-pass set need not reach the global optimum. The root cause is DESTRUCTIVENESS: a rewrite commits to one form, discarding the alternative that a later pass needed.
- THREE established dissolution strategies, each attacking a different facet of that root cause. (1) COMBINE-ON-ONE-STRUCTURE (Click-Cooper 1995; Lerner-Grove-Chambers 2002): run the analyses+transformations SIMULTANEOUSLY to a single monotone least-fixpoint over a PRODUCT LATTICE, so they communicate during the solve instead of in sequence — dissolves *inter-analysis* ordering. (2) EQUALITY SATURATION (Tate et al. 2009; egg 2021): make rewrites NON-DESTRUCTIVE — add equalities to a shared E-PEG/e-graph that grows monotonically, never committing, then run a SEPARATE global extraction to pick the cheapest representative — dissolves *rewrite* ordering. (3) VALUE-BASED IR / SEA-OF-NODES (Click ~1995; C2/Graal/TurboFan): relax the total instruction order into explicit data+control dependencies so a value is computed in one canonical place and motion is a late global step — dissolves *scheduling/code-motion* ordering.
- Each strategy ACHIEVES a real win but has a hard LIMIT. Combine-on-one-structure: scales poorly as more analyses join (the combined transfer function and product lattice grow; in practice 2-3 analyses, not 60); it dissolves ordering only AMONG the analyses you actually fused, not the whole pipeline. Equality saturation: the monotone saturation can NON-TERMINATE (true saturation is undecidable in general → fuel/size bounds), and the 'pick the best' extraction is a SEPARATE, often NP-hard step (sharing-aware DAG extraction is NP-hard; greedy is only sound for additive non-negative cost) — i.e. eqsat dissolves the ordering but RELOCATES the hard part into extraction. Value-based IR: V8 ABANDONED sea-of-nodes in 2025 (Turboshaft, a CFG IR) — compile time halved, up to 7x fewer L1 dcache misses, code simpler — because the freedom rarely paid off (JS ops have side effects so the effect chain just mirrors the CFG) and manual effect/control-chain management was bug-prone. Value-based IRs win for pure-heavy code and lose engineering-wise for effect-heavy code.
- Superoptimization (Massalin 1987; STOKE/ASPLOS 2013; Souper) is a DIFFERENT family — it SEARCHES program space (brute-force / SMT-synthesis / stochastic MCMC) for an optimal or near-optimal sequence rather than dissolving the ordering of a FIXED pass set. It sidesteps phase ordering by not having phases at the kernel scale, at the cost of scalability (small kernels only) and, for stochastic variants, completeness. ML-for-phase-ordering (MLGO, POSET-RL, AutoPhase) MITIGATES rather than dissolves — it learns a good ORDER (a policy over the NP-hard sequence-decision problem), accepting that destructive passes and ordering remain.
- POSITIONING THE PROLOGOS CLAIM ('a multi-pass compiler collapses to a lattice fixpoint on the propagator network'): it is NOT a fourth independent strategy and NOT a mere restatement — it is most precisely a SYNTHESIS that places strategy-1's mechanism (simultaneous monotone fixpoint over a product/structural lattice) at the SUBSTRATE level and recovers strategy-2 and strategy-3 as instances on the same substrate. Click-Cooper/Lerner-Chambers is the direct intellectual ancestor: Prologos's 'cells with monotone merges + propagators firing to a BSP fixpoint' IS Lerner-Grove-Chambers's 'super-analysis with implicit communication', generalized from a fixed handful of dataflow analyses to ALL compiler stages (typing, elaboration, reduction, optimization, codegen) on one network. The novelty is not the fixpoint idea — it is (a) making the IR ITSELF the fixpoint structure rather than a structure passes run over, and (b) using SRE-typed lattice domains so arbitrarily many 'analyses' compose by construction via product-of-lattices, where Click-Cooper had to hand-prove each pairwise combination.
- CRITICAL CAVEAT the canon forces onto the Prologos claim: 'collapses to a lattice fixpoint' cleanly covers only the MONOTONE part of the pipeline (Click-Cooper's combinable analyses; equality saturation's add-only e-graph; both are CALM-compatible, exactly as our E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §2.4 and the on-network mandate require). It does NOT by itself cover (i) the NON-MONOTONE/destructive choices the canon shows are the real difficulty — these resurface in Prologos as the STRATA above S0 (S1 NAF, S(-1) retraction) and as EXTRACTION; and (ii) the eqsat lesson that the cost-driven 'pick the cheapest equivalent program' step is genuinely hard (NP-hard with sharing) — in Prologos this is the tropical-quantale extraction, which is NOT free just because the saturation is a monotone merge. So the honest claim is: 'monotone phase ordering is dissolved into one S0 lattice fixpoint; the residual NP-hard/ non-monotone optimization difficulty is RELOCATED to stratum-ordering + tropical extraction, not eliminated.'

**PReduce implications:**
- PReduce's design must internalize the eqsat LIMIT, not just the eqsat WIN: equality saturation dissolves rewrite-ordering by making rewrites monotone (add-only e-class merges = CALM-safe cell merges), but it RELOCATES the hard part into extraction. The E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §2.5 note already records that sharing-aware DAG extraction is NP-hard and greedy is sound only for additive non-negative cost. PReduce should state up front WHICH cost regime it targets (additive/non-negative tropical => greedy bottom-up extraction is the cell-merge itself, O(n); anything sharing-aware => an explicit harder extraction stratum). The '4A.d-style' claim 'extraction IS the merge operation' (superopt doc §3.2) is only TRUE in the additive-non-negative regime — flag this boundary in the PReduce master so it is not over-claimed.
- PReduce gets to claim something Click-Cooper could only do pairwise: because rewrite rules are SRE-registered DPO rules over typed lattice domains, adding a new rewrite rule is adding a propagator to the existing S0 fixpoint — the 'combination' of optimizations is automatic (product-of-lattices), not a hand-proved per-pair framework. This is the strongest precise statement of PReduce's advantage over the combine-on-one-structure canon and should anchor the PReduce novelty section: 'Lerner-Grove-Chambers super-analysis, generalized to arbitrary rule count via typed structural lattices + confluence-by-construction (prop:ctor-desc-tag).'
- Termination discipline is a first-class PReduce concern, inherited directly from the eqsat canon: true saturation is undecidable, so egg-style fuel/size bounds are mandatory. Map this onto Prologos's existing tropical-FUEL quantale (PPN 4C Phase 1B) — the fuel budget that bounds saturation is the SAME resource as the cost lattice, which is an elegant unification the PReduce master should make explicit (fuel-bounded saturation and cost-extraction share one quantale).
- Non-confluent rewrite rule sets (where order WOULD matter destructively) are exactly the case PReduce should route through BSP-LE speculation (worldview forks per E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §1.3): the e-graph's monotone merge handles confluent rules for free on S0; genuinely non-confluent / non-monotone choices become per-worldview branches with tropical cost deciding the winner. This is the concrete realization of 'the residual non-monotone difficulty is relocated to strata,' and PReduce's design doc should draw the S0(confluent saturation) / Sk(speculative non-confluent) line precisely where the canon draws the monotone/destructive line.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- The V8-leaving-sea-of-nodes 2025 datapoint is a DIRECT warning for the .pnet-as-IR-into-LLVM lowering path. V8's value-based IR lost on (a) compile time (2x), (b) cache locality (up to 7x more L1 dcache misses from pointer-chasing a graph where 'nodes change once every 20 visits'), and (c) effect-heavy code where the dependency freedom never materialized. Prologos's propagator network is even MORE graph-shaped and pointer-chasing than sea-of-nodes. Implication: the on-network fixpoint is the right place to do the MONOTONE optimization, but the LOWERING to LLVM should SCHEDULE the network into a CFG/linear form early (the sea-of-nodes 'Global Code Motion' analogue) rather than keeping the network shape down to codegen. Do not assume the propagator-graph representation is also a good low-level execution representation — V8's experience says it is not.
- Cache-locality is a substrate concern the .pnet/LLVM work cannot defer: V8's '7x L1 dcache misses' came from exactly the access pattern CHAMP/union-find e-graphs have. The 'cell/propagator/scheduler orthogonality' rule's cell-level storage-strategy hook (hot/warm/cold, unboxed-fixnum fast path) is the principled place to address this — but it must be VALIDATED with real cache measurements before the LLVM backend, not assumed. Flag a 'memory-layout for lowered networks' research item.
- The 'incremental compilation falls out of monotone fixpoint for free' claim (superopt doc §3.5) is genuinely supported by the canon (it is the work-list least-fixpoint of Click-Cooper, made demand-driven) — this is one place Prologos can claim a real structural win over Salsa-style engineered incrementality, BECAUSE the IR and the fixpoint engine are the same object. Keep this claim; it survives adversarial positioning. The .pnet content-addressing layer is what turns the structural incrementality into a cross-session cache.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- The defensible, precisely-scoped version of 'multi-pass compiler collapses to a lattice fixpoint': the MONOTONE inter-pass phase-ordering problem (Click-Cooper's combinable analyses; eqsat's add-only rewriting) collapses to a SINGLE S0 least-fixpoint over a product of SRE-typed lattice domains, with propagators as the (combined) transfer/rewrite functions and the BSP scheduler as the work-list solver. This is Lerner-Grove-Chambers 'super-analysis' lifted to substrate scope. State it this way — it is true and it has a 1995/2002 pedigree, which strengthens rather than weakens the claim.
- The OVER-claim to avoid: 'phase ordering is eliminated.' The canon shows the genuinely hard residue is (a) DESTRUCTIVE/non-monotone choices and (b) cost-driven EXTRACTION (NP-hard with sharing). In Prologos these do not vanish — they relocate to (a) the strata above S0 (the stratum stack IS a phase order, just a principled one derived from algebraic properties — monotone vs retracting vs topology-changing — instead of hand-tuned) and (b) tropical-quantale extraction. Honest framing: 'phase ORDERING is replaced by stratum STRUCTURE; the residual non-monotone+extraction hardness is relocated, not removed.' The superopt doc §3.7 already gestures at this ('what stratum does this rule live in?') — sharpen it to explicitly concede that stratum assignment is the residual ordering question.
- The stratum stack is itself a 2-3 layer 'phase order' (S0 monotone -> S1 NAF -> S(-1) retraction -> topology). Adversarial point the canon raises: is stratum ASSIGNMENT subject to its own phase-ordering problem? Answer (defensible): no, because strata are ordered by a FIXPOINT-REQUIREMENT partial order (Sk fires only after S0..S(k-1) quiesce), which is FORCED by the algebra (a non-monotone rule provably needs its inputs' monotone fixpoint first), not chosen for performance. This is a genuinely stronger position than LLVM/MLIR pass-pipeline tuning — but only because the order is DERIVED, and the doc must show the derivation, not assert it.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the cleanest one-line positioning for external audiences (papers, talks): 'Click-Cooper/Lerner-Chambers combine a FIXED SMALL SET of analyses into one fixpoint by hand; equality saturation makes rewriting non-destructive so a SEPARATE extractor can choose; sea-of-nodes makes scheduling a late global step. Prologos's bet is that ALL THREE are the same move — put the computation on a monotone lattice and take the fixpoint — and that doing it at SUBSTRATE granularity (the IR is the fixpoint object, the lattice domains are typed and compose automatically) is what lets the move scale past the handful-of-analyses ceiling of the 1995/2002 work, with the e-graph cost-extraction and the non-monotone strata as the named, bounded residue.'

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- The phase-ordering-as-monotone-fixpoint framing is what makes the non-von-Neumann / massively-parallel reach credible: Click-Cooper's combined fixpoint and eqsat's saturation are both CALM-monotone, hence order-independent, hence parallelizable WITHOUT coordination (Hellerstein 2010). This is the same property HVM2's interaction nets exploit for GPU-scale parallelism (superopt doc §2.4). So 'collapse to a lattice fixpoint' is not only a phase-ordering claim — it is the enabling condition for the non-VN reach, because a monotone fixpoint has no inherent sequential dependency. KEEP and emphasize this linkage.
- CAVEAT for the non-VN story, also from the canon: the NON-monotone residue (strata above S0, extraction) is exactly the part that DOES need coordination/barriers and is therefore the part that will NOT trivially map to a coordination-free GPU/non-VN substrate. The honest non-VN claim is 'the S0 monotone saturation is embarrassingly parallel / non-VN-friendly; the stratum transitions are global barriers (BSP rounds), and extraction is a separate possibly-sequential solve.' This mirrors HVM2's own structure (parallel reduction + sequential-ish readback).
- EXTRAPOLATION: the V8 cache-locality lesson scales to non-VN too — graph-shaped IRs with poor locality are even worse on bandwidth-bound accelerators. If the non-VN reach is to be real, the on-network representation that is GREAT for expressing the monotone fixpoint will likely need a DIFFERENT, locality-optimized representation for the actual parallel execution (the way HVM2 compiles to a flat node array, not a pointer graph). Flag as an open substrate question, not a solved consequence.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- PRIOR ART (direct ancestor, must cite): Lerner, Grove & Chambers, 'Composing Dataflow Analyses and Transformations', POPL 2002. Independently-specified analyses+transformations communicate IMPLICITLY (via graph transformations) and reach a mutual optimistic fixpoint = 'super-analysis.' This is structurally the Prologos propagator-network claim (independent propagators + cells + monotone fixpoint + implicit communication through shared cell state). Prologos's contribution OVER this is generalization-to-substrate (all stages, not just dataflow) + typed lattice domains that make N-way combination automatic. The novelty is NOT the combined-fixpoint idea; claiming it would be a restatement.
- PRIOR ART (theoretical backbone, must cite): Click & Cooper, 'Combining Analyses, Combining Optimizations', TOPLAS 1995 — explicitly: 'getting the best code may require iterating optimizations until a fixed point is reached; combining phases discovers more facts.' This is the 1995 statement of the exact thesis Prologos restates. Prologos must position as 'we operationalize the Click-Cooper fixpoint as the IR itself, and remove the per-pair hand-combination via SRE-typed product lattices,' NOT as a new discovery that combination beats sequencing.
- PRIOR ART (the non-destructive strategy): Tate-Stepp-Tatlock-Lerner equality saturation (POPL 2009) + Willsey et al. egg (2021). Prologos's 'eqsat happens for free when cells are tropical-quantale-valued and propagators are rewrite rules' (superopt doc §3.2) is a realization claim, not a new optimization paradigm. Novel part = the SUBSTRATE unification (same network does typing + saturation + extraction) and the adhesive-category confluence-by-construction (E-graphs are adhesive, CALCO 2025), which egg does NOT provide (egg rewrites must be hand-audited for soundness).
- GENUINELY NOVEL (defensible, with caveats): (1) the IR IS the fixpoint object — Click-Cooper/Lerner-Chambers run their super-analysis OVER a CFG/SSA; Prologos has no separate IR the fixpoint runs over, the network of cells IS both. (2) Arbitrary-rule-count automatic combination via SRE-typed lattice domains + confluence-by-construction, vs the hand-proved pairwise combinations of the 1995 work. (3) One quantale serving BOTH as saturation fuel-budget AND as extraction cost. (4) Provenance/ATMS threaded through the same fixpoint (no analogue in the optimization canon). These are real; each is a COMBINATION/substrate claim, and the doc already (§6) honestly marks them designed-but-not-delivered.
- CONTRA-EVIDENCE the positioning must metabolize: V8 leaving sea-of-nodes (2025) is a high-credibility 'the value-based-IR phase-ordering dissolution has practical limits' datapoint, and the 2024 'Solving the Phase Ordering Problem != Globally Optimal Code' result shows ordering-search (and by extension any single dissolution strategy) is not sufficient for global optimality. Both should be CITED IN the Prologos positioning to demonstrate the claim is made with eyes open, which strengthens credibility versus an uncritical 'we dissolved it' framing.

**Open questions:**
- Is stratum ASSIGNMENT a re-introduction of phase ordering under another name? The defensible answer is 'no — strata are ordered by a forced fixpoint-requirement partial order derived from each rule's algebra (monotone/retracting/topology), not chosen for performance.' But this needs to be PROVEN in the PReduce/SH design (show the derivation procedure: given a rule, which stratum, and why uniquely), not asserted. Until shown, the 'subsumes pass ordering' claim is partially open.
- Which cost regime does PReduce actually target, and therefore is extraction the cell-merge (O(n), additive/non-negative) or a separate NP-hard solve (sharing-aware DAG)? The whole 'extraction IS the merge' claim hinges on this and the answer is currently unstated. This is the single most load-bearing open question for whether the super-optimization claim is real or over-stated.
- Does the propagator-network representation that cleanly EXPRESSES the monotone fixpoint also EXECUTE well? V8's data (2x compile time, 7x L1 misses) says graph-shaped value-based IRs can be a poor execution representation. Open: does Prologos need a separate locality-optimized lowered form (HVM2-style flat array) for both LLVM codegen AND non-VN execution, distinct from the network used for the fixpoint?
- Can the monotone-fixpoint parallelism (CALM, order-independent) actually be HARVESTED on non-VN hardware given that the non-monotone strata and extraction need barriers/coordination? Open empirical question: what fraction of a real compile is the embarrassingly-parallel S0 saturation vs the coordinated residue? HVM2 suggests reduction parallelizes but readback does not; the analogue for Prologos is unmeasured.
- Is there measurable benefit (not just architectural shape) to the substrate unification over a well-engineered Lerner-Grove-Chambers super-analysis + a separate egg pass? The combination is novel; whether the combination is FASTER or produces BETTER code than the same techniques bolted together conventionally is the empirical question the superopt doc §6 already flags as speculative — it remains genuinely open and is the crux of whether 'distinctive' also means 'better.'


## FR3 — Emitting OPTIMAL LLVM IR from a high-level / functional / dataflow front-end (the human's Q2b)

**Mode:** frontier-web  |  **SHA:** n/a (web)

**Sources:**
- (external-url) https://llvm.org/docs/Frontend/PerformanceTips.html — LLVM 'Performance Tips for Frontend Authors' — THE canonical primary checklist for emitting IR the optimizer can handle: entry-block allocas (mem2reg/SROA), avoid aggregate values, noalias/align/dereferenceable/nonnull/noundef arg attrs, readnone/readonly/argmemonly/willreturn/nounwind fn attrs, TBAA + noalias metadata, lifetime.start/end, !invariant.load, inbounds GEP, poison-over-undef, nsw/nuw, avoid ptrtoint/inttoptr + wide ints + atomics + llvm.assume.
- (external-url) https://blog.llvm.org/2010/05/glasgow-haskell-compiler-and-llvm.html — LLVM Project blog: GHC's LLVM backend lowers Cmm (C--) to LLVM, with the custom ghccc calling convention pinning STG machine registers into hardware registers for NCG ABI compatibility.
- (external-url) https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1805.08842 — Farvardin & Reppy, 'Compiling with Continuations and LLVM' (ML/OCaml 2016). Definitive treatment of functional-front-end→LLVM: musttail marker (caller/callee type match), custom JWA (Jump-With-Arguments) calling convention (all regs caller-clobbered, arg regs == live-on-return regs), naked attribute to skip prologue/epilogue, shared spill frame, CFG IR as first-order machine-like rep. Speedups vs MLRISC.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/1908.05647 — Ullrich & de Moura, 'Counting Immutable Beans' (IFL 2019) — Lean4's λrc IR: explicit reference-counting, borrowed-vs-owned parameter inference, reuse analysis (FBIP destructive in-place update), drop specialization. The high-level-IR optimizations that must precede C/LLVM emission.
- (external-url) https://xnning.github.io/papers/perceus.pdf — Reinking, Xie, de Moura, Leijen, 'Perceus: Garbage Free Reference Counting with Reuse' (PLDI 2021) — Koka's precise RC: ownership passed into functions, garbage-free, reuse for guaranteed in-place updates, drop specialization; beats OCaml/Haskell/sometimes C++.
- (external-url) https://github.com/swiftlang/swift/blob/main/docs/OptimizerDesign.md — Swift OptimizerDesign — explicit statement that SIL-level optimizations (ARC optimizer, devirtualizer) run on Ownership-SSA BEFORE lowering to LLVM because 'when SIL is lowered to LLVM-IR the high-level information of the program is lost.'
- (external-url) https://mlir.llvm.org/docs/Tutorials/Toy/Ch-5/ — MLIR Toy Ch.5/6 — progressive/partial lowering: optimize in the high-level dialect, then discard structure on lowering to LLVM dialect; 'lowering too early obscures high-level structure.'
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09363 — 'eqsat: An Equality Saturation Dialect for Non-destructive Rewriting' (EGRAPHS/PLDI 2025) — represents the e-graph NATIVELY in MLIR so the IR stays an e-graph through compilation, reusing MLIR passes (CSE) and avoiding library↔IR translation. Direct prior art for PReduce's 'substrate IS the IR'.
- (external-url) https://dl.acm.org/doi/fullHtml/10.1145/3391902 — Reissmann et al., RVSDG (Regionalized Value State Dependence Graph, TACO 2020) — a dataflow IR that encodes control-flow as data-flow, relaxes CFG ordering, exposes parallelism, targets LLVM-IR abstraction level and lowers to it. Closest existing analog of 'a dataflow/propagator graph as the optimizing IR'.
- (external-url) https://llvm.org/docs/Statepoints.html — LLVM 'Garbage Collection Safepoints' — gc.statepoint/gc.relocate; RewriteStatepointsForGC must run LATE (after most opt) because relocation forces pointer spills to stack + stack-map encoding, acting as an optimization barrier.
- (external-url) https://janvitek.org/pubs/oopsla18b.pdf — Pelenitsyn et al., 'Julia: Dynamism and Performance Reconciled by Design' (OOPSLA 2018) — the three codegen optimizations (method-inlining devirtualization, unboxing, specialization) precede LLVM; turning off LLVM opt alone is 1.1–7.1x slower, but the front-end devirt/unbox is what makes IR optimizable at all.
- (external-url) https://groups.google.com/g/llvm-dev/c/N3r_a1Vbrog — LLVMdev thread 'LLVM IR is a compiler IR' — community statement that LLVM is biased toward C runtime conventions and a poor target for heap-allocated continuation closures / specialized calling conventions / guaranteed TCO without custom work.

**Key findings:**
- THE CENTRAL LESSON (Swift OSSA + Julia + MLIR + Lean + GHC, unanimous): LLVM optimizes only what survives lowering. High-level optimization (devirtualization, unboxing, reference-count elision, reuse/FBIP, equality-saturation extraction) MUST happen in the FRONT-END IR before lowering, because — verbatim from Swift's OptimizerDesign — 'when SIL is lowered to LLVM-IR the high-level information of the program is lost.' This directly validates Prologos's 'the propagator network IS the IR / PReduce extracts on-network BEFORE LLVM' architecture: the e-graph + tropical-cost extraction is the analog of SIL/SROA-level opt, and LLVM is the final dumb-but-fast backend.
- What makes IR OPTIMIZABLE vs not (LLVM Frontend PerformanceTips, primary): (a) values live in SSA virtual registers, not memory — entry-block allocas only, so mem2reg/SROA promote them; (b) scalar fields, not aggregate struct/array values; (c) rich attributes — noalias/align/dereferenceable/nonnull/noundef on args+returns, readnone/readonly/argmemonly(now memory(...))/willreturn/nounwind on functions; (d) aliasing metadata LLVM cannot deduce — !tbaa, !noalias/!alias.scope, inbounds GEP; (e) lifetime.start/lifetime.end + !invariant.load to bound and CSE memory; (f) poison over undef, nsw/nuw, fast-math when valid.
- What DEFEATS the optimizer (same doc + LLVMdev): ptrtoint/inttoptr (kills pointer-aliasing analysis — use GEP), values of aggregate type, non-byte i1 loads/stores, atomics/ordered ops, very wide integers (>~a few thousand bits), and llvm.assume (negative compile-time + opt effects, use sparingly). MOST IMPORTANTLY: lowering a computation to a SEQUENCE OF OPAQUE RUNTIME CALLS — LLVM cannot see across an opaque call, so it cannot inline, cannot prove no-alias, cannot eliminate redundant loads, cannot SROA, cannot vectorize. Call-heavy 'runtime shim' IR is the canonical pessimal emission.
- THE FUNCTIONAL-LANGUAGE ANTI-PATTERN, named precisely (Farvardin/Reppy CWC-LLVM; LLVMdev thread): LLVM has a 'bias toward C runtime conventions' — it assumes the C calling convention (callee-saved regs, prologue/epilogue, standard return) and does NOT natively give you guaranteed tail-call optimization, heap-allocated continuation closures, or specialized register conventions. A naive functional front-end that lowers every call through a generic C-convention call + a GC-spill at every safepoint produces IR LLVM optimizes poorly. The fix is a CUSTOM calling convention.
- CWC-LLVM's concrete fix (the template for a non-C front-end): (1) `musttail` call marker to FORCE tail calls to stay tail calls (requires caller/callee TYPE MATCH — they pad all params with i64s to consume every GP register and unify the type); (2) a custom 'Jump-With-Arguments' (JWA) calling convention where NO register is caller- or callee-saved and the argument registers ARE exactly the live-on-return registers (call and return have identical register use) — this erases prologue/epilogue overhead; (3) the `naked` attribute to suppress LLVM-generated prologue/epilogue; (4) one shared spill frame sized for max register pressure instead of per-call frames. Result: beats MLRISC on several benchmarks. This is the playbook for 'a functional/dataflow runtime emitting GOOD LLVM.'
- GHC's TBAA-for-aliasing technique (most relevant to a lattice/cell runtime): GHC's STG machine has pinned registers (Sp, Hp, R1..Rn, BaseReg) passed via the ghccc ('cc 10') convention. Because LLVM sees these as generic memory, GHC ANNOTATES every load/store with !tbaa metadata declaring a disjoint alias hierarchy (heap vs stack vs each STG register vs base reg) so LLVM's alias analysis knows e.g. a stack write cannot clobber a heap read → enables redundant-load elimination and reordering. A cell-based runtime can do the identical thing: tag distinct cell-classes / arena regions with disjoint TBAA roots.
- Lean (Counting Immutable Beans) + Koka (Perceus) define the OTHER axis of high-level opt that LLVM can't recover: precise reference counting with (a) borrowed-vs-owned parameter inference (skip inc/dec when a param doesn't escape), (b) reuse analysis / FBIP (a unique node is destructively updated in place — pattern-match-then-reconstruct compiles to in-place writes), (c) drop specialization (inline the is-unique check + recursive free). Koka's Perceus reports beating OCaml, Haskell, and sometimes C++. These are SOURCE/HIGH-IR transforms; by the time you reach LLVM they are baked into plain loads/stores LLVM CAN optimize. Critical for Prologos's QTT multiplicities (m0/m1/mw) which ARE borrow/ownership annotations — Prologos already HAS the linearity information Lean/Koka must infer.
- GC integration is an optimization tax (LLVM Statepoints doc): gc.statepoint/gc.relocate force every live GC pointer to be SPILLED to the stack at each safepoint and recorded in a stack map; RewriteStatepointsForGC is deliberately run LATE 'after most optimization is already done' precisely because the relocation sequence is an optimization BARRIER. Implication for Prologos: a precise-RC / linear-types runtime (Perceus-style, no tracing GC, no statepoints) AVOIDS this entire barrier — QTT linearity is an argument for NOT needing statepoints, keeping IR maximally optimizable.
- Julia confirms the front-end-decides thesis quantitatively: the three Julia codegen opts are method-inlining(devirtualization), unboxing, and specialization — all DONE BEFORE LLVM. Disabling only the LLVM passes is 1.1–7.1x slower, but the front-end devirt/unbox is what turns dynamic dispatch into the direct, inlinable, attribute-rich calls LLVM can then crush. Generalization: the front-end's job is to PRODUCE monomorphic, unboxed, attribute-decorated, inlinable IR; LLVM's job is the machine-specific grind on top.
- DIRECT PRIOR ART for PReduce's thesis (eqsat MLIR dialect, EGRAPHS/PLDI 2025): instead of an external e-graph library that you translate to/from IR, eqsat represents the e-graph NATIVELY as MLIR, keeping e-graph state live through compilation and reusing existing MLIR passes (CSE) for e-matching/extraction, 'avoiding translation overhead between the e-graph library and MLIR.' This is precisely Prologos's 'substrate IS the IR; equivalence classes ARE shared cells' — and it independently validates that extraction-then-lower (the e-graph is the optimizing IR, LLVM is downstream) is the right shape, NOT e-graph-as-bolt-on.
- RVSDG (TACO 2020) is the closest existing analog to 'a dataflow/propagator graph as the optimizing IR that lowers to LLVM': it encodes control-flow inside data-flow, RELAXES the over-strict ordering of the input program, enforces SSA, explicitly encodes loops, exposes parallelism, and targets the LLVM-IR abstraction level. RVSDG's whole pitch — 'expressing control flow as part of data flow reduces the need for complex optimizations to extract performance and enables easy conversion to parallel circuits' — is almost verbatim Prologos's mantra. Prologos's propagator network is a more radical RVSDG; RVSDG is proof the dataflow-IR→LLVM lowering path is viable.
- Opaque pointers (LLVM 15+, now mandatory) SIMPLIFY a fresh front-end: there is no longer a typed-pointer obligation, so a lattice/cell runtime that emits `ptr` for all cell handles is idiomatic; type information for optimization now flows through TBAA metadata + load/store element types + GEP source-element-type, NOT through pointer types. A green-field emitter (Prologos has no legacy) should target opaque-pointer IR from day one.

**PReduce implications:**
- PReduce's core architecture is VALIDATED by industry convergence: e-graph + tropical-cost EXTRACTION is the high-level optimization that must run BEFORE LLVM, exactly as Swift's ARC-opt/devirt runs on SIL before IRGen and Julia's devirt/unbox runs before codegen. The 'substrate IS the IR; the imperative reduction.rkt is retired' thesis maps onto eqsat's 'keep the e-graph native in the IR' (EGRAPHS 2025) — PReduce should cite eqsat as the closest prior art and as evidence that NOT bolting the e-graph on (no library↔IR translation) is the architecturally correct choice.
- Track 0.3 ('.pnet extension + LLVM lowering interface') is the LOAD-BEARING design surface for all of FR3. It must specify: (a) WHAT the LLVM consumer reads from a post-extraction .pnet — i.e. the single cheapest-derivation term selected by tropical residuation, NOT the whole e-graph (extraction collapses the e-graph to one DAG before lowering); (b) the cost model in tropical-fuel.rkt must be CALIBRATED to LLVM's downstream behavior — extraction picking a term that LLVM then de-optimizes (e.g. an aggregate-returning form, or a form that becomes an opaque runtime call) is a cost-model bug. The extraction cost lattice should penalize forms that lower to opaque calls / aggregate values / statepoints.
- CRITICAL ANTI-PATTERN for PReduce-to-LLVM: do NOT lower extracted terms to a sequence of opaque runtime-library calls (the 'runtime shim' pattern). That is the single worst thing a functional front-end does to LLVM — it cannot inline, alias-analyze, or eliminate redundant work across opaque calls. Every primitive that CAN be expressed as inlinable, attribute-decorated IR (arithmetic, constructor allocation, pattern-match discrimination, projection) should be emitted as such, reserving opaque calls strictly for the Track 7 effect/FFI boundary (which the design already correctly walls off as 'opaque cells uninterpretable to PReduce').
- Track 4 (cost-guided extraction via tropical-quantale residuation) should add a SECOND, LLVM-aware cost component or post-pass: the architectural cost (rewrite-rule cost) gives the cheapest DERIVATION, but the LOWERED cost (does this term lower to inlinable IR with good attributes, or to a spill-heavy opaque-call sequence?) is what the human's Q2b ('OPTIMAL LLVM') actually demands. PPN 4C addendum §4's multi-quantale composition is the mechanism — compose a 'derivation-cost' quantale with a 'lowered-IR-cost' quantale. This is the concrete realization of 'optimal' that distinguishes super-optimization from mere normalization.
- Persistence regimes (Axis 2) already align with TBAA disjointness: ground/closed e-classes lower to !invariant.load + TBAA-constant memory (the value never changes — LLVM can hoist freely); contextual/worldview-tagged classes must NOT be marked invariant. The .pnet→LLVM emitter should derive LLVM invariance/TBAA metadata directly from the persistence-regime tag — a free, principled source of the alias metadata GHC has to hand-build.
- PReduce should add an explicit open question / sub-track on the EXTRACTION→EMISSION boundary representation: eqsat (MLIR) and RVSDG both suggest a region/dataflow IR sits BETWEEN the e-graph and LLVM. Prologos can either (a) emit LLVM directly from the extracted DAG, or (b) emit an RVSDG-like regionalized dataflow form first (which preserves the parallelism the propagator network discovered) and lower THAT to LLVM. Option (b) better preserves 'all-in-parallel' through to the backend and matches RVSDG's proven 'easy conversion to parallel circuits' — relevant to the kept-in-reach GPU/non-von-Neumann ambition.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- CHECKLIST — what 'optimal LLVM emission' from a lattice/propagator runtime should look like (synthesized from LLVM Frontend PerformanceTips + GHC + CWC-LLVM + Lean/Koka):
- [1] SSA-FIRST, MEMORY-LAST: emit cell values into LLVM SSA virtual registers wherever a value is single-assignment in a region; reserve `alloca` for genuinely mutable/escaping cells and place ALL allocas in the entry block so mem2reg/SROA promote them. A propagator that reads a cell once and writes once is a pure SSA dataflow node, NOT a load/store pair.
- [2] NO AGGREGATE VALUES: lower compound cells (CHAMP nodes, tagged-cell-value entries, scope-cells) to per-field scalar loads/stores, never to `load {...}`/`store {...}` of struct type. Aggregates defeat SROA. Multi-field returns use the multi-register-return exception only.
- [3] ATTRIBUTE-DECORATE EVERYTHING: every emitted function/propagator-kernel carries memory(none)/memory(read)/memory(argmem: ...) (the modern argmemonly/readonly/readnone), nounwind, willreturn when valid; every cell-pointer argument carries noalias (cells in distinct arenas don't alias), align, dereferenceable(N), nonnull, noundef. A monotone propagator over disjoint input cells is the textbook noalias+readonly case.
- [4] TBAA ALIAS HIERARCHY OVER CELL CLASSES (the GHC technique): define disjoint !tbaa roots per cell-class / arena (e-class-cell memory, scope-cell memory, decision-cell memory, worklist memory) so LLVM knows a write to one class cannot clobber a read of another → unlocks redundant-load elimination + reordering across the propagator's body. Derive these roots structurally from the SRE domain registration, not by hand.
- [5] INVARIANCE FROM PERSISTENCE REGIME: emit !invariant.load + TBAA-constant for ground/closed e-class values (immutable once computed); emit lifetime.start/lifetime.end around per-round scratch cells so LLVM can stack-color and CSE them.
- [6] CUSTOM CALLING CONVENTION for the scheduler/propagator dispatch loop (the CWC-LLVM/GHC lesson): if propagator firing is dispatched via indirect calls, use `musttail` for the tail-dispatch + a JWA/ghccc-style convention (no callee-saved regs, arg-regs == live-on-return regs) to erase prologue/epilogue overhead in the hot scheduler loop. Pad params to unify types for musttail. Do NOT route firing through the generic C convention.
- [7] MONOMORPHIZE + UNBOX BEFORE LOWERING (the Julia lesson): the front-end (elaborator/PReduce) must devirtualize trait dispatch and unbox Nat/Int/Bool/known-layout cells into machine values BEFORE emitting LLVM; do not emit a boxed-everything + runtime-dispatch IR and hope LLVM recovers it — it cannot see across the box/dispatch.
- [8] NO OPAQUE-RUNTIME-CALL SHIM: every reducible primitive emits inlinable IR (or is marked alwaysinline); opaque calls are confined to the effect/FFI stratum. The presence of opaque calls in the hot path is the #1 measurable defeater of LLVM passes.
- [9] PRECISE RC / LINEARITY INSTEAD OF STATEPOINTS (the Perceus + Statepoints lesson): exploit QTT multiplicities (m0/m1/mw) to emit Perceus-style precise reference-count inc/dec with borrow-elision and FBIP in-place reuse, AVOIDING gc.statepoint/gc.relocate entirely — statepoints force pointer spills + stack maps and are a deliberate optimization barrier. Prologos already HAS the linearity Lean/Koka must infer, so this is a strength to cash in, not infer.
- [10] POISON, NSW/NUW, FAST-MATH, INBOUNDS-GEP, OPAQUE-PTR: use poison (not undef); attach nsw/nuw to index/arithmetic on Nat where overflow is impossible; inbounds on every cell-address GEP; emit opaque-pointer IR (LLVM 15+) from day one — Prologos is green-field, no typed-pointer legacy.
- [11] PIPELINE: start from the stock -O2/-O3 pipeline (LLVM's explicit advice), then add IRCE for any bounds-checked Nat/array access and extra LICM/LoopUnswitch for guard-heavy logic-programming loops; re-run -O2 on the optimized output to validate the emitter didn't produce un-canonical IR.
- [12] VERIFICATION GATE: after emission, assert via `opt` that the hot kernels are (a) free of opaque calls, (b) carry the expected memory()/noalias attributes (LLVM's own attribute inference can confirm/deny what you declared), and (c) survive mem2reg with zero residual entry-block allocas for pure-SSA propagators. This is the 'Network Reality Check' analog for the LLVM boundary.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- EXTRAPOLATION: 'Phase collapse' (collapsing the multi-stage elaborate→type-check→reduce→lower pipeline so that one on-network fixpoint does the work of several passes) is exactly what eqsat (EGRAPHS 2025) and RVSDG argue for at the IR level — keep ONE rich IR live through compilation rather than translating between a parse IR, a type IR, an e-graph library, and LLVM, because each translation LOSES information (the Swift 'high-level info is lost on lowering' lesson). Prologos's collapse of phases onto one propagator substrate is the maximal form of this; the FR3 risk is that the FINAL collapse-to-LLVM step is where information is unavoidably lost, so all alias/attribute/invariance metadata must be HARVESTED at that boundary before collapse, not reconstructed by LLVM.
- EXTRAPOLATION: collapsing typing and reduction onto one substrate means QTT linearity (a typing-phase fact) is available AT the reduction/lowering phase without a separate analysis — this is precisely what lets Prologos emit Perceus-style precise-RC WITHOUT the borrow-inference pass Lean/Koka need. Phase collapse turns 'infer borrow annotations' (a Counting-Immutable-Beans pass) into 'read the multiplicity already on the cell'. This is a concrete, defensible win of collapse for FR3.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the danger of phase collapse for optimal LLVM is the inverse of MLIR's 'don't lower too early' warning. If the collapsed network eagerly normalizes/reduces before the LLVM-cost-aware extraction runs, it may have already discarded the structure (e.g. a higher-arity form, a sharing pattern) that LLVM would have optimized better. The collapse must keep the e-graph's MULTIPLE equivalent forms live (non-destructive rewriting, the eqsat property) until the LLVM-aware cost extraction chooses — i.e. collapse the phases but NOT the equivalence classes.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- RVSDG is the strongest concrete evidence that a dataflow/propagator IR keeps the non-von-Neumann door open: the RVSDG papers explicitly note 'expressing control flow as part of data flow ... enables easy conversion to parallel circuits' and target HLS (the R-HLS / hardware-synthesis follow-on, arXiv:2408.08712). A propagator-network IR that lowers to RVSDG-like regions before LLVM would preserve the 'all-in-parallel' structure all the way to a circuit/GPU backend, whereas lowering straight to LLVM's sequential virtual-CPU model (RVSDG paper: LLVM 'is somewhat at odds with ... data dependence') re-sequentializes it.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the LLVM path and the GPU/non-vN path SHARE the front-end optimization work (monomorphize, unbox, devirtualize, extract cheapest dataflow DAG) and DIVERGE only at final emission. This means Prologos should design Track 0.3's lowering interface to emit a target-NEUTRAL post-extraction dataflow representation (the extracted e-graph DAG / an RVSDG-like region) and have LLVM-emission + GPU-emission be sibling consumers — matching MLIR's progressive-lowering rationale (optimize high, lower late, pick backend last). Lowering directly and only to LLVM IR would couple the whole compiler to the von-Neumann target.
- EXTRAPOLATION: LLVM itself has a GPU path (NVPTX/AMDGPU backends) and MLIR has gpu/nvvm dialects, so 'kept-in-reach' is realistic WITHOUT abandoning LLVM — but only if the emitter avoids von-Neumann-specific assumptions (statepoint-based GC has no GPU analog; opaque host-runtime calls don't exist on-device). The precise-RC/linear, opaque-call-free, attribute-rich IR that is optimal for CPU LLVM is ALSO the IR most portable to GPU/accelerator — the FR3 checklist and the non-vN ambition pull in the SAME direction, not against each other.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the custom-calling-convention lesson (JWA/ghccc) is von-Neumann-specific (it is about hardware GP registers). On a non-vN target this concern dissolves — the propagator graph maps to spatial dataflow directly. So the CWC-LLVM machinery is scaffolding for the CPU backend specifically, and should be labeled as such, not baked into the target-neutral IR.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- PRIOR ART (validates, does not pre-empt PReduce): eqsat MLIR dialect (Cheng et al., EGRAPHS/PLDI 2025, arXiv:2505.09363) is the closest existing realization of 'the e-graph IS the IR, lower from it.' It is in MLIR, not on a propagator network, and does NOT have Prologos's tropical-quantale cost extraction, QTT linearity, or speculative/worldview machinery. Prologos's contribution is the propagator-native substrate + cost algebra + linearity-aware lowering; eqsat is the citation that the e-graph-as-IR shape is sound and being pursued by the production-compiler community in 2025.
- PRIOR ART: RVSDG (Reissmann/Meyer/Bahmann/Själander, TACO 2020) — dataflow IR exposing parallelism that lowers to LLVM; and DialEgg (equality-saturation MLIR optimizer using egglog, 2025) and 'Equality Saturation for Optimizing High-Level Julia IR' (arXiv:2502.17075, 2025). These show the field is actively converging on (e-graph extraction at a high IR)→(lower to LLVM). Prologos is differentiated by doing the SAME on a monotone-lattice propagator substrate with QTT/session/capability types in the same network.
- PRIOR ART (the functional-front-end→LLVM playbook): Farvardin & Reppy CWC-LLVM (2016) for musttail+JWA+naked; GHC ghccc+TBAA (2010+); Lean Counting Immutable Beans (2019) and Koka Perceus (PLDI 2021) for precise-RC/reuse/FBIP. None target a propagator/lattice runtime. The NOVEL synthesis Prologos can claim: QTT multiplicities give the borrow/ownership info Lean/Koka must INFER, and the persistence-regime tags give the invariance/TBAA metadata GHC must hand-BUILD — i.e. Prologos's type system makes the optimal-LLVM emission information available for free.
- NOT NOVEL (do not claim): 'use TBAA/noalias/lifetime/attributes for better IR', 'optimize at the high IR before LLVM', 'custom calling convention for tail calls', 'precise RC beats tracing GC' — all are established LLVM/PL practice. Prologos's novelty is strictly the SUBSTRATE these are realized on (one monotone propagator network) and the SOURCE of the metadata (lattice/type structure), not the LLVM techniques themselves.

**Open questions:**
- Does Prologos emit LLVM IR DIRECTLY from the extracted e-graph DAG, or lower through an intermediate RVSDG-like regionalized dataflow IR first? RVSDG/eqsat/MLIR all suggest an intermediate region IR better preserves discovered parallelism (and the non-vN reach), at the cost of one more lowering stage. This is the single biggest unresolved FR3 architecture decision and belongs in Track 0.3.
- Should the tropical cost lattice (Track 4) be CALIBRATED against measured LLVM-lowered cost, or remain a pure architectural/derivation cost? 'Optimal LLVM' (the human's literal Q2b) demands the former; a pure derivation cost can pick a term LLVM then de-optimizes. Open: is this a second quantale composed in (PPN 4C §4 multi-quantale), a post-extraction LLVM-aware re-rank, or a feedback loop (emit→measure→re-extract, the equality-saturation superoptimization loop)?
- How is the custom calling convention for the propagator-dispatch hot loop realized through the .pnet→LLVM boundary — does Prologos register a ghccc/JWA-style cc with LLVM (requires the convention to exist in the LLVM build) or rely on musttail + naked + manual register discipline? CWC-LLVM needed LLVM-side support for JWA.
- Can QTT multiplicities be lowered SOUNDLY to Perceus-style precise RC in all cases, or do mw (unrestricted) cells still require a fallback tracing/statepoint path for cyclic structures? Perceus and Counting Immutable Beans handle cycles with weak refs / explicit breaking; Prologos must decide whether any cell class needs statepoints at all (and pay the optimization-barrier cost) or whether linearity + cycle-detection fully avoids it.
- What is the verification gate at the LLVM boundary — does Prologos run `opt`-based checks (no opaque calls in hot kernels, expected memory()/noalias attributes present, zero residual entry-block allocas after mem2reg) as a CI gate analogous to the existing 'Network Reality Check'? Without it, emission regressions (an accidental opaque-call shim, a dropped noalias) are silent perf cliffs.
- For opaque pointers + TBAA: how does Prologos derive the disjoint TBAA alias roots from SRE domain registrations automatically, and does the worldview/speculation tagging (which makes a cell's value context-dependent) correctly PREVENT marking those cells' loads as invariant? Mis-tagging a contextual cell as invariant would be a miscompile, not just a missed opt.


## FR4 — Dataflow/Lattice IRs + Abstract-Interpretation-as-Compilation + Declarative Compilers: novelty grounding for "the compiler IS a lattice fixpoint."

**Mode:** frontier-web  |  **SHA:** n/a (web)

**Sources:**
- (external-url) https://courses.cs.washington.edu/courses/cse501/15sp/papers/kildall.pdf — Kildall, 'A Unified Approach to Global Program Optimization' (POPL 1973) — the founding lattice-dataflow framework: global optimization as a meet-over-paths / fixpoint computation of an 'optimizing function' on a lattice over a CFG. ESTABLISHED.
- (external-url) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/512950.512973 — Cousot & Cousot, 'Abstract Interpretation: A Unified Lattice Model for Static Analysis of Programs by Construction or Approximation of Fixpoints' (POPL 1977) — establishes static analysis = least/greatest fixpoint of a monotone operator on a complete lattice; dataflow analysis is a special case. ESTABLISHED.
- (external-url) https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~pingali/CS380C/2010/papers/p291-wegman.pdf — Wegman & Zadeck, 'Constant Propagation with Conditional Branches' (POPL 1985 / TOPLAS 1991) — SCCP. The sharpest in-production example (LLVM, GCC) of analysis+optimization FUSED in one lattice fixpoint: a single dual-worklist fixpoint tracks CFG-edge executability AND SSA-value constants simultaneously; optimism (start at TOP) makes it strictly stronger than separate CP+DCE iterated. ESTABLISHED.
- (external-url) https://cs.nyu.edu/~pcousot/COUSOTpapers/POPL02.shtml — Cousot & Cousot, 'Systematic Design of Program Transformation Frameworks by Abstract Interpretation' (POPL 2002) — optimization/transformation ITSELF designed AS abstract interpretation: composition of source→semantics, semantics→transformed-semantics, semantics→source abstractions over fixpoint trace semantics; constant prop, partial evaluation, monitoring as instances. ESTABLISHED.
- (external-url) https://hal.science/hal-04535159 — Bautista, Jensen, Monat, Miné, 'Compiling with Abstract Interpretation' (PACMPL/PLDI, June 2024) — turns abstract interpreters INTO compilers via a free algebra over the standard signature of abstract domains; functor domains = compiler passes; soundness=forward simulation, completeness=backward simulation; derives SSA translation as an abstract domain. The strongest recent 'compiler IS abstract interpretation' result. ESTABLISHED (2024).
- (external-url) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/2908080.2908096 — Madsen, Yee, Lhoták, 'From Datalog to Flix: A Declarative Language for Fixed Points on Lattices' (PLDI 2016); plus Flix OOPSLA 2025 'A Design for Language-Integrated Datalog' (PLG/Waterloo). A language whose core IS least-fixpoint-of-monotone-functions on user-declared lattices — Datalog generalized from relations to lattices. ESTABLISHED; closest existing 'lattice-semantics language.'
- (external-url) https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~nk480/datafun.pdf — Arntzenius & Krishnaswami, 'Datafun: A Functional Datalog' (ICFP 2016; seminaïve follow-up POPL 2020) — pure total functional language that types monotonicity and computes fixed points of monotone maps on semilattices; generalizes Datalog; the fixpoint operator's least-fixpoint guarantee comes from monotonicity, paralleling stratification. ESTABLISHED.
- (external-url) https://souffle-lang.github.io/docs.html — Soufflé (Datalog→parallel C++ via Futamura projections / staged compilation) + Doop (declarative points-to analysis in Datalog). Declarative-compiler prior art: the analysis is a logic spec, the 'compiler' synthesizes a fast fixpoint engine. Doop's Datalog-to-Datalog optimization passes = order-of-magnitude speedups. ESTABLISHED.
- (external-url) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3591239 — Zhang, Wang, Willsey, Tatlock et al., 'Better Together: Unifying Datalog and Equality Saturation' / egglog (PACMPL/PLDI; tutorialized PLDI 2025) — one fixpoint reasoning system unifying Datalog (lattice/relational fixpoint) and equality saturation (rewriting/optimization), with cooperating analyses and lattice-based reasoning. The closest existing system to Prologos's PReduce + on-network-typing collapse. ESTABLISHED (2023–2025).
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.20782 — Arbore, Cheung, Willsey, 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' (PACMPL/PLDI 2026; arXiv Nov 2025) — recasts e-class analysis as abstract interpretation and gives a UNIFIED fixpoint algorithm for OPTIMISTIC analysis (start at TOP, à la SCCP) + non-destructive rewriting on e-graphs, explicitly tying together equality saturation + Datalog + abstract interpretation as one lattice fixpoint. The freshest and most direct convergent prior art for 'analysis + optimization = one fixpoint on the IR.' ESTABLISHED (2025–2026).
- (external-url) https://mirror.csclub.uwaterloo.ca/csclub/sussman-propagator-slides.pdf — Radul & Sussman, 'The Art of the Propagator' (MIT-CSAIL-TR-2009-002) + Radul's 'Propagation Networks' thesis — the propagator substrate Prologos builds on: autonomous machines over shared cells accumulating monotone partial information; computation by propagation-and-storage. ESTABLISHED foundation, but NOT used as a compiler IR by Radul/Sussman.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/tracking/2026-05-02_PREDUCE_MASTER.md — Prologos PReduce Series Master — the on-network reduction/super-optimizer plan (e-graph + DPO + tropical-quantale + GoI on the SAME propagator substrate that hosts parsing/typing/elaboration). The target of this novelty grounding.

**Key findings:**
- WEBSEARCH AVAILABLE — 10 searches + 4 successful fetches (3 fetches blocked by paywall/PDF-compression; substituted with HTML mirrors and search snippets).
- The equivalence 'optimization = least fixpoint of a monotone function on a lattice' is ~50-year-old, fully ESTABLISHED prior art. Kildall 1973 (dataflow as fixpoint of an optimizing function on a CFG lattice), Cousot & Cousot 1977 (static analysis = fixpoint of a monotone operator on a complete lattice; dataflow is a special case), and Kam-Ullman 1977 (monotone dataflow frameworks) jointly nail it. Tarski 1955 supplies the fixpoint existence theorem. None of this is novel to Prologos.
- SCCP (Wegman-Zadeck 1985, shipping in LLVM and GCC) is the SHARPEST in-production example of analysis-and-optimization FUSED in one lattice fixpoint. A single dual-worklist fixpoint simultaneously tracks (a) which CFG edges are executable (reachability) and (b) SSA-variable constants — and the fusion is provably stronger than running constant-prop + dead-code-elimination separately in any order, any number of times. The fixpoint already collapses two 'phases.' Caveat: the rewrite (replace value with constant, delete unreachable code) is applied AFTER the fixpoint, so SCCP fuses TWO ANALYSES, not analysis-with-rewriting.
- Cousot & Cousot POPL 2002 ('Systematic Design of Program Transformation Frameworks by Abstract Interpretation') already designs the TRANSFORMATION ITSELF as abstract interpretation — constant propagation, partial evaluation/specialization, and static monitoring are instances of composing abstractions over fixpoint trace semantics. So 'optimization is an abstract interpretation' (not merely 'enabled by one') is established since 2002.
- 'Compiling with Abstract Interpretation' (Bautista-Jensen-Monat-Miné, PLDI 2024) is the strongest recent 'the compiler IS abstract interpretation' result: abstract interpreters are turned into compilers via a free algebra over abstract-domain signatures; functor domains = compiler passes; soundness = forward simulation, completeness = backward simulation. They even derive SSA construction as an abstract domain. This directly weakens any Prologos claim that 'compiler-as-fixpoint' is itself novel.
- Flix (Madsen PLDI 2016, OOPSLA 2025) and Datafun (Arntzenius-Krishnaswami ICFP 2016 / POPL 2020) are EXISTING languages whose semantics IS 'least fixpoint of monotone functions on (semi)lattices.' Flix generalizes Datalog from relations to user-declared lattices with monotone functions; Datafun tracks monotonicity in the type system and computes fixpoints of monotone maps on semilattices. Prologos's lattice-cell + monotone-merge substrate is the same mathematical core, applied to a different surface (propagators rather than a relational/functional DSL).
- FRESHEST AND MOST THREATENING TO NOVELTY: egglog (PLDI 2025) + 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' (Arbore-Cheung-Willsey, PLDI 2026 / arXiv Nov 2025) already unify Datalog + equality saturation + abstract interpretation into ONE lattice fixpoint, and the 2026 paper gives a unified fixpoint algorithm for OPTIMISTIC e-class analysis (start at TOP, exactly SCCP's principle) + non-destructive rewriting on e-graphs. This is precisely Prologos's PReduce thesis (e-graph + analysis + rewriting on one substrate) — convergent prior art arriving in real time, on the SAME e-graph + Datalog + abstract-interpretation triad.
- Souffle + Doop are the canonical 'declarative compiler' prior art: the analysis is a Datalog logic spec; the system synthesizes a fast parallel fixpoint engine (Soufflé via Futamura projections); Doop applies Datalog-to-Datalog optimization passes for order-of-magnitude speedups. 'Compiler-as-declarative-fixpoint-spec' is an industrial reality, not a frontier.
- HONEST NOVELTY VERDICT: The COMPONENTS of Prologos's 'compiler IS a lattice fixpoint' are all established (lattice dataflow = fixpoint; SCCP fuses phases in one fixpoint; AI-as-compilation; lattice-semantics languages; egglog/eqsat unify analysis+rewriting). What is NOT clearly anticipated is the TOTALIZING SCOPE + the PROPAGATOR REALIZATION: prior art fuses a BOUNDED set of phases (SCCP: two analyses; AI-as-compiler: an analysis-derived pass pipeline; egglog: rewriting + analysis over terms). Prologos proposes parsing + elaboration + type-checking + QTT + reduction + module-loading ALL as cells-and-propagators on ONE BSP-scheduled monotone network where 'the network IS the IR.' No surveyed system runs the WHOLE front-to-back compiler as one propagator fixpoint. That breadth-claim is the defensible novelty; the per-phase 'it's a fixpoint' claim is not.
- FROM-MEMORY (unverified): MLIR's dataflow framework (mlir::dataflow, the SparseConstantPropagation/dense-analysis sparse-lattice infrastructure) is a modern, widely-deployed generic 'lattice fixpoint as compiler-infrastructure' substrate inside a production compiler — additional evidence that 'lattice fixpoint = compiler IR machinery' is mainstream, not frontier. Prologos should cite MLIR's dataflow framework as the closest production analogue to its on-network-typing layer.

**PReduce implications:**
- PReduce's core thesis (reduction as e-graph + rewriting + cost-extraction on a lattice substrate) has strong convergent validation from egglog (PLDI 2025) and 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' (PLDI 2026): both demonstrate that e-class analysis + non-destructive rewriting CAN run as one lattice fixpoint. PReduce should explicitly position against egglog as the nearest peer system and articulate what the propagator/BSP realization adds beyond egglog's Datalog-engine realization (answer: scheduler-portability + the SAME substrate also hosting typing/parsing — egglog is reduction+analysis only).
- ADOPT SCCP-STYLE OPTIMISM in PReduce's e-class analysis. 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' (Arbore-Cheung-Willsey) shows that initializing e-class abstract values at TOP (optimistic) rather than BOTTOM (pessimistic) is necessary to analyze CYCLIC programs (SSA / recursive terms) effectively, and yields strictly more optimization. PReduce's cost-lattice and any e-class-attached analysis (the 'cost annotation' and 'representative' components in PReduce Master Layer 2) should default to optimistic initialization where the merge is a meet toward a refined value — this is a concrete, citable design import for Track 4 (cost extraction) and Track 6 (speculative reduction).
- PReduce's 'refinement-poset e-class cell' (Layer 2, ':order :refinement') is the e-class-analysis lattice of the eqsat literature. Cross-reference Willsey et al. 2021 egg e-class analysis AND the 2026 optimism paper directly in Track 0.1's NTT model so the e-class cell's merge semantics inherit the established (now optimistic) abstract-interpretation framing rather than being derived from scratch.
- PReduce Open Question Q2 (e-class merge under partial information: merge immediately vs wait) is exactly the pessimistic-vs-optimistic question the 2026 optimism paper answers: optimistic merge (proceed at TOP, refine via the monotone meet) is the resolution that preserves both parallelism AND information, and is sound for the fixpoint. Q2 can be CLOSED by adopting the optimism paper's framing; cite it.
- The 'transformation applied after the fixpoint' caveat from SCCP is a design fork PReduce must decide explicitly: equality saturation (PReduce's model) does rewriting DURING the fixpoint (e-graph grows monotonically), unlike SCCP which analyzes-then-rewrites. PReduce should state in Track 0.1 that it adopts the eqsat 'rewrite-during-fixpoint' discipline (monotone e-graph growth = CALM-safe), explicitly distinguishing from the SCCP analyze-then-transform pattern, and note this is what makes the on-network mantra ('rule application IS propagator firing') coherent.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- 'Compiling with Abstract Interpretation' (PLDI 2024) derives SSA CONSTRUCTION as an abstract domain (functor domain = compiler pass, soundness = forward simulation). Since LLVM is SSA-based and the lowering target, PReduce/SH Track 4 should consider expressing the .pnet→LLVM lowering as a sequence of abstract-domain functors with forward-simulation soundness obligations — this gives a principled, peer-reviewed correctness scaffold for the lowering rather than ad-hoc translation. The forward-simulation proof obligation maps cleanly onto Prologos's monotone-merge / CALM guarantees.
- LLVM's own SCCP and the MLIR dataflow framework (sparse/dense lattice analyses) are the production realization of 'lattice fixpoint inside the compiler.' When PReduce lowers to LLVM, it will hand off to a backend that ALREADY runs lattice fixpoints (SCCP, value-range, etc.).
