The .pnet→LLVM interface (Track 0.3) should be designed knowing the downstream consumer re-runs its own lattice analyses — avoid PReduce redundantly proving facts LLVM's SCCP will re-derive; instead emit the e-graph's extracted optimum and let LLVM's fixpoints handle target-specific value propagation. Document this division-of-labor in Track 0.3.
- Soufflé's 'Datalog → parallel C++ via Futamura projections / staged compilation' is a direct model for SH Track 4: a declarative fixpoint spec (the propagator network) compiled to fast native code. The .pnet-as-network-value (SH Track 1) + LLVM lowering is structurally a Futamura-projection story (specialize the propagator interpreter w.r.t. a fixed network → a compiled network). Citing Soufflé's staged-compilation pipeline gives a proven engineering template for lowering an on-network fixpoint to native code while preserving parallelism.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- The 'phase collapse' (parsing/typing/elaboration/reduction as ONE fixpoint, not a pipeline) is PARTIALLY established and PARTIALLY novel. ESTABLISHED: within a single concern, fusing phases in one lattice fixpoint is routine — SCCP fuses reachability+constant-analysis; AI-as-compilation fuses analysis with pass generation; egglog/eqsat fuse rewriting+analysis. NOVEL-IN-PROLOGOS: no surveyed system collapses the ENTIRE front-to-back compiler (lexing→parsing→elaboration→type/QTT-checking→reduction→codegen) into ONE monotone propagator network. The phase-collapse claim is defensible ONLY at the totalizing scope; it is NOT defensible phase-by-phase.
- SCCP is the canonical proof-of-concept that phase collapse BUYS PRECISION, not just elegance: fusing constant-prop + dead-code-elim into one optimistic fixpoint finds constants that NO sequential ordering of the two passes finds. Prologos should make the analogous PRECISION argument for its collapse (e.g., type information unlocking a reduction that unlocks more type information, reachable only at the joint fixpoint) — without a concrete 'unreachable-by-any-phase-ordering' example, the collapse is an elegance claim, not a capability claim. This is the single strongest framing available for the phase-collapse narrative and it is grounded in a 40-year-old, shipping result.
- The optimism principle (start at TOP, refine downward via meet) is the mechanism that makes phase collapse SOUND in the presence of cycles/recursion. SCCP and the 2026 optimism-in-eqsat paper both depend on it. Prologos's collapsed network, which has genuine cyclic dependencies (a type depends on a reduction that depends on a type), MUST use optimistic (top-initialized) cells for the cross-phase dependencies or the joint fixpoint will be needlessly imprecise / fail to converge to the strongest result. This is a load-bearing, citable design constraint on the phase-collapse architecture.
- HONEST CAVEAT for the novelty narrative: 'the compiler is one fixpoint' will read as incremental to a PL audience that knows AI-as-compilation (2024), Flix/Datafun, and egglog, UNLESS Prologos foregrounds (a) the propagator/cell realization (vs relational engine), (b) the scheduler-orthogonality (one network runs under BSP / Gauss-Seidel / future LLVM-lowered scheduler), and (c) the breadth (whole compiler, including dependent types + QTT + sessions + logic programming, not just a dataflow analysis or a term rewriter). Lead with breadth + substrate, NOT with 'it's a fixpoint.'

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- Datalog/Flix/Souffle compile lattice fixpoints to PARALLEL backends (Soufflé → parallel C++; Doop-on-Soufflé reports up-to-4x from parallelism for free). This validates the Prologos premise that a declarative lattice-fixpoint spec is a good SOURCE for automatic parallelization — the GPU/non-von-Neumann reach inherits the same property: a monotone fixpoint with no imposed ordering is the parallelization-friendly form. Cite Soufflé's parallel-datastructure results as evidence that the on-network fixpoint is hardware-portable.
- The CALM theorem framing (monotone = coordination-free) that Prologos already uses is exactly why these lattice systems parallelize. For non-von-Neumann reach, the established lesson is: keep the cross-phase merges MONOTONE (join/meet-semilattice) so the collapsed compiler stays coordination-free; the moment a phase needs non-monotone retraction (PReduce S(-1)), it becomes a synchronization point that bounds GPU/distributed scalability. The strata boundaries are therefore the natural 'sync barrier' map for a non-vN backend — design the GPU lowering to run S0 (monotone) on-device and bounce to host only at stratum boundaries.
- EXTRAPOLATION: 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' notes pessimistic analysis is ineffective on cyclic programs; optimistic top-initialization is what lets the fixpoint converge across cycles. On a non-vN substrate (no cheap global coordination), optimistic-monotone-refinement is doubly attractive because each processing element can proceed from TOP locally and refine on incoming messages without waiting — the optimistic discipline maps naturally onto asynchronous/dataflow hardware. This strengthens the case that PReduce's optimism choice is also the right NON-VON-NEUMANN choice.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- ESTABLISHED (not novel): 'optimization/analysis = least fixpoint of a monotone function on a lattice.' Kildall 1973; Cousot & Cousot 1977; Kam-Ullman 1977; Tarski 1955 (fixpoint existence). This is the founding consensus of dataflow analysis and abstract interpretation.
- ESTABLISHED (not novel): analysis+optimization FUSED in one lattice fixpoint, in production, since 1985 — SCCP (Wegman-Zadeck), shipping in LLVM and GCC, fuses CFG-reachability + constant-value analysis in one optimistic worklist, provably stronger than separate CP+DCE. Caveat: SCCP fuses two ANALYSES; the rewrite is a post-fixpoint pass.
- ESTABLISHED (not novel): optimization/transformation designed AS abstract interpretation — Cousot & Cousot POPL 2002 (constant prop, partial evaluation, monitoring as abstractions over fixpoint trace semantics).
- ESTABLISHED (not novel, and recent): the compiler ITSELF as abstract interpretation — 'Compiling with Abstract Interpretation' (Bautista-Jensen-Monat-Miné, PLDI 2024): abstract interpreters turned into compilers; free algebra over abstract domains; functors = passes; soundness=forward-sim, completeness=backward-sim; SSA derived as an abstract domain.
- ESTABLISHED (not novel): languages whose semantics IS least-fixpoint-of-monotone-functions on lattices — Flix (Madsen, PLDI 2016 + OOPSLA 2025) and Datafun (Arntzenius-Krishnaswami, ICFP 2016 / POPL 2020). Flix = Datalog generalized to lattices + monotone functions; Datafun = monotonicity-typed functional Datalog.
- ESTABLISHED (not novel): declarative compilers — Souffle (Datalog→parallel C++ via Futamura projections) + Doop (Datalog points-to with Datalog-to-Datalog optimization passes).
- ESTABLISHED AND VERY RECENT — the most direct convergent prior art: egglog (PLDI 2025) and 'Optimism in Equality Saturation' (Arbore-Cheung-Willsey, PLDI 2026 / arXiv 2511.20782) unify Datalog + equality saturation + abstract interpretation into ONE lattice fixpoint, with optimistic (SCCP-style) e-class analysis + non-destructive rewriting in a single algorithm. This anticipates the analysis-and-rewriting half of Prologos's PReduce thesis on the SAME e-graph + Datalog + AI triad. Prologos MUST cite and differentiate.
- FOUNDATION (used, not original to Prologos): Radul & Sussman 'The Art of the Propagator' (2009) — the propagator substrate. Note: Radul/Sussman did NOT use propagators as a compiler IR; that application is Prologos's.
- GENUINELY NOVEL IN PROLOGOS (defensible claim): the TOTALIZING scope + PROPAGATOR realization — running the WHOLE front-to-back compiler (lexing→parsing→elaboration→dependent-type/QTT/session checking→reduction→codegen, plus logic-programming) as ONE BSP-scheduled monotone propagator network where 'the network IS the IR,' under explicit cell/propagator/scheduler orthogonality (portable across schedulers). No surveyed system collapses the entire compiler this way; all prior art fuses a BOUNDED set of phases or a single concern (analysis, or term rewriting).
- NOVELTY NUANCE to state honestly: each INDIVIDUAL claim ('typing is a fixpoint', 'reduction is rewriting on a lattice', 'analysis is an abstract interpretation') is anticipated. The novelty is COMPOSITIONAL — the conjunction at full compiler scope on one substrate — not any single conjunct. A reviewer who knows AI-as-compilation + egglog + Flix will find per-phase claims familiar; lead with the conjunction, the substrate, and a precision-gain example (SCCP-analogue) that no phase-ordering achieves.

**Open questions:**
- Can Prologos exhibit a CONCRETE precision-gain example analogous to SCCP — a result (e.g., a type or a reduction or a dispatch) reachable ONLY at the joint cross-phase fixpoint and by NO ordering of separate phases? Without this, the phase-collapse claim is elegance, not capability. This is the single highest-value artifact to produce for the novelty argument.
- What precisely distinguishes Prologos's PReduce-on-propagators from egglog (PLDI 2025) beyond the substrate (Datalog-engine vs propagator-network)? Both run analysis+rewriting as one lattice fixpoint. Candidate differentiators: scheduler-portability, dependent-type/QTT awareness, the same substrate also hosting parsing/typing. Needs a crisp, defensible answer before any external write-up.
- Does 'Compiling with Abstract Interpretation' (PLDI 2024) already subsume Prologos's 'compiler-as-fixpoint' claim for the analysis-derived pass-pipeline portion? Need to read the full paper (HAL PDF was access-blocked here) to determine how much of the front-end (parsing, elaboration, dependent typing) it does NOT cover — that gap is Prologos's room.
- Is the cross-phase dependency genuinely cyclic in a way that REQUIRES optimistic (top-initialized) cells and a single joint fixpoint, or is it stratifiable into a DAG of phases (in which case 'collapse' is presentational, and a staged pipeline of fixpoints would be equivalent and simpler)? The answer determines whether phase-collapse is load-bearing or cosmetic.
- How does Prologos's whole-compiler-as-one-network claim interact with the well-known undecidability of constant propagation (and a fortiori dependent-type-checking + reduction)? The lattice-fixpoint framing must clarify which approximations (TOP/widening) are taken where, so the 'collapse' does not implicitly claim to decide undecidable problems exactly.
- MLIR's dataflow framework is the closest production 'generic lattice-fixpoint compiler infrastructure' — does Prologos's on-network-typing layer offer anything MLIR's sparse/dense analysis framework does not, beyond running the rest of the compiler on the same substrate? Needs a direct MLIR comparison (was FROM-MEMORY here; verify against current MLIR docs).


## FR5 — GPU/GPGPU + parallel-target lowering from a dataflow/BSP IR (keeping non-von-Neumann in reach)

**Mode:** frontier-web  |  **SHA:** n/a (web)

**Sources:**
- (external-url) https://mlir.llvm.org/docs/Dialects/GPU/ — MLIR 'gpu' dialect reference (fetched). Establishes that GPU lowering requires EXPLICITLY PARALLEL IR — grid/block/thread dims via gpu.launch, kernel boundaries (gpu.module/gpu.func), explicit memory address spaces (global/workgroup/private/constant), async deps via !gpu.async.token, in-kernel reductions via gpu.all_reduce/gpu.shuffle/gpu.subgroup_reduce. The pipeline 'does not do GPU parallelization' — the source IR must already expose the parallelism.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2311.02206v4 — 'Optimizing Datalog for the GPU' (GDlog/GPUlog, Sun et al., fetched). THE closest match to a propagator network: monotone fixpoint over relations on GPU. HISA dense hash-indexed-sorted-array beats pointer/hash stores; semi-naive new/delta/full = delta-driven iteration; 4 kernel launches/iteration with kernel-boundary BSP barriers; temporal materialization to balance warp divergence on N-way joins; Eager Buffer Management for dynamic relation growth; 35-45x over Soufflé.
- (external-url) https://mlir.llvm.org/docs/Dialects/NVVMDialect/ — MLIR 'nvvm' dialect + GPUToNVVMPipeline. The default NVVM-target lowering path; nvgpu is the intermediary between target-agnostic gpu/vector dialects and NVVM intrinsics; models NVIDIA address spaces and PTX functionality. Shows the staged-dialect lowering model a Prologos backend would plug into.
- (external-url) https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/advancing-gpu-programming-with-the-cuda-tile-ir-backend-for-openai-triton/ — NVIDIA CUDA Tile IR backend for OpenAI Triton (2025-2026). Triton operates on block-level tiles (statically-shaped sub-arrays); compiler auto-handles coalescing, shared memory, thread scheduling. Confirms the modern abstraction is TILE-of-dense-data, not single-element and not sparse-graph — the wrong granularity for sparse propagator activation.
- (external-url) https://futhark-lang.org/publications/ppopp19.pdf — 'Incremental Flattening for Nested Data Parallelism' (Henriksen et al., Futhark). Moderate/incremental flattening — middle ground between NESL full flattening (max parallelism, O(n^3) blow-up, destroys locality) and no flattening. Relevant to how Prologos's nested/irregular network parallelism would need flattening to hit flat GPU SIMT.
- (external-url) https://hackage.haskell.org/package/accelerate — Data.Array.Accelerate (Haskell EDSL -> CUDA via LLVM PTX). Explicitly LIMITED to flat data-parallelism over regular multidimensional arrays; the Acc/Exp stratification 'statically excludes nested, irregular data-parallelism.' Concrete evidence the array-DSL family is structurally mismatched to sparse dynamic propagator graphs.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/1701.01170 — Gunrock: GPU Graph Analytics (Wang et al.). Data-centric BSP abstraction over a FRONTIER (explicit active subset of vertices/edges); all ops bulk-synchronous, manipulate/compute new frontier. The frontier IS an explicit activation set — the design pattern Prologos needs for sparse per-round propagator activation. 'Frontier' beats Gather-Apply-Scatter for GPU graph frameworks.
- (external-url) http://regent-lang.org/images/regent2015.pdf — Regent/Legion (Slaughter, Bauer et al.). Dataflow task-graph runtime: logical regions express locality+independence; runtime DYNAMICALLY extracts parallelism by computing a dataflow graph; supports irregular parallelism; sequential-appearing programs, no explicit sync. The architectural cousin to Prologos's cell/propagator/scheduler split, but CPU/distributed-HPC, not pure SIMT.
- (external-url) https://www.comp.nus.edu.sg/~tulika/CGRA-Survey.pdf — CGRA survey (Liu et al.) + FlexC (Woodruff 2023, via search). Coarse-grained reconfigurable arrays embrace the dataflow computing model; PE-array mapping is NP-complete; FlexC uses EQUALITY SATURATION (e-graphs) for dataflow rewriting to expand mapping coverage 2.2x. Direct bridge: PReduce's e-graphs could double as a spatial-mapping engine for a dataflow/CGRA target.
- (external-url) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dataflow_architecture — Dataflow architecture overview + 2024-2025 hardware (via search): NextSilicon Maverick-2 dataflow HPC accelerator (Oct 2024, Sandia Spectra), Efficient Computer Electron E1 dataflow chip (Jul 2025), SPADA spatial-dataflow language (arXiv 2511.09447), content-addressable parallel processors. A propagator network IS a dataflow graph — these are MORE natural targets than von-Neumann GPUs.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/research/2026-05-02_PARALLEL_DECOMPOSITION_AND_HASSE_ADJACENCY_FOR_PROPAGATORS.md — OUR-DOC. PTF synthesis: two-mind-space framing (algorithmic vs information-flow); Hyperlattice Conjecture (Hasse diagram = optimal parallel decomposition), graded by variety; Boolean=hypercube confirmed, distributive=Garg LLP, tropical=Bellman-Ford/Floyd-Warshall; communication-bound parallelism; broadcast-propagator = Blelloch prefix-scan; Kahn/CALM monotone determinism. The theory layer FR5 must respect when choosing GPU lowering shapes.

**Key findings:**
- GPU lowering today is LOWERING, not PARALLELIZATION. MLIR's gpu dialect (and Triton, XLA:GPU) all REQUIRE explicitly-parallel source IR: grid/block/thread dims via gpu.launch, kernel boundaries (gpu.module/gpu.func), explicit memory address spaces (global/workgroup/private/constant), async deps via !gpu.async.token, in-kernel reductions via gpu.all_reduce/gpu.shuffle. Quote: 'This pipeline specifically requires explicitly parallel IR and doesn't do GPU parallelization.' IMPLICATION: a Prologos GPU backend must make the network's parallelism (which propagators fire this round, on which cells) EXPLICIT and machine-extractable in .pnet — the backend will never recover it from imperative scheduler code.
- THE ARRAY-DSL FAMILY IS THE WRONG SHAPE. Triton (tiles of dense sub-arrays), XLA/StableHLO (dense tensor ops), Halide/TVM (dense stencil pipelines, compute/schedule split), Accelerate ('statically excludes nested, irregular data-parallelism'), Futhark (regular arrays + flattening) ALL target dense, regular, statically-shaped array computation. Prologos propagator networks are SPARSE, DYNAMIC, IRREGULAR GRAPHS (cells = nodes, propagators = hyperedges, topology grows mid-fixpoint). Lowering a sparse dynamic graph through a dense-array IR is a fundamental impedance mismatch — these are NOT the right precedents to imitate.
- THE RIGHT PRECEDENTS ARE MONOTONE-FIXPOINT + FRONTIER-GRAPH ENGINES. (a) GDlog/GPUlog (Datalog on GPU) is almost a literal blueprint for 'monotone fixpoint on GPU': semi-naive new/delta/full evaluation = activation-set/delta-driven firing; one fixpoint iteration = a few kernel launches with kernel-boundary BSP barriers; convergence = empty delta = quiescence. (b) Gunrock's FRONTIER abstraction = explicit active subset processed bulk-synchronously per round. Prologos's S0 monotone merge-to-fixpoint maps onto BOTH far better than onto any array DSL.
- DENSE LAYOUT BEATS POINTER-CHASING ON GPU — the single most load-bearing data-structure lesson. GDlog's HISA (dense row-major data array + sorted index + open-addressing hash of references-not-tuples) beats hash/pointer stores because GPUs need COALESCED contiguous access; sparse pointer-chasing 'kills GPU cache efficiency' (35-45x speedup hinges on this). Prologos's current cell store (CHAMP/hasheq persistent maps, pointer-rich) is the OPPOSITE of GPU-friendly. A GPU backend needs a flat columnar/SoA mirror of cell state.
- WARP DIVERGENCE + LOAD IMBALANCE are the dominant irregular-parallelism enemies, and there are known mitigations. GDlog's 'temporal materialization' (split an N-way join into staged kernels so work rebalances across produced tuples) + strided iteration + Stream-K-style work-centric balancing. For Prologos: heterogeneous propagators (cheap narrower vs expensive structural-unify) firing in one BSP round will diverge badly under naive SIMT; the round must be BUCKETED BY PROPAGATOR KIND (uniform fire-fn per kernel) so each kernel is divergence-free — this is the broadcast-propagator pattern taken to its GPU conclusion.
- NON-VON-NEUMANN IS THE MORE NATURAL FIT, AND IT IS LIVE HARDWARE. A propagator network IS a dataflow graph; dataflow CHIPS map onto it without the SIMT impedance mismatch. Live 2024-2025: NextSilicon Maverick-2 (dataflow HPC accelerator, deployed at Sandia), Efficient Computer Electron E1 (dataflow chip), SPADA spatial-dataflow language, CGRAs (FlexC maps kernels via EQUALITY SATURATION/e-graphs — a direct bridge to PReduce), content-addressable/associative processors (associative reductions = monotone merges in hardware). GPU is the pragmatic near-term target; dataflow/CGRA/PIM is where the architecture wants to live.
- ASSOCIATIVE+COMMUTATIVE merges are the currency of GPU/accelerator reductions. gpu.all_reduce/gpu.subgroup_reduce, segmented scan, and content-addressable associative ops ALL require the combine op to be associative (and want commutative+idempotent for order-independence). Prologos's CALM-monotone join-semilattice merges already satisfy this — this is a STRENGTH to PROTECT: any merge that is not associative+commutative+idempotent forfeits segmented-reduction lowering and forces serialization.
- COMPUTE/SCHEDULE SEPARATION (Halide/TVM) is the one array-DSL idea worth importing wholesale, and Prologos ALREADY HAS IT. Halide/TVM separate the algorithm (what) from the schedule (how: tiling/parallelization/placement) so one algorithm retargets to CPU/GPU/accelerator with different schedules. This is structurally identical to Prologos's Cell/Propagator/Scheduler orthogonality (on-network.md): cell=state, propagator=rule, scheduler=execution. Keeping that orthogonality CLEAN (no scheduler-coupled cell/propagator semantics) is exactly what makes a future GPU/dataflow scheduler a drop-in — the rule already in the codebase is the GPU-portability rule.

**PReduce implications:**
- PReduce's e-graph + tropical-quantale cost extraction is itself a MONOTONE FIXPOINT (congruence closure + cost relaxation), so GDlog's lessons apply directly: store e-classes/e-nodes as dense columnar arrays (parent-pointer + signature columns), run rebuilding/saturation as semi-naive delta-driven kernel rounds, and treat the e-node worklist as a Gunrock-style frontier. egglog is already 'Datalog + e-graph'; GDlog shows Datalog runs on GPU — so PReduce saturation is a credible GPU/accelerator workload, not just a CPU pass.
- FlexC (CGRA mapping via equality saturation) is a stunning convergence: it uses e-graphs to map kernels onto a spatial dataflow array, expanding coverage 2.2x. PReduce's e-graph could DOUBLE as the spatial-placement/mapping engine when Prologos eventually targets a CGRA/dataflow accelerator — the same saturation that super-optimizes the program also discovers how to lay it out on the fabric. Worth a forward note in PREDUCE_MASTER as a non-vN synergy.
- Tropical-quantale cost extraction = min-plus shortest-path on the e-graph (per our-doc: Bellman-Ford/Floyd-Warshall structurally). min-plus is associative — it lowers to segmented-min reductions / gpu.all_reduce<minui>. KEEP the cost lattice's combine associative+commutative so extraction parallelizes; the moment cost extraction needs a non-associative tie-break, it serializes. Flag any non-associative cost rule as a GPU-hostile design smell.
- GDlog's Eager Buffer Management (allocate full + k*delta, reallocate every k iterations not every iteration) is the concrete answer to e-graph growth during saturation. PReduce's e-graph grows monotonically per saturation round exactly like a Datalog relation; adopt EBM-style amortized growth for the columnar e-graph mirror rather than per-round reallocation (GDlog: merge/realloc was up to 45% of runtime).

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- The .pnet cache is the de-facto Prologos IR; to keep a GPU/dataflow backend reachable it must SERIALIZE THE PARALLEL STRUCTURE EXPLICITLY, because (per MLIR) no backend will recover it: (a) the cell set as a flat indexed table (cell-id -> lattice-domain-tag, not pointer graph); (b) the propagator set as (reads-cell-ids, writes-cell-ids, fire-fn-kind) tuples — a static bipartite dataflow graph; (c) per-round ACTIVATION SETS / dependency edges explicit, never implicit in imperative scheduler control flow. If activation is only discoverable by running the Racket BSP loop, the GPU backend is dead on arrival.
- LLVM path is staged-dialect: Prologos would define (or lower into) an MLIR dialect 'pnet' -> existing gpu/nvvm or spirv dialects -> LLVM IR -> PTX/SASS or SPIR-V. The fire-fn bodies (arithmetic on lattice values, structural decomposition) are the leaf computations that become LLVM/PTX; the BSP round structure becomes the gpu.launch sequence with gpu.wait/!gpu.async.token between rounds. This is the SAME staged-lowering Triton (Triton->TritonGPU->LLVM->PTX) and XLA (StableHLO->Linalg->LLVM) use — adopt that shape rather than inventing a bespoke codegen.
- Cell merges must be expressible as MLIR reduction ops: associative+commutative merges lower to gpu.all_reduce / gpu.subgroup_reduce / segmented-scan; non-associative merges cannot. ENCODE the algebraic properties (associative/commutative/idempotent) of each cell's merge IN .pnet as first-class metadata (Prologos already declares these via register-merge-fn!/SRE property declarations) so the backend can pick segmented-reduction vs serial-fold per cell. This is a near-free .pnet addition NOW that pays off at backend time.
- Memory address spaces must be derivable: hot per-round activation/delta cells -> GPU global (coalesced columnar); within-round shared scratch -> workgroup; constant registries (trait tables, ctor-desc) -> constant memory. Prologos's cell hot/warm/cold storage-strategy hooks (cell-layer optimization per on-network.md orthogonality) are the right place to record an address-space HINT per cell now, so it isn't reverse-engineered later.
- AVOID baking scheduler-specific assumptions into .pnet (CALM order-independence + the codebase's cell/propagator/scheduler orthogonality rule). A .pnet that records 'fire order' or 'worklist drain semantics' is portable only to the Racket BSP scheduler; a .pnet that records only the dataflow graph + per-cell merge algebra + activation sets is portable to a GPU scheduler, a dataflow-chip scheduler, AND the current one. The orthogonality rule is literally the cross-backend portability rule — enforce it at .pnet serialization time.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- EXTRAPOLATION: 'Phase collapse' (fusing successive BSP strata/rounds) is the propagator-network analog of array-DSL FUSION (Accelerate/Halide fuse successive array traversals to cut memory traffic and intermediate arrays). On GPU, kernel-launch overhead + global-memory round-trips per round dominate; collapsing N monotone rounds that don't cross a non-monotone stratum boundary into ONE kernel is the highest-value GPU optimization. Frame phase-collapse explicitly as kernel-fusion-for-dataflow-IR.
- GDlog's per-iteration cost is real (merge alone up to 45% of runtime): every BSP round that survives to GPU codegen pays a kernel-launch + buffer-merge tax. This QUANTIFIES the payoff of phase collapse — fewer surviving rounds = fewer kernel launches = directly less overhead. Phase collapse is not just elegance; it is the dominant constant-factor lever on a GPU backend.
- Stratum boundaries (S0 monotone | S(-1) retraction | S1 NAF | topology) are HARD kernel/launch barriers — they are exactly where fusion MUST stop (non-monotone or topology-changing work cannot fuse into a monotone reduction kernel). The stratification.md strata map cleanly to gpu.launch boundaries with gpu.wait between them. Phase collapse should fuse WITHIN a stratum and respect stratum edges as the fusion firewall — same discipline as keeping non-associative work out of segmented reductions.
- EXTRAPOLATION: monotone idempotent merges make phase collapse SAFE under reordering (CALM/Kahn determinism) — fusing rounds cannot change the fixpoint as long as every fused merge is associative+commutative+idempotent. So the same algebraic property that enables segmented-reduction lowering ALSO licenses phase collapse. Tie the two: a stratum whose merges are all ACI is both fusable AND GPU-reducible; the property check is shared.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- LOCK IN NOW (cheap, load-bearing): make ACTIVATION SETS EXPLICIT in .pnet and in the scheduler interface. Gunrock's frontier and GDlog's delta both prove the winning pattern for sparse parallel iteration is an explicit active subset processed per round. Prologos should treat 'which cells/propagators are live this round' as a first-class on-network object (a frontier cell / delta set), NOT an implicit consequence of worklist state. This is the difference between a GPU/dataflow-reachable IR and a Racket-only one.
- LOCK IN NOW: keep every cell merge ASSOCIATIVE + COMMUTATIVE (+ idempotent where possible). This is the single property that unlocks segmented reduction (GPU), gpu.all_reduce, associative content-addressable hardware, AND dataflow-chip reduction units. Prologos's CALM-monotone join-semilattices already satisfy it; the rule is to PROTECT it — treat any proposed non-associative/non-commutative merge as forfeiting all parallel-hardware targets and require explicit justification (mirrors the existing on-network lattice-test discipline).
- LOCK IN NOW: AVOID POINTER-CHASING CELL STORES on the path to backend. CHAMP/hasheq persistent maps are great for speculation/structure-sharing on CPU but are GPU-hostile (HISA lesson). Introduce a flat COLUMNAR / struct-of-arrays mirror of cell state (cell-id-indexed columns per lattice domain) as the backend-facing representation, kept in sync with the CPU CHAMP. Decide the columnar schema NOW even if it's only populated lazily, so the persistent-map design doesn't foreclose it.
- LOCK IN NOW: BUCKET PROPAGATORS BY KIND so each fire-fn-kind is a uniform, divergence-free kernel. SIMT/dataflow fabrics punish heterogeneous control flow; the broadcast-propagator (one propagator, N items, one fire) is already this pattern and already measured 75.6x at N=100. Generalize: a BSP round should lower to one kernel PER propagator-kind over its activation set, not one kernel over a heterogeneous mixed worklist. Record propagator-kind tags in .pnet.
- EXTRAPOLATION (the excited frontier): a propagator network is LITERALLY a dataflow graph, so a DATAFLOW CHIP (NextSilicon Maverick-2, Efficient Computer Electron E1) or a CGRA is a more faithful target than a GPU — no SIMT impedance mismatch, cells map to PEs/memories, propagators to configured routing, merges to in-fabric reduction units. The CGRA mapping problem is NP-complete but FlexC solves it with EQUALITY SATURATION — i.e., PReduce's own e-graph engine could place the network on the fabric. The long-game architecture: Prologos's e-graph super-optimizes the program AND maps it onto a spatial dataflow accelerator, closing the self-hosting loop on non-vN hardware. Keep .pnet a clean spatial dataflow graph (cells, edges, merge-algebra, activation) and BOTH GPU and dataflow-chip backends stay in reach.
- EXTRAPOLATION: dynamic topology (cells/propagators allocated mid-fixpoint via the topology stratum) is the hardest thing for ANY static-fabric target — GPUs hate reallocation (GDlog uses Eager Buffer Management to amortize it), CGRAs are statically placed. Mitigation to design NOW: bound topology change to STRATUM BOUNDARIES (already the CALM-safe protocol per stratification.md) and pre-reserve capacity (EBM-style full+k*delta growth) so within-round execution sees a FIXED graph. A fixed-per-round graph + topology-changes-only-at-barriers is the contract that keeps both GPU and CGRA backends viable; making that contract explicit now avoids a future rewrite.
- EXTRAPOLATION: associative content-addressable / processing-in-memory hardware (CAPP, associative parallel processors) is the dual of Prologos's two recurring operations — structural unification (a MEET/match-by-content) and monotone merge (an associative reduce). Content-addressable match IS what a unification propagator does; an associative reduction IS what a merge does. If Prologos keeps unification expressed as content-match and merges as associative reduces (rather than imperative loops), associative-memory hardware becomes a reachable target for the two hottest compiler operations. This is a far-horizon note, but it is cheap to preserve by keeping those two operations declarative now.

**Novelty / prior art:**
- PRIOR ART (strong, directly applicable): GDlog/GPUlog 'Optimizing Datalog for the GPU' (Sun et al., arXiv 2311.02206) — monotone fixpoint over relations on GPU via HISA + semi-naive delta + kernel-boundary BSP. This is the closest existing system to 'a propagator network on a GPU'; Prologos should treat it as the reference implementation to study, not reinvent. egglog (Datalog+e-graph) + GDlog jointly imply PReduce saturation is GPU-viable.
- PRIOR ART (architectural cousin): Legion/Regent — dataflow task graph with logical regions, runtime parallelism extraction, irregular-parallelism support, sequential-appearing source. Same cell/propagator/scheduler decomposition philosophy as Prologos, but CPU/distributed-HPC and task-granular (coarse) rather than cell-granular (fine) and not SIMT. Useful as a design template for the scheduler/runtime split; NOT a GPU codegen path.
- PRIOR ART (frontier abstraction): Gunrock — frontier-based BSP graph analytics on GPU; proves explicit active-set + bulk-synchronous rounds is THE GPU-friendly sparse-iteration pattern and that frontier beats Gather-Apply-Scatter. Prologos's per-round activation set is the same idea; adopt the vocabulary and the load-balancing techniques.
- PRIOR ART (compute/schedule separation): Halide + TVM — the algorithm/schedule split that lets one algorithm retarget across CPU/GPU/accelerator. Prologos's Cell/Propagator/Scheduler orthogonality is an independent rediscovery of the same principle at the dataflow-IR level; the novelty is applying it to a MONOTONE-LATTICE dataflow IR (not a dense-stencil pipeline) and to a self-hosting compiler.
- APPARENT NOVELTY: lowering a SPARSE, DYNAMIC, LATTICE-VALUED propagator network (with stratification + speculation/worldview bitmasks + dependent-type elaboration) to GPU/dataflow hardware. The array-DSL family (Triton/XLA/Halide/Futhark/Accelerate) targets dense regular arrays; the graph-engine family (GDlog/Gunrock) targets untyped relations/graphs. A typed, lattice-valued, stratified, speculative dataflow network as a GPU codegen target has no direct precedent in the search — worth external verification but appears genuinely novel.
- APPARENT NOVELTY / SYNERGY: using the compiler's OWN e-graph (PReduce) as the spatial-mapping engine for a CGRA/dataflow target (FlexC shows equality saturation maps kernels onto CGRAs; no one has closed this loop for a self-hosting propagator-network compiler). The combination 'e-graph super-optimizes the IR AND places it on the fabric, and the compiler is itself a propagator network' is, per our-doc's self-hosting-closure claim, Prologos-specific.

**Open questions:**
- What is the columnar/SoA schema for cell state? Per-lattice-domain columns (one table per SRE domain) vs a tagged-union mega-table? GDlog uses per-relation HISA; Prologos has many small heterogeneous cells — does it batch by domain, by propagator-kind, or by round? Needs a concrete .pnet-mirror design experiment.
- Granularity mismatch: GPUs want thousands of uniform work-items per kernel; a typical Prologos elaboration round may fire tens of heterogeneous propagators over small cells. Is the compiler's OWN networks ever wide enough to benefit from GPU, or is GPU/dataflow only relevant for USER programs compiled to propagator networks (e.g., large BSP-LE logic solves, e-graph saturation, big relational fixpoints)? The answer reshapes which networks get a GPU backend.
- How is worldview/speculation (ATMS bitmask tagging, tagged-cell-value) lowered? Per-branch tagged entries on shared carriers are a sparse, dynamic, branch-indexed structure — does it become an extra column dimension (worldview-bit) in the columnar store, or are branches materialized as separate dense sub-networks? No precedent found; GDlog/Gunrock have no speculation analog.
- Dynamic topology vs static fabric: even with stratum-boundary-only changes + EBM pre-reservation, a CGRA is statically placed — can a network whose topology grows across saturation be mapped to a fixed fabric at all, or only to a GPU (which tolerates reallocation)? This may bifurcate the roadmap: GPU for dynamic-topology networks, CGRA only for topology-frozen ones.
- Does phase collapse (stratum fusion) preserve the dependent-type elaboration semantics, or only the monotone solver semantics? Fusing rounds is provably safe for ACI merges (CALM), but elaboration mixes monotone propagation with non-monotone resolution (solve-meta! coupling per pipeline.md) — where exactly is the fusion firewall in the ELABORATOR network (vs the solver network)?
- Is the Racket BSP scheduler's round structure already a faithful proxy for kernel-launch rounds, or does it interleave work that a GPU would have to split? I.e., does one Racket BSP round = one valid kernel, or do mixed propagator-kinds within a round force a 1-round -> N-kernel expansion? Measuring real round homogeneity on existing networks would tell us how far the current scheduler is from a GPU-shaped schedule.


## FR6 — Cost models / semirings+quantales / e-graph extraction at the frontier (validates the tropical-quantale choice + per-ISA cost gap for PReduce + non-VN codegen-across-targets)

**Mode:** frontier-web  |  **SHA:** n/a (web)

**Sources:**
- (external-url) https://effect.systems/blog/egraph-extraction.html — external-url — community write-up (egglog/extraction-gym ecosystem) proving e-graph DAG-cost extraction is NP-complete via reduction FROM minimum set cover; in NP because reducible TO ILP; uses uniform node costs + strictly-monotonic cost model; cites Stepp 2023 independent Min-SAT-reduction proof.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2408.17042 — external-url — 'E-Graphs as Circuits, and Optimal Extraction via Treewidth' (Sun/Goharshady et al. 2024). Bijection e-graph↔monotone Boolean circuit; extraction NP-hard AND hard to approximate to ANY constant factor; FPT-in-treewidth DP at 2^O(w^2)·poly(w,n); requires ADDITIVE + LOCAL + monotone node cost; explicitly flags non-additive local cost as OPEN.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/html/2403.14242 — external-url — 'E-Syn: E-Graph Rewriting with Technology-Aware Cost Functions for Logic Synthesis' (2024). Real per-target (7nm tech library) area/delay cost is NON-linear & NON-monotone, so they LEARN it with XGBoost (R^2≈0.76-0.78) instead of an algebraic cost; explicitly reject ILP's linear-monotone constraint; run SEPARATE per-objective optimizations + manual Pareto exploration; 'pool extraction' heuristic.
- (external-url) https://www.csl.cornell.edu/~yc2632/data/smoothe_asplos2025_final.pdf — external-url — 'SmoothE: Differentiable E-Graph Extraction' (ASPLOS 2025). Softmax-relaxes the 0/1 selection variables → gradient-based extraction; competitive-or-better than ILP/greedy, much faster; but explicitly LIMITED to additive/local/node-wise scalar cost; single-objective; no per-target / non-local cost.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.02413 — external-url — 'Semantic Foundations of Equality Saturation' (ICDT 2025). Fixpoint semantics via TREE AUTOMATA + connection to the database CHASE; acyclicity-based syntactic termination criterion. Notably does NOT use semiring/quantale/lattice cost algebra — the canonical semantic foundation is automata-theoretic, leaving the cost/extraction algebra open.
- (external-url) https://2024.splashcon.org/details/splash-2024-oopsla/144/Fast-and-Optimal-Extraction-for-Sparse-Equality-Graphs — external-url — 'Fast and Optimal Extraction for Sparse Equality Graphs' (OOPSLA 2024, Parreaux et al.; ACM doi 10.1145/3689801). Parameterized optimal DAG-cost extraction for low-treewidth e-graphs (treewidth ≤10) in a fraction of ILP time; complements the circuit/treewidth result.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.13020 — external-url — 'e-boost: Boosted E-Graph Extraction with Adaptive Heuristics and Exact Solving' (ICCAD 2025). State-of-art practical extractor: parallel heuristic extraction + adaptive cost-threshold pruning + warm-start ILP; frames extraction as the NP-hard bottleneck of equality saturation in logic synthesis.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.15882 — external-url — 'Equivalence Hypergraphs: DPO Rewriting for Monoidal E-Graphs' (2024). Categorical semantics: e-graphs as morphisms in SEMILATTICE-ENRICHED symmetric monoidal categories; the algebraic frontier is converging on enriched-category structure for e-graph REWRITING (not yet cost/extraction).
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2112.13290 — external-url — 'An equational approach to enriched distributivity' (2021). QUANTALE-enriched (V-enriched) categories + distributive law between down-set/up-set monads → completely-distributive lattices. Confirms quantale-enrichment is an active, mathematically mature direction adjacent to the cost-lattice story.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.18099 — external-url — 'A Ternary Gamma Semiring Framework for Multi-Objective Network Optimization' (Nov 2025). Ternary Tropical Gamma Semiring (TTGS): MULTI-parameter generalization of the tropical semiring for joint (cost,time,risk) optimization with an O(n^2 m) Bellman-Ford analogue. Closest published prior art to a multi-dimensional per-target cost algebra — but framed as a SEMIRING, not a quantale, and not for codegen.
- (external-url) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.06127 — external-url — 'Efficiently Synthesizing Lowest Cost Rewrite Rules for Instruction Selection' (2024). SMT-synthesized equivalence-preserving instruction-selection rewrite rules generated lowest-cost-first against a per-target cost metric; up-to-92x speedups. The per-ISA-cost-driven rewriting frontier in the LLVM/instruction-selection space.
- (external-url) https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/spe.1079 — external-url — Lokuciejewski et al. 2011, 'Approximating Pareto-optimal compiler optimization sequences — trade-off between WCET, ACET and code size'. Establishes that per-target multi-objective (worst-case time / avg time / size) compiler optimization is a genuine Pareto problem, historically attacked with evolutionary search, not algebraic cost extraction.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/docs/research/2026-04-21_TROPICAL_QUANTALE_RESEARCH.md — our-doc — Prologos tropical-quantale research (75KB). Defines T_min quantale for the Phase-9 fuel cell; §5/§10.3/§10.7 frame backward-explanation AND PReduce e-graph extraction as tropical-quantale RESIDUATION; §10.4 Galois bridge fuel↔TypeFacet; cites Gaubert-Katz-Cohen-Quadrat tropical-series residuation as evidence residuation is algorithmically feasible.
- (our-doc) /Users/avanti/dev/projects/prologos/research/quantale research/outputs/quantale-propagator-annotations.md — our-doc — Prologos quantale-annotations brief (37KB, 43 sources). Argues semirings are insufficient for propagator merge (4 gaps: idempotency, ⊤, completeness, induced order) and quantales close them; novelty triangle (quantale↔linear-logic↔LP); Damásio-Pereira residuated LP as nearest miss; product-lattice (value×annotation) independence caveat (C2).

**Key findings:**
- WEBSEARCH AVAILABLE (10 searches, 6 WebFetch). The 2024-2025 e-graph-extraction frontier is large and active (treewidth/circuit theory, SmoothE differentiable, e-boost, OOPSLA-2024 sparse, Semantic-Foundations ICDT-2025, categorical/monoidal e-graphs).
- ESTABLISHED — extraction NP-completeness is NOW RIGOROUSLY SETTLED, not folklore: DAG-cost extraction is NP-complete (reduction FROM minimum set cover; in NP via reduction TO ILP; Stepp-2023 independent Min-SAT proof). The treewidth paper (2408.17042) strengthens this to 'hard to approximate to ANY constant factor' via an e-graph↔monotone-Boolean-circuit bijection (extraction = min-cost satisfying circuit evaluation).
- ESTABLISHED — the tree-cost vs DAG-cost split is THE pivot: TREE cost (counts shared subexprs multiply) is poly-time bottom-up; DAG cost (counts each shared e-node ONCE) is the realistic-but-NP-hard one. Greedy-DAG = fast/suboptimal; ILP = optimal/may-not-terminate; this exact tradeoff is what extraction-gym benchmarks (faster-bottom-up, greedy-dag, ILP, bisection extractors).
- ESTABLISHED — the frontier's cost model is almost universally ADDITIVE + LOCAL (cost(enode)=op-cost + Σ child-costs) + MONOTONE SCALAR. Treewidth-FPT, SmoothE, ILP, and bottom-up all REQUIRE this. The treewidth paper EXPLICITLY names non-additive local cost as an OPEN problem; SmoothE EXPLICITLY states it cannot handle non-local/global-sharing/hardware-dependent cost.
- ESTABLISHED — the per-target cost gap is REAL and currently unsolved algebraically: E-Syn (2403.14242) finds real per-technology (7nm) area/delay is NON-linear & NON-monotone, so they LEARN it with XGBoost (R^2≈0.77) and run SEPARATE per-objective passes + manual Pareto exploration. Nobody in the e-graph frontier drives competitive multi-target codegen from a single principled multi-dimensional cost algebra.
- ESTABLISHED-ADJACENT — multi-dimensional tropical cost EXISTS as published prior art but framed as a SEMIRING not a quantale and not for codegen: the Ternary Tropical Gamma Semiring (2511.18099, Nov-2025) does joint (cost,time,risk) with an O(n^2 m) Bellman-Ford analogue. Multi-objective shortest-path / Pareto-product-order theory is mature but suffers exponential Pareto-front blowup even at 2 objectives.
- EXTRAPOLATION: Prologos's 'extraction = tropical-quantale residuation' (TROPICAL_QUANTALE §10.7) is WELL-ALIGNED but ONLY for the case the frontier already solves easily — the scalar additive-monotone tropical/Lawvere cost IS the local-cost = e-class-analysis fixpoint that bottom-up extraction computes. Residuation buys backward blame-explanation (genuinely under-served at the frontier) but does NOT by itself escape DAG-cost NP-hardness: residuation is the LEFT-ADJOINT existence guarantee, not a tractable extraction algorithm.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the genuinely-beyond-frontier ambition is the MULTI-DIMENSIONAL PER-ISA cost QUANTALE (cycles×size×power, per target). The honest framing: the frontier shows (a) scalar additive cost = tractable-ish (FPT/heuristic), (b) real per-target cost = non-additive/non-monotone → learned or Pareto-searched, (c) NO principled algebra bridges the two. A product/multi-dimensional tropical quantale is the right SHAPE but inherits BOTH the DAG-cost NP-hardness AND the Pareto-front exponential blowup — it organizes the problem, it does not dissolve it.
- ESTABLISHED — the algebraic frontier for e-graphs is converging on ENRICHED-CATEGORY structure (semilattice-enriched / quantale-enriched monoidal categories: 2406.15882, 2505.00807, 2112.13290) — but for REWRITING/representation, while the canonical SEMANTIC foundation (Semantic-Foundations 2501.02413, ICDT) is automata+chase, NOT semiring/quantale. So quantale-FOR-COST-EXTRACTION is an open seam adjacent to active work, not occupied prior art.

**PReduce implications:**
- VALIDATION (tropical choice, scalar case): PReduce's planned tropical-quantale cost extraction is exactly the SCALAR-ADDITIVE-LOCAL-MONOTONE cost the frontier calls 'local cost / e-class analysis' and solves with a bottom-up fixpoint. For TREE cost this is poly-time and the tropical-quantale fixpoint (T_min merge=min, ⊗=+) literally IS that algorithm — clean alignment, low risk. Cite this as the well-trodden base case, not a novel claim.
- CRITICAL CORRECTION to the design doc's optimism: TROPICAL_QUANTALE §10.7 says 'Extraction = find min-cost representative ... Computed via tropical-quantale Kleene star.' That is TRUE for tree cost but FALSE for DAG cost (the realistic one that counts shared e-nodes once). DAG-cost extraction is NP-complete and inapprox-to-any-constant-factor (set-cover reduction; circuit bijection). Kleene-star/residuation does NOT make it tractable. PReduce MUST decide tree-cost (tractable, but double-counts sharing → suboptimal) vs DAG-cost (faithful, NP-hard → needs ILP/FPT/heuristic) up front. This is the single most load-bearing finding for PReduce.
- ADOPT the proven escape hatches rather than reinventing: extraction-gym's extractor menu (faster-bottom-up, greedy-dag, ILP, bisect) is the de-facto standard; OOPSLA-2024 + treewidth-FPT (2^O(w^2)·poly) give OPTIMAL DAG extraction when e-graph treewidth is small (≤10), and e-boost (ICCAD-2025) gives the practical warm-start-ILP+pruning recipe. PReduce should target one of these as its DAG-cost backend and reserve the tropical-quantale residuation for the BACKWARD-explanation layer, where the frontier is genuinely thin.
- RESIDUATION's real differentiated payoff is blame/provenance, not speed. The frontier (treewidth, SmoothE, ILP) extracts a min-cost term but offers NO principled 'why this cost / which rewrites/e-nodes dominated the cost' story. Tropical-quantale residual (a\b = b−a) computed along the e-class-analysis chain is a legitimately novel, well-typed cost-provenance mechanism — position PReduce's residuation contribution THERE (cost-explanation, fuel-exhaustion blame, regression-attribution), not as a faster extractor.
- PReduce's cost-model must heed the additive/monotone boundary the way the frontier does: SmoothE and the treewidth/ILP results all break the moment cost is non-local or non-monotone. Prologos's quantale ⊗ is plain `+` for fuel (additive, monotone, fine) — but ANY richer real cost (register pressure, cache effects, pipeline hazards) is non-local/non-monotone and will fall outside both ILP AND the tropical-quantale clean case. Mirror E-Syn's honesty: either keep cost additive-monotone (and accept the abstraction gap) or accept a learned/heuristic outer loop. Do not claim the quantale dissolves non-monotone cost.
- On-network fit (matches the mantra, not a stretch): the frontier independently confirms PReduce's architecture — 'local cost function ⇒ extraction IS an e-class analysis ⇒ fixpoint traversal' (egglog/Willsey). That is precisely a monotone propagator fixpoint on cost cells. PReduce can implement scalar/tree-cost extraction as native on-network cost-cell propagation (merge=min) with ZERO new scheduler machinery — the frontier's e-class-analysis = Prologos's cost-cell propagator, term-for-term. Use this as the on-network validation in the PReduce design.
- Beware the product-lattice independence caveat (C2 in quantale-propagator-annotations.md) at extraction time: a cost-quantale value attached to (e-class structure × cost) is only a clean product if cost-merge is independent of structural-merge. For DAG cost it is NOT independent (cost depends on which shared sub-DAG is chosen elsewhere) — this is the algebraic root of the NP-hardness (global sharing = non-product). PReduce's design should name this explicitly: DAG-cost extraction is non-product/non-local BY CONSTRUCTION, which is why no local fixpoint solves it optimally.

**.pnet/LLVM implications:**
- The LLVM lowering target inherits instruction-selection's per-ISA cost head-on: 'Efficiently Synthesizing Lowest-Cost Rewrite Rules for Instruction Selection' (2405.06127) generates lowest-cost-first rewrite rules against a TARGET-SPECIFIC cost metric and reports up-to-92x speedups. This is the concrete shape of what a per-ISA cost quantale would feed: if Prologos lowers via e-graph rewriting + cost extraction, the extraction cost model IS the instruction-selection cost model, and it is per-target by necessity.
- EXTRAPOLATION: LLVM already embodies the multi-dimensional-cost reality the quantale would formalize — its scheduler/ISel use per-subtarget MachineModel/SchedModel (latency, μops, throughput, register-pressure). A Prologos per-ISA cost quantale lowering to LLVM should ALIGN its cost dimensions with LLVM's SchedModel rather than invent orthogonal ones, so that on-network extraction and LLVM backend agree on the same per-target numbers (avoids E-Syn's abstract-vs-real cost gap by reading LLVM's measured model).
- Honest scoping for codegen-across-targets (open-Q5): the frontier evidence says competitive MULTI-target codegen from ONE principled multi-dimensional cost algebra is UNSOLVED — E-Syn runs separate per-objective passes; Lokuciejewski-2011 used evolutionary Pareto search for (WCET,ACET,size). A Prologos multi-dim per-ISA cost quantale would be ahead of the field IF it actually drives extraction across targets, but PReduce should not assume one saturated e-graph + one cost-quantale residuation yields the per-target optimum for free; expect per-target re-extraction (changing the quantale's scalar weights per ISA) at minimum.
- Concrete on-network recipe consistent with both the frontier and the mantra: keep the e-graph saturation ISA-AGNOSTIC (rewrites are equivalences, target-independent), and make ONLY the cost cell's quantale weights per-target. Then per-ISA codegen = re-run the (monotone, on-network) cost-extraction fixpoint with that target's weight vector. This matches egglog's 'extraction parameterized by cost function' design and isolates per-target variation to a single cell's lattice parameters — clean orthogonality (Cell owns per-ISA cost; Propagator/Scheduler unchanged).
- FPT-in-treewidth (2^O(w^2)·poly) is the realistic optimal-DAG-extraction backend for the LLVM path: typed/SSA-shaped programs lowered from a propagator IR tend to have bounded-treewidth dependency structure, so the OOPSLA-2024/treewidth route may give OPTIMAL per-ISA extraction in poly-time for real Prologos modules where generic ILP would not terminate. Worth a treewidth measurement on lowered .pnet networks before committing to heuristic extraction.

**Phase-collapse implications:**
- N/A to the cost/extraction facet directly — phase-collapse (worldview/ATMS) is orthogonal to cost extraction. But one bridge: TROPICAL_QUANTALE §10.6 frames cost-per-worldview as a map Q_n(hypercube)→T_min, so the SAME tropical-quantale cost machinery that PReduce uses for extraction also gives cost-bounded WORLDVIEW pruning (drop branches whose accumulated cost exceeds budget). If phase-collapse over speculation branches is ever cost-aware, it reuses the cost cell, not a new mechanism.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the e-graph↔monotone-Boolean-circuit bijection (treewidth paper) is the same Boolean-lattice structure as the ATMS worldview hypercube (Q_n) cited in Prologos's Hyperlattice/structural-thinking rules. 'min-cost satisfying circuit evaluation' (extraction) and 'min-cost consistent worldview' (cost-bounded ATMS search) are the SAME optimization shape over a monotone Boolean lattice — a unifying observation if a future track wants one solver for both.

**Non-vN reach implications:**
- The strongest non-von-Neumann hook from this facet: tropical/min-plus cost computation IS the algebra of shortest-path/all-pairs (Bellman-Ford = T_min matrix-vector product; Floyd-Warshall = tropical Kleene star), which maps DIRECTLY onto GPU/systolic tropical-matrix hardware. A multi-dimensional per-ISA cost extraction whose inner loop is tropical matrix Kleene-star (per TROPICAL_QUANTALE §9.5, O(n^4)) is a candidate for the same non-VN acceleration as min-plus GEMM — cost extraction could itself run on the accelerator the codegen targets.
- EXTRAPOLATION: the multi-dimensional cost quantale (cycles×size×power) is a PRODUCT of tropical quantales, i.e. a tropical SEMIMODULE over a vector of min-plus dimensions — exactly the structure the Ternary Tropical Gamma Semiring (2511.18099) realizes for (cost,time,risk) with a parallel Bellman-Ford. This is the most concrete published evidence that the multi-dim-tropical cost algebra Prologos wants is (a) coherent and (b) has a known parallel/non-VN-amenable fixpoint algorithm. Adopt the product-of-min-plus framing rather than an ad-hoc multi-objective scalarization.
- CAUTION for the non-VN ambition: multi-objective tropical extraction does NOT collapse to a single fixpoint the way scalar tropical does — the Pareto front can be exponential even at 2 objectives (MO-SPP literature). So a non-VN 'extract all Pareto-optimal codegens in parallel' is NOT free; the hardware-parallelism win is on the per-DIMENSION min-plus relaxation, while the Pareto-frontier combination remains combinatorially hard. Frame non-VN acceleration as 'parallelize each scalar tropical pass,' not 'parallelize away the multi-objective NP-hardness.'

**Novelty / prior art:**
- NOVELTY (still standing, but narrow): no published work frames e-graph cost EXTRACTION specifically as quantale RESIDUATION. Confirmed by searching extraction + quantale/residuation/category-theory: the categorical e-graph work (2406.15882 semilattice-enriched monoidal cats; 2505.00807 bindings; 2112.13290 quantale-enriched distributivity) targets REWRITING/representation; the canonical semantic foundation (2501.02413, ICDT-2025) is automata+chase. So 'extraction = residuation on a tropical/cost quantale' is an open seam — but it is adjacent to fast-moving work, so claim it as 'not-yet-occupied,' not 'untouched.'
- PRIOR ART that tempers the 'quantales-are-needed-and-novel' pitch: the cost algebra the extraction frontier ACTUALLY uses is the scalar local cost = e-class analysis = monotone fixpoint (egglog/Willsey thesis). For that base case, plain (idempotent) semiring / tropical-semiring suffices; the QUANTALE additions (completeness, residuation, ⊤) buy backward-explanation and infinite-join handling, NOT a better forward extractor. Keep the quantale claim scoped to those specific extras (as quantale-propagator-annotations.md §6.2 C1/C3 already cautions: don't oversell completeness machinery for a finite problem).
- PRIOR ART for the multi-dimensional per-target cost: Ternary Tropical Gamma Semiring (2511.18099, Nov-2025) is the nearest published structure — multi-parameter tropical optimization with a Bellman-Ford analogue. It is framed as a SEMIRING, lacks residuation, and is not applied to codegen. Prologos's contribution differentiator would be: (a) QUANTALE (residuation + completeness for backward cost-blame), (b) applied to per-ISA CODEGEN extraction, (c) on-network. Cite TTGS as the closest cousin and state the three deltas explicitly to avoid a reviewer's 'this is just a product tropical semiring' objection.
- PRIOR ART for the per-target gap itself: E-Syn (2403.14242) is the canonical 'real per-technology cost is non-linear/non-monotone → learn it' data point, and Lokuciejewski-2011 the canonical 'per-target multi-objective compiler optimization = Pareto search' data point. These bound the open-Q5 gap from both sides: the field has NEITHER a principled multi-dim algebraic cost model NOR competitive single-pass multi-target codegen from one. A multi-dim per-ISA cost quantale that demonstrably drives codegen would be a genuine contribution — but the bar is 'beat XGBoost-learned + separate-per-objective-Pareto,' which is non-trivial.
- The 'semirings are insufficient for propagators; quantales close 4 gaps' thesis (quantale-propagator-annotations.md) is SUPPORTED by the e-graph evidence in one direction: the extraction frontier DOES need idempotent merge + ⊤ + induced order for the cost cell (those are exactly the e-class-analysis lattice requirements). It is NOT supported as 'the extraction community is moving to quantales' — they are not; they use scalar additive cost + ILP/heuristics. So the gap analysis is correct about REQUIREMENTS but should not claim convergence with extraction practice.

**Open questions:**
- Tree-cost vs DAG-cost decision for PReduce: does Prologos's super-optimizer need DAG cost (faithful to on-network sharing, NP-hard) or can it accept tree cost (tractable, double-counts sharing)? The whole tractability story hinges on this and the design doc currently assumes tractability that only holds for tree cost.
- Can the tropical-quantale RESIDUAL actually be computed as a useful cost-blame over a DAG extraction whose optimum required ILP/FPT? Residuation is clean on the chosen extracted DAG, but is the 'why this cost' explanation meaningful when the optimum was found by a global solver rather than a local fixpoint? Needs a concrete worked example before claiming the residuation-blame contribution.
- What is the typical TREEWIDTH of Prologos's lowered .pnet / e-graph dependency structure? If ≤~10, the OOPSLA-2024/treewidth-FPT optimal-DAG extractor applies in poly-time and the NP-hardness is largely moot for real modules — a measurement that would substantially de-risk PReduce. Untested.
- For the multi-dimensional per-ISA quantale: scalarize-then-extract (weighted sum per target, one tropical pass) vs Pareto-front extraction (exponential blowup)? The frontier offers no good answer; Prologos must pick a stance and the choice determines whether on-network extraction stays a single monotone fixpoint or becomes a multi-front search.
- Does the multi-dim cost quantale's ⊗ stay additive-and-monotone per dimension once REAL hardware effects (register pressure, cache, hazards) enter? E-Syn says no (non-linear/non-monotone). If Prologos wants algebraic purity it may have to restrict to additive proxies (op-count, spill-count) and accept E-Syn's abstraction gap, OR adopt a learned outer loop — which breaks the 'principled algebra drives codegen' claim. Unresolved tension to name in the PReduce design.
- Is there a principled bridge between the tropical-quantale forward cost and LLVM's measured per-subtarget SchedModel, so the on-network cost matches the backend's real numbers (closing E-Syn's gap WITHOUT learning)? I.e., can the quantale's per-ISA weights be POPULATED from LLVM's MachineModel rather than guessed? Worth investigating before any learned-cost detour.


---

# Synthesis (full)


## preduce_vision_distilled

PReduce's leading direction (PREDUCE_MASTER, 2026-05-02; TRACK01 sketch; E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §7): reduction lifts ENTIRELY on-network as e-graph + DPO + tropical-quantale + GoI on the SAME propagator substrate that already hosts parsing/typing/elaboration/module-loading, retiring the imperative reduction.rkt (VERIFIED 3562 lines) in full at Track 8. Four structural identities (not metaphors): substrate IS the IR; rule application IS propagator firing; cost extraction IS quantale residuation; equivalence classes ARE shared cells.

THREE-LAYER DECOMPOSITION. Layer 1: the AST is ONE Pocket Universe; per-node occurrence-state (reduction status, cost-in-context, subst env, provenance) is compound-cell COMPONENTS keyed by node-position (not per-node PUs), referencing Layer 2 by cell-id. Layer 2: shared e-class cells — the engineering-memo UPGRADE moves the NTT declaration from `:lattice :structural :order :refinement` to `:lattice :structural :enrichment :semilattice` (+ `:Q-module Q` when cost in scope); merge = union-find (e-node-set union + structural-hash canonical rep + tropical-min cost + monotone provenance); hashcons = CHAMP, cell-id = structural hash; Lévy-optimal sharing because identical terms share one e-class cell. Layer 3: ONE unified rule registry CHAMP (rule-id → rule-data) generalizing the SRE form registry.

TWO ORTHOGONAL AXES. Axis 1 (rule-property → stratum+parallelism): IN-fragment (Lafont binary-principal-port + strong confluence → Lévy-optimal/HVM2-style, S0); adhesive-DPO (critical-pair, S0); confluence-by-construction (SRE pattern, S0); non-monotone/retraction-eligible (S(-1)); opaque/FFI (effect stratum). Tags stack; scheduler exploits the strongest guarantee. Axis 2 (persistence-regime → cacheability): ground/closed | contextual(+worldview-bitmask) | retraction-eligible | open(persist the rule template) | opaque. The COMBINATION of the two axes is what makes the SH super-optimization story shippable.

TWO-STRATUM minimum: S0 = single monotone rewriting stratum hosting ALL confluent rewriting with per-rule property-tag dispatch WITHIN S0; S(-1) = retraction (consumes BSP-LE 2B). Effect boundary respected via topology-request handoff.

SUBSTRATE PREREQUISITE SHIPPED (correcting stale Master/Track-0.1 'in flight' framing): PPN 4C Phase 1B tropical-fuel.rkt landed (PIR 2026-05-17) — exports tropical-left-residual = (a b)→(if (>= b a) (- b a) 0); Track 1 e-class cells adopt :tier 'hot + specialized storage; hashcons = cell-id-from-structural-hash. CATEGORICAL FRAME PIVOT (2026-05-09): primary frame is TBGH semilattice-enriched SMC (LICS 2025, arXiv:2406.15882, Thm 6.5), NOT Biondo M-adhesive — because our lattice-valued cells ARE the required semilattices (recognition, not new machinery), and the theory is CLOSED. NEW load-bearing requirement: NACs (Negative Application Conditions) first-class in the rule schema FROM DAY ONE (else expansive rules x→(+ x 0) don't terminate) — an owner-approval point before Track 0.2 opens.

CRITICAL CORRECTION the sweep forces onto this vision (VERIFIED against our own docs at HEAD e5ede4b2): E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §188/§192/§471 OVER-CLAIM 'the residual IS the extraction algorithm; we don't need to engineer an extractor.' That is TRUE only for TREE cost (each shared subexpr counted per-use, poly-time greedy DP). The DAG/sharing-aware cost real codegen needs (each emitted instruction counted ONCE) is NP-complete and inapproximable to any constant factor (E_GRAPHS §118/§377 correctly say so; TROPICAL_QUANTALE §185 admits residuation is 'a STRUCTURAL existence guarantee, not an algorithmic one'). So the residuation contribution is genuine for cost-PROVENANCE/blame, not for an NP-escaping optimal extractor. This must be edited in both docs before Track 4 ships.


## preduce_aspects_needing_llvm_awareness

- 1. TREE-COST vs DAG-COST extraction (HIGHEST PRIORITY, gates the whole 'super-optimizing' claim). WHY LLVM changes it: a shared subexpression in extracted code is ONE emitted instruction, so the realistic cost is DAG/sharing-aware = NP-complete + inapprox-to-any-constant-factor (FR1/FR6; reduction from min-set-cover; e-graph↔monotone-Boolean-circuit bijection arXiv:2408.17042). The tropical residual computes TREE cost only. DECIDE: (a) accept tree-cost (poly, double-counts sharing, suboptimal LLVM) and scope residuation to cost-provenance; or (b) add a DAG-cost backend (e-boost warm-start-ILP arXiv:2508.13020 / treewidth-FPT 2^O(w^2)·poly arXiv:2408.17042 / sparse-extraction OOPSLA-2024). Edit E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH §471 + TROPICAL_QUANTALE §10.7 either way.
- 2. EAGER-vs-SATURATE per rule-kind (re-scopes Tracks 1/4/6, decide BEFORE Track 1 builds the substrate). WHY LLVM changes it: the only production e-graph mid-end (Cranelift aegraph, Fallin Apr-2026) is EAGER single-pass cost-reducing rewriting, NOT saturation, with avg e-class = 1.13 e-nodes (~88% singletons), 2 suboptimal extractions in 4M value-nodes, 7-8% MORE compile time for ~2% runtime win — and the fat-e-class machinery bought little. PReduce's S0 'monotone rewriting stratum' implicitly assumes saturation for everything (the regime Cranelift abandoned). DECIDE: an explicit Axis-1 EAGER tag (IN-fragment/confluence-by-construction/arithmetic run eager, no held alternatives) vs SATURATE (genuinely non-confluent cost-differing rules only); and MEASURE the e-class-size distribution on real Prologos reduction first — if ~88% singletons, Tracks 4/6 drop from central to rare-case.
- 3. WHERE sharing/register-pressure/code-motion cost LIVES (PReduce extraction vs LLVM-side placement). WHY LLVM changes it: Cranelift keeps its e-graph cost SIMPLE (per-node additive) and defers sharing/scheduling/code-placement to a separate elaboration pass (GVN/LICM/remat); FR1+FR3 both recommend 'collapse the front, phase-separate the back.' DECIDE: keep PReduce extraction tree-cost-cheap and let LLVM's own backend own sharing/scheduling (de-risks Track 4 enormously) vs. fold register-pressure into PReduce's DAG cost (makes Track 4 NP-hard). Recommended: Cranelift's split.
- 4. NO OPAQUE-RUNTIME-CALL SHIM in extracted-term lowering (the #1 measurable LLVM-pass defeater, FR3). WHY LLVM changes it: LLVM cannot inline, alias-analyze, eliminate redundant loads, SROA, or vectorize ACROSS opaque calls. A naive PReduce-to-LLVM that lowers each primitive (constructor alloc, arith, pattern-match discrimination, projection) as a runtime-library call produces pessimal IR. DECIDE: every reducible primitive emits inlinable attribute-decorated IR (or alwaysinline); opaque calls confined strictly to the Track-7 effect/FFI boundary (which the design already walls off as 'opaque cells'). Confirm the effect-stratum boundary == the pure/liftable-into-e-class boundary (Cranelift lifts only PURE ops into the acyclic e-graph).
- 5. ACYCLIC / PURE-ONLY e-class liftability for the LLVM path. WHY LLVM changes it: LLVM IR has phi/loops but Cranelift's aegraph stays acyclic by lifting only PURE operators into the e-graph and leaving the CFG/control skeleton outside — this is what makes the tractable acyclic-extraction results (treewidth/sparse) APPLICABLE and the lowering well-defined. DECIDE: confirm PReduce's per-AST-PU e-graphs lift pure sub-terms only, keep control flow / effects outside (Track-7 effect stratum / Axis-1 'opaque' tag), and characterize the pure/liftable boundary precisely so acyclic-extraction tractability and LLVM lowering don't break at the seam.
- 6. COST-MODEL CALIBRATION to LLVM's measured per-subtarget SchedModel (per-ISA gap). WHY LLVM changes it: real per-target area/delay/cycle cost is NON-linear & NON-monotone (E-Syn arXiv:2403.14242 had to LEARN it with XGBoost; instruction-selection rule synthesis arXiv:2405.06127 is per-ISA by necessity). A pure architectural/derivation cost can pick a term LLVM then de-optimizes. DECIDE: keep e-graph saturation ISA-AGNOSTIC (rewrites are target-independent equivalences) and make ONLY the cost cell's quantale weights per-target, POPULATED from LLVM's MachineModel/SchedModel rather than guessed — so on-network extraction and the LLVM backend agree on the same numbers (closes E-Syn's abstract-vs-real gap without learning). Per-ISA codegen = re-run the monotone cost-extraction fixpoint with that target's weight vector (clean Cell-layer orthogonality).
- 7. NAC serialization into the .pnet rule registry visible to the lowering layer. WHY LLVM changes it: the substrate-call boundary's per-cell ENRICHMENT ANNOTATION DETERMINES the parallelism lowering strategy (IN-fragment → HVM2-style massive parallelism; adhesive-DPO → DPOI critical-pair-aware scheduling) (engineering-memo §2 Track-0.3). DECIDE: per-cell enrichment annotation (:semilattice / :commutative-monoid / :Q-module+quantale-id) AND per-rule property-tags + NAC specs + worldview-bitmask must serialize into the Track-0.3 .pnet schema (NAC field is a Track-0.2 deliverable + owner-approval point not yet acknowledged in the read docs).


## pnet_to_ir_design

OPTIMAL Q2a TRANSFORM (the network-as-value → lowered-IR design; IG4 + SH-path §4 + PM-13). The transform is the addition of three serializable sub-formats beyond today's 24-slot value+17-registry payload, partitioned so the LLVM-DIRECTIVE half ships BEFORE the harder topology-round-trip half:

TARGET .pnet = serialize(elaboration-state) ⊕ CELL-TABLE ⊕ MERGE/BODY-SYMBOL-TABLE ⊕ TOPOLOGY-EDGE-LIST.

(A) CELL-TABLE — each cell as {cell-id, initial-value, domain-name, specialized-cell-meta(tier storage fires-on on-write-check on-read-check), enrichment-annotation, merge-algebra-properties(assoc/comm/idem), address-space-hint, persistence-regime}. CRUCIAL & cheap-NOW: the specialized-cell-meta is already #:transparent with all plain-SYMBOL fields EXCEPT merge-fn (VERIFIED propagator.rkt:327; 8 production call sites propagator.rkt:1077-1193) — so the LLVM-directive vocabulary (tier/storage/fires-on) serializes by the existing struct→vector path TODAY, with no closure problem. This is the Q2b-enabling partition: ship cell-table declarations first.

(B) MERGE/BODY-SYMBOL-TABLE — replace every Racket CLOSURE (merge-fn, fire-fn, on-write-check, on-read-check, preparse expander, marshaller) with a SYMBOLIC reference resolved at load against the SRE form registry (SH-path §4: 'propagators-as-DATA not propagators-as-Racket-procedures', partly upstream of NTT). This is the hard, unbuilt half — the closure problem IS the linchpin blocker.

(C) TOPOLOGY-EDGE-LIST — each propagator as {input-cids, output-cids, body-symbol-ref, broadcast-profile, flags, component-paths, assumption-id, decision-cell-id, srcloc}: a serialization of the propagator struct with fire-fn replaced by the body-ref. The deserializer RE-INSTALLS propagators from the edge list rather than rebuilding 'fresh and empty'.

THE LOWERING IS ONE ARTIFACT AT THREE INTERPRETATION LAYERS (PM-13 §2): a round-tripped .pnet is simultaneously (i) loadable by the Racket scheduler, (ii) lowerable to LLVM where cells=typed memory regions / propagators=functions over regions / scheduler=worklist (SH Master Track 2 Low-PNet IR), and (iii) the LHC compiler's own IR. The specialized-cell-meta declarations are the directives that make (ii) emit FAST code rather than an interpreter.

EXTRACT-THEN-LOWER CONTRACT (FR1/FR3, mirror Cranelift's two-stage shape): the .pnet payload an LLVM consumer reads for codegen is the EXTRACTED post-reduction term-DAG + cost annotations — NOT the full saturated e-graph. Persisting the full e-graph is for cross-SESSION REUSE of equivalences (Track 5); the lowering handoff is the chosen representative. Stage 1 = extract a cheapest DAG from the reduced e-class cells; Stage 2 = apply LLVM-lowering rewrite rules to that DAG. Make this split explicit so the consumer reads the extracted representative DIRECTLY (zero-copy-ish), not by reconstructing a separate term tree — the data-movement cost Fallin engineered away reappears at the .pnet boundary as serialize/deserialize.

DIRECT-to-LLVM vs intermediate-region-IR (a genuine fork): the docs commit to .pnet-DIRECT-to-LLVM (no MLIR, to preserve the single-fixpoint property; concurrency-primitives §1). FR3/FR5 note an intermediate regionalized-dataflow IR (RVSDG-like / eqsat-MLIR dialect) better preserves discovered parallelism to a GPU/CGRA backend and is the production-validated 'optimize high, lower late, pick backend last' shape. Recommend designing the post-extraction handoff as a target-NEUTRAL dataflow representation with LLVM-emit and GPU-emit as sibling consumers (keeps the non-vN constraint reachable; see non_von_neumann_reach).


## pnet_current_limitations

- SENTINEL PROBLEM — live network NOT round-tripped (VERIFIED pnet-serialize.rkt:104,429-432). The live prop-network and elab-network are REPLACED with '(runtime-prop-network) / '(runtime-elab-network) sentinels on serialize and rebuilt via make-prop-network FRESH on deserialize. TOPOLOGY (what-watches-what) is LOST, not serialized. So '.pnet IS the IR' is true ONLY for elaboration-state values + 17 registries, NOT for the live network that did the elaborating.
- CLOSURE PROBLEM — merge/fire/check functions become error stubs (VERIFIED pnet-serialize.rkt:99,440). Any procedure value (merge-fns, fire-fns, preparse expanders, non-foreign marshallers, the on-write-check/on-read-check predicate fields of specialized-cell-meta) serializes to a '(foreign-proc name) stub that ERRORS if invoked ('deserialized stub for ~a — needs re-link'). Merge functions as Racket closures are the central blocker. The named fix (symbolic refs into the SRE form registry, propagators-as-data) is undesigned and 'partly upstream of NTT progress.'
- SCHEDULER-STATE off-network (PM-13 §5.1 Q4/Q5). The stratum-handler registry (a Racket box), the worklist, and fuel are off-network Racket procedures/state today. PM-13 Option α (handler-as-procedure) 'complicates .pnet' (closures don't serialize); Option β (handler-as-data) is required for round-trip but undesigned. fuel-cost + worldview-cache are ALREADY on-network cells (the easy precedent); the handler registry is the foundational-redesign case. Q2a is incomplete until scheduler-state is on-network.
- STALENESS MODEL is build-co-located, not content-addressed (VERIFIED pnet-serialize.rkt:465-496). .pnet invalidates on source mtime+size change and driver_rkt.zo-newer-than-.pnet (infrastructure-stale?). For .pnet to be a DEPLOYMENT artifact (SH Stage A.5), the format needs content-addressing (SH-path §10 names IPVM/Fission deterministic-Wasm precedent) — the current model assumes a co-located Racket build, exactly the dependency Stage A.5 retires.
- SUSPECT EXTERNAL CITATION to verify before any write-up: the Schlatt 'E-Graphs as a Persistent Compiler Abstraction' arXiv ID '2602.16707' appears at E_GRAPHS_RESEARCH:275,635 + PREDUCE_MASTER:51,113,245. arXiv YYMM months are 01-12, so '2602' (month 26) is STRUCTURALLY INVALID — the ID is almost certainly transcription-garbled or hallucinated. Schlatt is cited as the closest existing peer (cross-phase e-graph persistence), so the citation is load-bearing for the novelty positioning; find and fix the real ID.


## optimal_llvm_emission

Q2b = what emitting OPTIMAL LLVM looks like, in three parts (FR3 checklist + IG4 directive-mapping + the call-shim avoidance).

PART 1 — THE LLVM-IR EMISSION CHECKLIST (FR3, from LLVM Frontend PerformanceTips + GHC/CWC-LLVM/Lean/Koka):
[1] SSA-FIRST, MEMORY-LAST: emit cell values into SSA virtual registers; reserve alloca for genuinely mutable/escaping cells, ALL allocas in the entry block so mem2reg/SROA promote them. A read-once/write-once propagator is a pure SSA dataflow node, not a load/store pair.
[2] NO AGGREGATE VALUES: lower compound cells (CHAMP nodes, tagged-cell-value entries, scope-cells) to per-field SCALAR loads/stores; never load/store {struct} (aggregates defeat SROA). Multi-field returns use the multi-register-return exception only.
[3] ATTRIBUTE-DECORATE EVERYTHING: every kernel carries memory(none)/memory(read)/memory(argmem:...) + nounwind + willreturn when valid; every cell-pointer arg carries noalias (distinct arenas don't alias), align, dereferenceable(N), nonnull, noundef. A monotone propagator over disjoint inputs is the textbook noalias+readonly case.
[4] TBAA ALIAS HIERARCHY over cell-classes (the GHC technique): disjoint !tbaa roots per cell-class/arena (e-class-cell / scope-cell / decision-cell / worklist memory), DERIVED structurally from SRE domain registration — unlocks redundant-load elimination + reordering. Worldview/speculation tagging makes a cell's value context-dependent → must NOT mark those loads invariant.
[5] INVARIANCE FROM PERSISTENCE REGIME: ground/closed e-class values → !invariant.load + TBAA-constant (immutable once computed, LLVM hoists freely); per-round scratch → lifetime.start/end for stack-coloring + CSE. (Free, principled alias metadata GHC has to hand-build.)
[6] CUSTOM CALLING CONVENTION for the dispatch hot loop: musttail + a JWA/ghccc-style cc (no callee-saved regs; arg-regs == live-on-return regs) + naked to erase prologue/epilogue; pad params to unify types for musttail. Do NOT route firing through the generic C convention. (Note: von-Neumann-specific scaffolding; dissolves on a non-vN target.)
[7] MONOMORPHIZE + UNBOX BEFORE LOWERING (Julia lesson): the front-end (elaborator/PReduce) devirtualizes trait dispatch + unboxes Nat/Int/Bool/known-layout cells into machine values BEFORE emitting LLVM; don't emit boxed-everything + runtime-dispatch and hope LLVM recovers it.
[8] POISON over undef; nsw/nuw on Nat arithmetic where overflow is impossible; inbounds on every cell-address GEP; fast-math when valid; emit OPAQUE-POINTER IR (LLVM 15+) from day one (Prologos is green-field).
[9] PRECISE-RC INSTEAD OF STATEPOINTS (Perceus/Lean lesson + Statepoints doc): exploit QTT m0/m1/mw multiplicities to emit Perceus-style precise inc/dec with borrow-elision + FBIP in-place reuse, AVOIDING gc.statepoint/gc.relocate (which force pointer spills + stack maps and are a deliberate optimization barrier run LATE). Prologos already HAS the linearity Lean/Koka must INFER — cash it in, don't infer it.
[10] PIPELINE: start from stock -O2/-O3 (LLVM's own advice), add IRCE for bounds-checked Nat access + LICM/LoopUnswitch for guard-heavy logic loops; re-run -O2 to validate canonical IR.

PART 2 — SPECIALIZED-CELL-DECLARATION → LLVM-DIRECTIVE MAPPING (IG4; carrier VERIFIED specialized-cell-meta(tier storage fires-on …) propagator.rkt:327).
